- **Decision:** principal-architect amends ADR-0010/0014 (SEC-COND-02); the requirement side and the configuration defaults are: (1) two bulkheads per carrier account, `rates` = 6 (`getRates`, `findServicePoints`, `validateAddress`) and `transactional` = 4 (`createShipment`, `cancelShipment`, `schedulePickup`, `cancelPickup`, `track`), replacing the single pool of 10 (REQ-CARR-017, DR-B32, NFR-RES-003, A-037); (2) anonymous callers get at most 3 of the 6 `rates` slots and a global Redis token bucket `CARRIER_ANON_CALLS_PER_MIN=120` per account; when exhausted, guests receive `carrier_error {code: CARRIER_BUSY}` for that carrier or cached results, authenticated quotes are unaffected (new REQ-CARR-022, security event `carrier_anon_budget_exhausted`); (3) daily quota guard `CARRIER_DAILY_QUOTA` per account (0 = off, default for Mock; 500 seeded for the DHL sandbox) with `CARRIER_QUOTA_RESERVE_PCT=40`: anonymous fan-out stops at 60 %, only `transactional` calls above 90 %, `carrier_quota_pressure` plus alarm at 70 % and 90 % (new REQ-CARR-023); (4) `CARRIER_BUSY` and quota refusals are local admission rejections: retryable, non-ambiguous, retried with backoff inside the 30-minute saga deadline and never by themselves moving a shipment to DRAFT or EXCEPTION (REQ-BOOK-023, DR-B03 step 2, EC-B48); (5) `streamQuoteResults` never fans out (fan-out once at `POST /quotes`) and concurrent streams are capped at 2 per IP and 5 per user (REQ-CARR-009, DR-A22, NFR-SEC-005); (6) new NFR-RES-007 states the measurable isolation target; new story US-293 (Must, M1, not DC) carries REQ-CARR-022/023; US-130, US-135, US-205 amended; EC-B48/B49 added.
- **Why:** The threat is real and cheap (T-46: botnet or IPv6 rotation defeats per-IP limits) and the design coupled it to paying customers through the shared bulkhead, which made CF3 bookings fail and risked sandbox-account suspension (about 500 DHL calls a day). Separate pools make the booking path independent of guest traffic; the anonymous budget bounds total guest load however many IPs it comes from; the quota guard protects the account itself. 120/min is about half the `rates` pool's throughput at the Mock `fast` profile (6 slots ≈ 0.9 s p95 → roughly 240–400 calls/min), honouring the review's "guests ≤ 50 % of the rates pool" with headroom for the 20/min per-IP limit times a handful of genuine concurrent guests. Treating `CARRIER_BUSY` as retryable is the one change the saga needs: an admission rejection involves no network I/O, so it is never ambiguous and must never release a paid authorisation.
- **Alternatives:** Keep one pool and lower the guest per-IP limit (rejected: does not bound a distributed source and hurts genuine guests); refuse guest quoting during pressure entirely (rejected: cached results and other carriers still serve them); make `CARRIER_BUSY` a transient failure counted against the 5 retries (rejected: a long flood would then push a paid booking to DRAFT).
- **Reversibility:** High (four configuration keys; the k6 `wave` profile re-thresholds). Counts: +2 REQs (CARR-022/023, Must M1), +1 NFR (RES-007), +1 Must story (US-293).

### D-049 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · Registration in the `email-send` rate class plus `registration_probe`; the register-then-login enumeration oracle is accepted as RR-16 / PRD R-12 (D-009 kept)
- **Decision:** `POST /auth/register` is counted in the `email-send` class as well as `auth` (3 accepted registrations per hour per normalised address, 10 per hour per IP); more than 5 distinct normalised addresses from one IP in an hour returns 429 `RATE_LIMITED` and emits the security event `registration_probe` (DR-A22, REQ-AUTH-001, REQ-SEC-005, REQ-SEC-012, NFR-SEC-005, EC-A52, A-095; US-015 and US-040 amended). The account-existence oracle created by the register → 202 → login pair (free address ⇔ login succeeds) is **accepted** as residual risk RR-16, recorded as PRD risk R-12. Sign-in before verification (D-009) and the client-side auto sign-in (D-045 (1)) are kept.
- **Why:** The oracle is inherent to D-009, not to the auto-login (adjudication (c)); removing it means no sign-in until verification, which breaks UX flow F02 and the funnel rationale that D-009 and D-038/D-046 exist to protect (A-006 already budgets ≤ 15 % drop-off at verification; forcing verification before the wizard would raise it). The probe is noisy (every attempt e-mails the target) and now slow (14k/day/IP already bounded by `auth`; 5 distinct addresses/hour/IP bounds the distinct-address rate far more), so the remaining disclosure is account existence for a handful of addresses per IP per hour, which is the same information a password-reset form leaks in most consumer products. The identical 202 still denies a free oracle on the register call alone, so it stays.
- **Alternatives:** No sign-in before verification (rejected: F02 and funnel cost; the review itself does not recommend it); CAPTCHA on register (rejected: D-UX-026 no CAPTCHA, accessibility).
- **Reversibility:** High (two rate-class entries and one counter; D-009 reversal is a separate product decision).

### D-050 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · Pre-hijack protection: verification revokes other sessions, a redeemed reset token verifies the mailbox, unverified accounts expire after 7 days
- **Decision:** (1) Successful e-mail verification revokes every session of the user except the one that redeemed the token when it belongs to the same user (`SESSION_REVOKED` audit; REQ-AUTH-004, DR-A04, EC-A51; US-016 amended). (2) A redeemed password-reset token sets `email_verified_at` when still null (REQ-AUTH-012; US-021 amended). (3) `PENDING_VERIFICATION` accounts expire to `DEACTIVATED` 7 days after registration through `housekeeping` (`AUTH_UNVERIFIED_TTL=7d`), revoking sessions, soft-deleting the personal organisation and freeing the address (new REQ-AUTH-026, M0 Must; new story US-292, Must, not DC). (4) The verification e-mail says "if you did not create this account, ignore this e-mail"; the "account already exists" e-mail carries a password-reset entry point (REQ-AUTH-001 note). ADR-0006's seam note that a future social or OTP login never links automatically to an unverified password account is principal-architect's (SEC-P3-06 item 5). A-096 records the defaults.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-06: with sign-in before verification (D-009), an attacker can pre-register a victim's address and keep a live session after the victim verifies, reading everything the victim enters. Revoking on verification kills that session at the exact moment mailbox control is proven, at zero UX cost (the verifying browser keeps its session). Reset-verifies-mailbox gives the real owner a recovery path without support. The 7-day expiry stops address squatting and matches the 24 h token plus a generous grace for people who verify late; it is a repeatable job the platform already has (REQ-PLAT-015, D-045 purge).
- **Alternatives:** Block sign-in until verified (rejected, see D-049); revoke all sessions including the verifying one (rejected: pointless sign-in for the legitimate user in the common case).
- **Reversibility:** High (one TTL, one revocation rule). Counts: +1 REQ (AUTH-026, M0 Must), +1 Must story (US-292, M0).

### D-051 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · Customs invoice: `invoice_print: FULL | MASKED` per reference type (narrows D-045 (5)); SENSITIVE documents never e-mailed as attachments; Viewer lacks `document:download`, `document:email` and `privacy:export`
- **Decision:** (1) The compliance pack's party identity reference types carry `invoice_print: FULL | MASKED` (default `MASKED`); only `FULL` types print unmasked on the commercial/pro-forma invoice, and a document carrying a `FULL` reference is classified `SENSITIVE` (REQ-COMP-001, REQ-CUST-012; US-057 and US-186 amended). Seed: the IN import receiver reference of the US → IN lane is `FULL` (customs reject the invoice without it, D-045 (5)); all other seeded types are `MASKED`; the `FULL` list needs `reviewed_by` before M5 (A-097). (2) `SENSITIVE` documents are never e-mail attachments: REQ-DOC-010 and the booking confirmation REQ-NOTIF-008 send a link to the shipment page instead; labels and pickup confirmations are still attached (US-228, US-283 amended). The PRD §6.2 step "confirmation email with documents" is met by the label attachment plus the invoice link. (3) Customer Viewer explicitly lacks `document:download`, `document:email` and `privacy:export` (REQ-RBAC-004 text; A-074 made binding; US-066 amended). (4) The decrypted reference exists only in the worker's render DTO, registered `SENSITIVE_PERSONAL` for Pino redaction (REQ-CUST-012 note). Storage, audit and retention controls are principal-architect's (ADR-0019/0021 amendments).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-09 / adjudication (f): the customs need is real but applies per reference type and per lane, not to every stored reference; some national identity numbers carry statutory masking rules, so "print everything in full" (D-045 (5) as written) over-disclosed. Making it pack data keeps the "compliance is data" principle and lets legal review flip one flag. E-mailing a PDF with a full tax ID puts it in mailboxes the platform cannot audit or erase; a link keeps the fail-closed `DOWNLOAD` audit and the 300 s URL in the loop. Viewer is the read-only role; a file that carries a full identity reference is not "read-only metadata".
- **Alternatives:** Mask on the invoice too (rejected again: customs rejection on the seeded lane); attach the invoice with the reference masked and let the user download the full version (rejected: two versions of a customs document invite the wrong one being printed; one authoritative document, fetched through the audited path).
- **Reversibility:** High for (1)–(3) (seed flag, attachment rule, seed matrix). D-045 (5) is not edited; this decision narrows it.

### D-052 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · Remaining requirement-side wording from the P3 security review (SEC-P3-11/13/14/15/21/22/25)
- **Decision:** Applied as requirement wording, no scope change: (a) REQ-ADMIN-004: admin idle timeout is 30 minutes of **user input**, enforced server-side by a 30-minute sliding admin refresh TTL (`AUTH_ADMIN_REFRESH_IDLE_TTL=30m`) and client-side by stopping silent refresh, with a 28-minute warning dialog (SEC-P3-11; US-033 amended). (b) REQ-CARR-018: base64/binary document fields become `{"$blob": {sha256, size, media_type, document_id?}}` and tracking/response payloads are allow-list redacted (SEC-P3-13, R-29; US-136 amended). (c) REQ-AUTH-018: outside `APP_ENV ∈ {local, test}` the first staff TOTP activation needs an e-mailed one-time code; the 24 h enrolment deadline is enforced now by `housekeeping` (suspension); REQ-DATA-002: non-local seeds create staff without passwords and send a set-password link (SEC-P3-14; US-025 and US-049 amended; A-098). (d) REQ-QUOTE-002: the guest access token stops being honoured 7 days after quote expiry (SEC-P3-15 condition 5; US-155 amended). (e) REQ-CARR-006: Mock registration and scenarios gated by explicit `MOCK_CARRIER_ENABLED` / `MOCK_SCENARIOS_ENABLED` flags defaulting to true only in local/test, start-up refused in production, header stripped at the edge elsewhere; REQ-PAY-012: a signed webhook with `livemode: true` is rejected and audited, unknown event types stored but never processed (SEC-P3-21; US-123 and US-213 amended). (f) NFR-SEC-012 reworded to the production/dev closures, SPDX rules and the direct-plus-risky-transitive 18-month gate of D-ARCH-003 rev 2 (SEC-P3-22). (g) REQ-API-014: RateLimit headers are the three draft-06 names (SEC-P3-25). (h) REQ-SEC-012 gains `registration_probe`, `carrier_quota_pressure`, `carrier_anon_budget_exhausted`. Not applied, with reasons: SEC-P3-21 item 4 (carrier pushes as hints confirmed by `track()`) stays with REQ-TRACK-006 (Should) unchanged because REQ-TRACK-002 already forbids backward or out-of-final-state moves and the confirmation rule is an adapter design detail for ADR-0010; SEC-P3-21 item 5 (label byte validation) is an ADR-0021 implementation rule with no requirement-level behaviour beyond REQ-DOC-005's hash.
- **Why:** Each item is a case where a requirement would otherwise contradict the ADR amendment principal-architect is making for the same finding, so G2 would flag a REQ-vs-ADR conflict. None changes what a customer sees in the exit demos (compose runs `APP_ENV=local`).
- **Reversibility:** High (wording and configuration keys).

### D-ARCH-003 rev 2 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Licence policy clarified (supersedes the text of D-ARCH-003; security-analyst review §5 (e), SEC-P3-22)
- **Decision:** (1) **Production closure** (runtime dependency graph of `apps/api`, `apps/worker` and the shipped web/admin bundles) must match LICENSE_ALLOWLIST plus the permissive equivalents MIT-0, 0BSD, BlueOak-1.0.0, PSF-2.0 / Python-2.0. (2) **Dev and build closure:** the same, plus the build-time data licences CC-BY-3.0 and CC-BY-4.0 (e.g. `caniuse-lite`, `spdx-exceptions`) provided the data is not shipped in bundles, with attribution in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. (3) **SPDX expressions:** `OR` passes if any branch passes; `AND` needs all branches; `UNKNOWN`, `UNLICENSED` or a missing licence fails, except workspace packages marked `"private": true`. (4) **Copyleft and source-available licences** (GPL, AGPL, LGPL, SSPL, BUSL, RSAL, Commons Clause, Semgrep Rules License) never appear in the npm closure, prod or dev; they are admissible only for separately executed tools run as pinned containers or actions and listed by name in the **tool register**: Terraform BUSL-1.1 (OpenTofu fallback), k6 AGPL-3.0, Semgrep CLI LGPL-2.1 and its registry rules (internal scanning of our own code only), Checkov, ZAP, Mailpit, RustFS, Valkey. The open-ended "source-available with a licence permitting our use" category of D-ARCH-003 is replaced by this register. (5) **Shipped images** contain no AGPL, SSPL, BUSL or RSAL component (Trivy licence scan on images); OS packages of the pinned base images are governed by the distribution's licences and reported, not gated. (6) **Maintenance (18-month) rule:** gating for direct dependencies and for any transitive dependency that is deprecated, archived or carries a Critical/High advisory; other stale transitives are reported in the freshness report. (7) **OFL-1.1:** only for font files embedded in generated PDFs (Noto Sans, ADR-0019); the web apps ship no web fonts (D-UX-001). Items (3)/(4) of the original D-ARCH-003 (rejections of `ua-parser-js` 2.x, Redis 7.4/8 images, `cookie-parser`, `jest-openapi`/`swagger-ui-express`) stand.
- **Why:** The registry spot checks in the review found `argparse` 3.x under PSF-2.0 and `caniuse-lite`/`spdx-exceptions` under CC-BY, which the original wording did not cover, and the "source-available" clause was open-ended. NFR-SEC-012 was reworded by product-manager (D-052 f). Verified in Wave 0 by dependency-auditor (R-ARCH-12); licence-check unit tests on SPDX expressions.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-017 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-01 (HIGH): 30-day absolute cap, `auth_time` claim, step-up scopes with `x-reauth-max-age`, CVC recollection for stale saved-card checkout, 7-day fallback (ADR-0006 am. 12, ADR-0012 am. 1, ADR-0016 am. 8; contract and data model amended)
- **Decision:** `AUTH_SESSION_ABSOLUTE_TTL=30d` (web; admin 12 h), `AUTH_REFRESH_TTL ≤` the cap asserted by config, 401 `SESSION_EXPIRED` past the cap. Access token claim `auth_time` = `sessions.authenticated_at` (last password or MFA verification; set at login, MFA completion and a successful re-auth proof; copied unchanged by refresh). New extension `x-reauth-max-age`: with `x-reauth-scope` alone a proof is always required (`X-Reauth-Proof` required); with both, a token fresh within the max-age suffices (`X-Reauth-Proof` optional, parameter `ReauthProofConditional`). `payment_method.manage` on the four saved-method operations and `privacy.export` on export request/download always require a proof; `org.member.manage` with 43,200 s on `createInvitation`, `resendInvitation`, `updateMember`, `removeMember`. Saved-card checkout with `auth_time` > 12 h creates the PaymentIntent with `require_cvc_recollection=true` and the shared `Payment` schema returns `cvc_recollection_required`; CVC recollection is the only mechanism (no conditional re-auth header) and a gateway without `capabilities().cvcRecollection` cannot enable saved cards. Fallback: `AUTH_REFRESH_TTL=7d` becomes the default if the step-up does not ship with the payments and organisation stories (D-046 re-opened).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-01 / adjudication (a): the 90-day cap failed ASVS 3.3.2 L1 and NIST AAL1; the D-038 compensations did not exist in the contract; member management, saved-card payment and privacy export were the persistence and financial paths of a hijacked session. One mechanism for saved cards avoids a conditional `x-reauth-*` extension and a second UX path while keeping PCI SAQ A (the CVC stays in the Stripe iframe). Requirement side: D-046/D-047 (REQ-AUTH-007/020/021/022/025, US-291).
- **Alternatives:** plain 7-day refresh (kept only as the fallback); conditional re-auth proof on checkout (rejected: contract machinery for a case Stripe and the Mock gateway already cover).
- **Reversibility:** High (configuration keys, route metadata, one response field).

### D-ARCH-018 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-02 (HIGH): two carrier bulkheads, anonymous budget, daily quota guard, `CARRIER_BUSY` retryable in the saga, subscribe-only SSE with caps (ADR-0010 am. 1–6, ADR-0014 am. 3, ADR-0016 am. 9)
- **Decision:** Per carrier account: `rates` pool (`getRates`, `findServicePoints`, `validateAddress`, `estimateLandedCost`) = 6 and `transactional` pool (`createShipment`, `cancelShipment`, `schedulePickup`, `cancelPickup`, `uploadCustomsDocuments`, `track`) = 4; guests hold ≤ 50 % of `rates` slots and share a global Redis token bucket `CARRIER_ANON_CALLS_PER_MIN=120` per account (exhausted → `CARRIER_BUSY` or cached results, event `carrier_anon_budget_exhausted`); daily quota guard `CARRIER_DAILY_QUOTA` (0 = off; 500 for the DHL sandbox) with `CARRIER_QUOTA_RESERVE_PCT=40` (anonymous fan-out stops at 60 %, only transactional above 90 %, alarms + `carrier_quota_pressure` at 70/90 %). `CARRIER_BUSY` and quota refusals are local admission rejections (`LOCAL_REJECT`): retryable, non-ambiguous, retried with backoff inside the 30-minute saga deadline, never by themselves moving a shipment to `DRAFT`/`EXCEPTION` or releasing the authorisation. Streams never fan out; concurrent streams capped at 2 per IP and 5 per user. Metrics per pool and a quota gauge.
- **Why:** T-46 / SEC-P3-02: one shared bulkhead coupled anonymous floods to paid bookings (CF3) and to sandbox quotas. Configuration defaults are the product-manager's (D-048, REQ-CARR-022/023, NFR-RES-007, US-293). Residual MEDIUM: guests (not customers) can still be denied rate shopping by a distributed flood until the M5 WAF (RR-03).
- **Alternatives:** single pool with a reserve counter (rejected: still couples queue wait); refusing guests under pressure (rejected: cached results and other carriers still serve them).
- **Reversibility:** High (configuration keys; one new saga outcome class).

### D-ARCH-019 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-03 (HIGH): `platform_audit_owner`, identity-based DDL event trigger, hourly ECDSA P-256 signed anchors with a mandatory external sink, locked sink as a G4-production precondition (ADR-0007 am. 7, ADR-0021 am. 1, data-model §4.4/§5, platform.md §4.3/§4.8, risks.md R-ARCH-18, SPK-08)
- **Decision:** A `NOLOGIN NOINHERIT` role `platform_audit_owner` owns every audit object and the audit functions; `platform_migrator` is not a member; audit DDL runs only in the bootstrap lane with a linter marker. Event trigger `platform.audit_ddl_guard()` on `ddl_command_end`/`sql_drop` uses `pg_event_trigger_ddl_commands()`/`pg_event_trigger_dropped_objects()` (object identity, not statement text) and allows only `platform_audit_owner` (inside `ensure_audit_partitions`). After every hourly incremental verification the worker writes a `CHAIN_CHECKPOINT`, the checkpoint heads and an `audit_anchors` record (`anchor_seq` dense, `prev_anchor_hash` chain, `anchor_hash`, `signature`) signed with **ECDSA P-256/SHA-256** under a worker-only private key (KMS `ECC_NIST_P256` non-exportable at M5); verifiers hold public keys only; the HMAC `AUDIT_CHECKPOINT_KEY` is dropped. The record is also appended to `AUDIT_ANCHOR_SINK`, mandatory in every environment (file on a dedicated volume locally; S3 Object Lock compliance mode or retention-locked CloudWatch Logs created in the per-account bootstrap stack with an explicit deny for CI apply roles at M5). The verifier fails on `ANCHOR_MISSING` (gap, duplicate, reorder, sink/table disagreement) and `CHECKPOINT_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, and recomputes streams at the anchored sequences hourly; nightly covers all anchors since the previous nightly. Residual MEDIUM (≤ 1 h superuser rewrite window) accepted for this run; the locked sink, RDS master break-glass and `pgaudit` DDL logging are a **G4 precondition for any production release** (R-ARCH-18).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-03 / T-84: the migrator login owned the audit tables, the event trigger matched statement text (defeated by dynamic SQL), anchors were nightly, symmetric and optional. ECDSA P-256 rather than Ed25519 because AWS KMS offers no Ed25519 signing and one algorithm keeps a single verifier for local and M5.
- **Alternatives:** keep HMAC with a key outside the DB (rejected: every verifier can forge; a regulator cannot verify); nightly cadence (rejected: 24 h rewrite window); Ed25519 (rejected: no KMS support).
- **Reversibility:** Medium during this run (roles, one table, one function); low once anchors are in a locked sink (by design).

### D-ARCH-020 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-04 (HIGH): `npm ci --ignore-scripts` with a committed `.npmrc` and an allow-listed rebuild step, 7-day release cooldown, lockfile provenance and `npm audit signatures`, image-job isolation (ADR-0023 am. F; platform.md §2.1/§3.2/§3.3/§7.1/§7.2)
- **Decision:** Root `.npmrc` with `ignore-scripts=true` (plus `engine-strict`, `save-exact`, `fund=false`, `update-notifier=false`; `min-release-age=7` added once SPK-01 confirms the bundled npm supports it); `npm ci --ignore-scripts` in every Dockerfile stage, workflow step and quality script; install-time work only through `npm run deps:rebuild` (`npm rebuild bcrypt --ignore-scripts=false` if the prebuild needs it, `npx prisma generate`, `npx playwright install --with-deps <browser>` for e2e); lane A `check-install-flags` fails any unflagged install and a repository test rejects lifecycle-style npm scripts. `DEP_MIN_AGE_DAYS=7` in the package-age script (lockfile diff on PRs, whole lockfile in lane C; security-fix override via `tools/ci/dependency-overrides.json` with a DECISIONS.md ID and ≤ 30-day expiry); Dependabot `cooldown: { default-days: 7 }`. `check-lockfile` requires `https://registry.npmjs.org/` sources and `sha512` integrity for every entry; `npm audit signatures` in lane A. `images` is the only `ci.yml` job with `packages: write` (the `release.yml` promote job re-tags by digest and runs no npm), needs `build-and-scan`, runs no package scripts. Enforced from the first `npm ci` in Wave 0 (SEC-TC-32/45; R-ARCH-20, SPK-01 extended).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-04 / T-102: lifecycle scripts ran on hosted runners holding `GITHUB_TOKEN` with `packages: write`, in BuildKit and on the developer host that holds sandbox credentials; a freshly hijacked version could be adopted on publication day; nothing checked lockfile sources.
- **Alternatives:** scoped install scripts per package (`npm` has no allow-list; rejected); private registry proxy with quarantine (budget $0; rejected for now).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-021 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-07 (MEDIUM): evidence verdict recomputed, required-suite manifest, untracked-file and `SKIP_*` checks, narrowed staleness exemption, hosted `security-integration` subset for security-critical paths, wave-end inventory cross-check; lane C `wave-end` dispatches default 5 → 3 (ADR-0023 am. G; platform.md §7.2/§7.8; risks.md R-ARCH-19)
- **Decision:** `quality:check-evidence` derives the verdict from the attached JUnit/JSON; `tools/quality/required-suites.json` lists required suites per profile and changed-path rule, with `skipped = 0` for the security suites (RLS cross-tenant, RBAC matrix, audit generator/immutability, abuse, SSRF, CSRF/cookie, idempotency, catalog C1–C12); the clean-tree check covers untracked files on code/test/config paths; `summary.json` records lockfile SHA-256, Testcontainers digests and the names of `SKIP_*`/`TEST_*` variables (any `SKIP_*` → `INVALID`); the docs-only staleness exemption excludes `docs/03-architecture/api/**` and tooling inputs. PRs touching `packages/authz/**`, `packages/audit/**`, `packages/config/**`, `prisma/**`, `apps/api/src/platform/**`, `apps/api/src/modules/identity/**` run a hosted `security-integration` job (Testcontainers PostgreSQL + Valkey; RLS, RBAC, audit, CSRF, catalog suites; ≈ 10–12 min) that degraded mode never waives. The lane C `wave-end` run compares suite inventories with the wave's lane B evidence; a discrepancy fails the wave end, is logged as an incident and reverts the PR. To fund ≈ 165 min/month the default number of `wave-end` dispatches drops from 5 to 3 (estimate ≈ 1,825 min ≈ 91 %); the 70/90/100 % guards stand.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-07 / adjudication (h): the integrity checks caught stale and wrong-SHA evidence but not a flipped verdict, a silently skipped suite or an untracked override; the docs-only exemption covered the contract that drives the drift test; security-regressing code could sit on `main` until wave end.
- **Alternatives:** hosted integration on every PR (≈ 800 min/month over budget); trust lane B for security paths (rejected by the review, RR-18).
- **Reversibility:** High (scripts, one manifest, one workflow condition, one repository variable).

### D-ARCH-022 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-08 (MEDIUM): key and secret register with `key_id` and AAD, encrypted carrier token cache, Valkey ACL users per service, environment-scoped CI secrets, restricted Stripe key, staging placeholder refusal, debug flags refused, developer-host hygiene (ADR-0018 am. 1–4, ADR-0010 am. 7, ADR-0012 am. 2, ADR-0013 am. 4, ADR-0023 am. H; platform.md §2.1/§2.2/§2.7/§7.1/§7.3/§9.2/§9.3; data-model §10.4)
- **Decision:** One key per purpose (config rejects duplicates and short keys); every key variable is `<key_id>:<base64>` with an optional `_PREVIOUS` for dual reads; every ciphertext and HMAC lookup column carries `key_id`; AES-256-GCM with 96-bit nonce and AAD = table ‖ column ‖ row id or token ‖ key_id; KMS envelope at M5. The register in ADR-0018 lists 20 keys/secrets with algorithm, holder, storage, rotation and blast radius (incl. the new `CARRIER_TOKEN_CACHE_KEY` and the ECDSA `AUDIT_ANCHOR_SIGNING_KEY`). Carrier OAuth tokens in Redis are encrypted (or memory-only); Valkey ACL users `api`/`worker` (plus a ping-only `healthcheck` user) with key-prefix limits locally (`infra/local/valkey/users.acl`) and in AWS. Carrier/Stripe test secrets are environment secrets of `carrier-sandbox` (main-only, dispatch-only, `contract-live` only) with a lane A workflow policy test. Default Stripe key is a restricted `rk_test_` key (minimum permissions); one webhook secret per endpoint. Placeholder/`local-only-*` refusal for `APP_ENV ∈ {staging, production}`; `NODE_DEBUG`/`DEBUG`/`--inspect` refused outside local; nodemailer and AWS SDK debug off. Developer host: credentials only in user-scoped env vars or git-ignored `.env.local`; Gitleaks pre-commit hook via `npm run hooks:install`.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-08 / adjudication (g): 13+ keys with no inventory, rotation or AAD; tokens in Redis in clear (R-16); repository secrets readable by any branch workflow (T-103); a full Stripe key broader than needed; placeholder refusal only in production.
- **Reversibility:** High (configuration format and columns added before any data exists).

### D-ARCH-023 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-10 (MEDIUM): `createQuote` and `createQuoteShare` are `x-idempotency: not-applicable`; `createQuoteShare` mints a new token and revokes the previous share; idempotency scope rewritten (ADR-0014 am. 4; openapi.yaml, api/README §5)
- **Decision:** Both operations lose the `Idempotency-Key` parameter, the `Idempotent-Replayed` header and the idempotency error codes; the drift test asserts `not-applicable` for every route on the ADR-0014 amendment 1 one-time-secret list, which gains `activateTotp` (it returns the recovery codes). `createQuoteShare` always mints a new token, sets `revoked_at` on the previous active share and returns 201; it never returns an existing token (only the hash is stored). README §5 "Scope" = organisation, or guest-token hash, or (anonymous) client IP /64 HMAC, plus actor, method, path and key; bodies encrypted with `key_id` and AAD; one-time-secret routes never stored.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-10 / T-79: the contract still allowed storing responses carrying `guest_token` and `share_token`, and "returns the active share link" was unimplementable without storing the token in clear. Mint-and-revoke rather than 409 `SHARE_EXISTS` because a re-sharing user wants a working link and revoking an older, possibly leaked link is the safer default.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-024 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-11 (MEDIUM): admin idle timeout driven by user input, 30-minute sliding admin refresh token (ADR-0006 am. 13, ADR-0026 am. 8)
- **Decision:** Server: `AUTH_ADMIN_REFRESH_IDLE_TTL=30m`, `refresh_tokens.expires_at = min(now + 30 min, absolute)` on every admin refresh; idle refresh → 401 `SESSION_EXPIRED`. Client: idle clock reset only by pointer/keyboard/touch/wheel input (never focus, visibility or background refetch); `alertdialog` warning at 28 minutes with "Stay signed in" (immediate refresh) and "Sign out"; at 30 minutes stop silent refresh, `logoutSession`, route to `/sign-in?reason=idle`. WCAG 2.2.1 satisfied. Requirement side D-052 (a).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-11: unconditional silent refresh every 14 minutes kept an unattended staff workstation signed in indefinitely; staff hold cross-tenant audit access.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-025 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-12 (MEDIUM): worker resolves tenant context from PostgreSQL via `platform.job_context(kind, id)`; job payload v2 carries identifiers only; Joi validation at dequeue (ADR-0013 am. 1–5; data-model §4.5)
- **Decision:** Payload `{ v: 2, kind, id, correlation_id, trace_context }` (no `org_id`, `actor_id` or business data; v1 never implemented). Processors call the allow-listed `SECURITY DEFINER` function `platform.job_context` (owner `platform_definer`, catalog test C11) before `withTenantTx`; no row → DLQ non-retryable; a payload carrying `org_id` → DLQ with security event `job_context_mismatch`. Per-queue Joi schemas validated at dequeue (`PAYLOAD_INVALID`); e2e asserts the schema on every job in Redis (SEC-TC-50); SEC-TC-20 is mandatory. Valkey ACL users per service (D-ARCH-022).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-12 / T-73: anyone who could write to Redis could make the worker act inside another tenant's RLS context, which RLS cannot detect because the context itself was forged.
- **Reversibility:** High (no code exists yet).

### D-ARCH-026 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-13 (MEDIUM): raw carrier payload redaction replaces embedded documents with `$blob` descriptors and allow-lists tracking/response fields (ADR-0010 am. 8)
- **Decision:** Every base64/binary document field in stored carrier requests and responses becomes `{"$blob": {sha256, size, media_type, document_id?}}`; tracking and response payloads are allow-list redacted (canonical codes, timestamps, carrier references, city/country, service/surcharge codes, amounts) with everything else `[REDACTED]`; a property test with synthetic PII and a sentinel PDF guards it (SEC-TC-52). Requirement side D-052 (b).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-13 / T-95: field-name redaction never touched binary content, so the `raw-carrier-payloads` bucket would have held receiver names, addresses and full tax IDs beyond the reach of erasure.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-027 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-14 (MEDIUM): staff first TOTP enrolment needs an e-mailed one-time code outside local/test; 24 h enrolment deadline enforced now by `housekeeping`; non-local seeds create staff without passwords (ADR-0006 am. 14; openapi `requestTotpEnrolmentCode`, `enrolment_code`, 403 `MFA_ENROLMENT_CODE_REQUIRED`; data-model §3.1)
- **Decision:** `POST /auth/mfa/totp/enrolment-code` (`email-send` class, `x-mfa-gate-exempt`, identical 202) sends a 6-digit single-use code (10 min); `enrolTotp` requires it as `enrolment_code` for an OPERATOR member's first enrolment when `APP_ENV ∉ {local, test}` (missing, wrong or expired → 403 `MFA_ENROLMENT_CODE_REQUIRED`; wrong attempts count against the per-user MFA lock). Overdue enrolment (24 h after first sign-in) → `status=SUSPENDED`, `status_reason=MFA_ENROLMENT_OVERDUE`, sessions revoked; re-activation is an audited operator CLI command in M1, an admin screen in M2. Non-local seeds create staff with no `user_credentials` row and e-mail a `SET_PASSWORD` link. Requirement side D-052 (c).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-14 / T-23 residual: whoever first signed in with a seeded or leaked staff password could enrol their own TOTP and become Super Admin; the deadline was deferred to M2.
- **Reversibility:** High (one operation, one optional field, one job rule).

### D-ARCH-028 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Alignment amendments for the review's remaining Medium/Low/Info items that product-manager already reworded (SEC-P3-05/06/09/15/21/25): ADR-0006 am. 15, ADR-0010 am. 9, ADR-0012 am. 3, ADR-0016 am. 7–10, ADR-0019 am. 1, ADR-0021 am. 1–3, ADR-0026 am. 9
- **Decision:** To keep G2 free of REQ-vs-ADR contradictions after D-049/D-050/D-051/D-052, the ADRs now state: registration oracle corrected and accepted as RR-16 with `email-send` class and `registration_probe`; verification revokes other sessions, reset verifies the mailbox, unverified accounts expire after 7 days, no automatic linking of future social/OTP logins to unverified accounts (ADR-0006 am. 15); Mock gated by `MOCK_CARRIER_ENABLED`/`MOCK_SCENARIOS_ENABLED`, carrier pushes confirmed by `track()` before terminal transitions (ADR-0010 am. 9); Stripe `livemode` rejection, event-type allow-list, SDK on the SSRF-safe dispatcher (ADR-0012 am. 3); RateLimit `draft-6`, step-up metadata, SSE caps, SDK egress exception register (ADR-0016 am. 7–10); `invoice_print: FULL | MASKED`, SENSITIVE documents never attached, Viewer without `document:download`, render-only decryption (ADR-0019 am. 1); `audit-anchors` sink, label byte validation, SENSITIVE document storage (ADR-0021 am. 1–3); `localStorage` guest-token conditions incl. the 7-day server grace and lazy Stripe.js (ADR-0026 am. 9). Not yet in the contract: `x-origin-check` on login/register/reset/verify (SEC-P3-18, LOW) and the edge strip of `X-Mock-Scenario` in the Nginx config (platform.md) — Wave-0 follow-ups for api-designer and devops-engineer.
- **Why:** The review's LOW/INFO items are owner backlog, but where the PM has already changed a requirement the ADR must say the same thing or G2 flags a contradiction. Each item is a wording or configuration-level change with no scope effect.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-053 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · G2-C3 / SEC-COND-07 (SEC-P3-29): unverified users are anonymous callers for the carrier budget; `quote-create` rate class 30/min + 300/h per user, 1,000/h per organisation; per-organisation daily-quota share `CARRIER_QUOTA_ORG_SHARE_PCT=10`
- **Decision:** (1) A signed-in user whose status is `PENDING_VERIFICATION` is an **anonymous caller** for every carrier budget of D-048: the global bucket `CARRIER_ANON_CALLS_PER_MIN=120`, the 50 % `rates`-slot share and the 60 % daily-quota stop (REQ-CARR-022, REQ-QUOTE-002, DR-B32, NFR-RES-007); the class is decided per request from the user status and flips to verified as soon as `email_verified_at` is set, without re-login. (2) An authenticated `POST /quotes` leaves the 600/min `authenticated` class and gets the new **`quote-create`** class: 30/min and 300/h per user, 1,000/h per organisation (REQ-SEC-005, DR-A22, NFR-SEC-005; US-040 AC); guest quote creation stays in the 20/min + 100/h per-IP class. (3) Each organisation may consume at most **`CARRIER_QUOTA_ORG_SHARE_PCT=10`** of a carrier account's `CARRIER_DAILY_QUOTA` in `rates`-pool calls per UTC day; beyond it that organisation receives `CARRIER_BUSY` or cached results for the account, `carrier_quota_org_share_exhausted` fires at most once per organisation per account per minute (REQ-SEC-012), other organisations and all transactional calls are unaffected; the share is off when the quota is `0` (Mock) and resets with the day counter (REQ-CARR-023, NFR-RES-007). US-293 AC extended (SEC-TC-62), EC-B50/B51 added, A-094 updated, A-099 new. principal-architect amends ADR-0010 and api-designer the contract (`x-rate-limit-class: quote-create`) in parallel.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-29 showed the residual of SEC-P3-02: registration is free and sign-in precedes verification (D-009), so one account at 600 quotes/min could push the seeded DHL sandbox (500/day) to 90 % in about a minute and stop rate shopping for every customer until midnight UTC. Treating unverified accounts as anonymous closes the free bypass without touching D-009. 30/min is roughly ten times what a real wizard user produces (each rate-relevant edit creates a quote, REQ-QUOTE-018), 300/h and 1,000/h bound a scripted account and a 20-seat business account without pinching either, and a 10 % share lets ten organisations quote a sandbox carrier all day before the anonymous stop at 60 % is reached, while one hostile organisation can no longer deny the others. The org share counts carrier calls, not cache hits, so the 300 s cache (REQ-CARR-010) keeps popular lanes cheap.
- **Alternatives:** Verification required to quote (rejected: breaks the guest-first funnel of D-009 and the §6.1 demo); keep 600/min and rely on the quota guard alone (rejected: the guard protects the account, not the other customers); a per-user share instead of per-organisation (rejected: a hostile organisation can mint users; the organisation is the billing and tenancy unit).
- **Reversibility:** High (three configuration keys and one rate-class assignment; SEC-TC-62 re-thresholds). Counts unchanged (no new REQ or story).

### D-054 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · G2-C3 / SEC-COND-06 (SEC-P3-28): saved-card checkout with CVC re-collection is authorised only when `cvc_check = pass`
- **Decision:** When REQ-PAY-014's CVC re-collection was required (`auth_time` > 12 h), one payment-state derivation, used by both the confirm call and the webhook processor, treats the payment as `AUTHORISED` only if the retrieved charge reports `payment_method_details.card.checks.cvc_check = 'pass'`. Any other value (`fail`, `unavailable`, `unchecked`, absent) voids the authorisation (manual capture, no money moves), sets the payment `FAILED` with `failure_code = AUTHENTICATION_FAILED`, writes the audited `UPDATE` on `payment`, emits `cvc_recollection_failed` (REQ-SEC-012), returns the shipments to QUOTED and counts the attempt against REQ-PAY-010's 5 per order per hour. The client offers "use another card" or "confirm it's you" (REQ-AUTH-020 proof, which advances `auth_time` so the retry needs no CVC). A checkout without re-collection ignores `cvc_check` (unchanged). The Mock gateway exposes CVC outcomes `pass`, `fail` and `unavailable` through `test_token` (contract names by api-designer). REQ-PAY-014 and US-217 AC amended (SEC-TC-37), EC-B52 added, A-100 new. The Radar rule "Block if CVC verification fails" on the test account and ADR-0012 am. 1 are principal-architect's.
- **Why:** Ruling 1 of the review accepted CVC re-collection as the only saved-card step-up on the condition that collection is verified: issuers may authorise despite a failed CVC, Radar ignores `unavailable`, and the design never read `cvc_check`, so a hijacker of a stale session could type any three digits. Checking one field closes the gap; voiding rather than capturing keeps the "no silent charge" promise of D-046; the re-auth fallback keeps the single-mechanism decision (no conditional header on checkout). Cost: one extra step for the rare stale-session saved-card checkout whose issuer does not verify CVC.
- **Alternatives:** Require a re-auth proof on every saved-card checkout (rejected: friction on the common fresh-session case and a second UX path); accept the gateway's authorisation as-is (rejected: it is the finding).
- **Reversibility:** High (one derivation rule; fake-outcome tests). REQ-PAY-014 and US-217 stay Should.

### D-055 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · SEC-P3-32: share link mint-and-revoke audited, mint cap 10 per quote per hour, byte-identical 404 for unknown, revoked, superseded and expired tokens
- **Decision:** REQ-QUOTE-015 now states: (1) every creation mints a new token and revokes the previous active share in the same transaction, writing `CREATE` for the new share and a separate `UPDATE` on `quote_share` (`revoked_at` set, reason `SUPERSEDED`, actor) for the old one; an explicit revoke still writes `DELETE`; any member holding `quote:share` can thereby revoke another member's link to the same quote (inside the tenant, audited). (2) At most `QUOTE_SHARE_MINTS_PER_HOUR=10` per quote, then 429 `RATE_LIMITED` (DR-A22, NFR-SEC-005). (3) `POST /public/shared-quotes/resolve` returns one byte-identical 404 for unknown, revoked, superseded **and expired** tokens; the "expired quote with inputs prefilled" shared view is **dropped** in favour of a generic "this shared quote is no longer available" page with a link to start a new quote. EC-B29 rewritten, EC-B53 added, US-165 AC amended (SEC-TC-53), A-101 new. api-designer updates `resolveSharedQuote` (today it still documents `EXPIRED` with inputs) and the `x-audit` of `createQuoteShare`.
- **Why:** Ruling 3 of the review accepted mint-and-revoke on these three conditions. The audit entry makes the implicit revocation visible to the owner whose link stopped working; the cap bounds revoked-row churn (600/min would otherwise allow about 36,000 rows an hour); the uniform 404 stops a holder of an old or leaked link from learning whether a quote still exists. The product value lost is small: the recipient of a share link is a third party, the quote lives 24 h, and the owner keeps the full re-quote path (REQ-QUOTE-017); US-165 is Should.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the expired view with prefilled inputs and accept the distinguishable state (rejected: contradicts the review's condition for a Should feature with little value after expiry); 409 `SHARE_EXISTS` instead of mint-and-revoke (rejected earlier by principal-architect, ADR-0014 am. 4).
- **Reversibility:** High (one configuration key, one response rule).

### D-056 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · SEC-P3-30 and SEC-P3-33 (items 3 and 4) requirement-side alignment: security-event catalogue completed; REQ-AUTH-018 follows ADR-0006 am. 14; REQ-BOOK-023 names `LOCAL_REJECT` and keeps DRAFT at the deadline
- **Decision:** (a) REQ-SEC-012 lists the ADR-only and verification-added events `job_context_mismatch`, `webhook_livemode_rejected`, `audit_anchor_missing`, `cvc_recollection_failed` and `carrier_quota_org_share_exhausted`, and names the contract test that compares `packages/shared` with `api/README.md` §13 (SEC-TC-63); api-designer adds the same names to §13. (b) REQ-AUTH-018 adopts ADR-0006 am. 14: the 24 h TOTP enrolment deadline starts at the staff member's **first sign-in** (`first_signed_in_at`), not at membership creation (a never-signed-in staff member is never suspended); a missing, wrong, expired or used enrolment code answers **403 `MFA_ENROLMENT_CODE_REQUIRED`** (was 401 `MFA_INVALID`), still counted by the per-user MFA lock and logged as `mfa_failed`; suspension sets `status_reason = MFA_ENROLMENT_OVERDUE`; re-activation in M1 is the audited CLI `npm run ops:staff-reactivate -- <email>` (SYSTEM_JOB actor), the admin screen is M2, a local seed re-run still works; re-enrolment after a first activation uses the re-auth proof. US-025 AC and A-098 amended. (c) REQ-BOOK-023 names the outcome class `LOCAL_REJECT`, states that the shipment stays in BOOKING with the attempt count recorded while local rejections are retried, and confirms **DRAFT** (not EXCEPTION) with trigger `SYSTEM_TIMEOUT` when the 30-minute deadline passes under `CARRIER_BUSY`, because no carrier request was ever sent and there is nothing ambiguous to review; the local-rejection backoff stays 2 s base, factor 2, full jitter, 60 s cap (configuration). US-205 note amended. principal-architect aligns ADR-0014 am. 3 (deadline state and backoff base) in parallel. (d) US-027 checked after D-047: M1, Must, not DC in the epic table, the story and `traceability.md`; consistent with REQ-AUTH-021/022 (M1 Must) and REQ-ADMIN-007 (M1 Should); no change.
- **Why:** G2 criterion 6 flags REQ-vs-ADR contradictions; the security-analyst catalogued these three in SEC-P3-33 for P4 exit. For (b) the ADR's design is the better product rule as well: a deadline from membership creation would suspend a staff member who was invited on Friday and first signs in on Monday, and seeded staff before the demo. For (c) the analyst's reading is right: DRAFT is what the saga does for "nothing happened at the carrier", and EXCEPTION would park a clean shipment in a queue that M2 staff tooling has to clear. For (a) uncatalogued events are unmonitored (A09).
- **Alternatives:** Keep membership creation as the deadline start and ask the architect to change the ADR (rejected: worse behaviour, see above); EXCEPTION at the deadline (rejected, see above).
- **Reversibility:** High (wording, one status code, one state).

### D-057 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · RR-19 (tricked verification of a pre-registered account) accepted as PRD risk R-15; optional sign-in-before-verification control not applied
- **Decision:** The residual risk recorded by the security-analyst in review §14.2 (SEC-P3-06 row) and §14.8 is **accepted**: a victim who redeems the verification link of an account an attacker pre-registered with the victim's address gives the attacker a verified account whose password the attacker holds; the attacker's session is revoked at verification (D-050) but they can sign in again until the victim resets the password. Mitigations already in place: the verification e-mail says "if you did not create this account, ignore this e-mail", the "account already exists" e-mail carries a reset entry point, verification revokes every other session, a redeemed reset token takes the account back and verifies the mailbox, and unverified accounts expire after 7 days (REQ-AUTH-001/004/012/026). The optional control "an anonymous verification redemption requires sign-in first" is **not** applied. PRD §9 R-15 (RR-19) added; A-102 records the validation (monthly review of anonymous-redemption share and of password resets within 24 h of verification; any pre-hijack report re-opens the control).
- **Why:** Opening the verification link on a different device than the one that registered (phone vs laptop) is the common path (D-UX-024, F02); forcing a sign-in there costs funnel conversion on every registration to defend against an attack that needs the victim to act on an e-mail for an account they never created, within 7 days, and yields the attacker an empty account the victim can reclaim by reset. LOW likelihood × LOW impact, with a working recovery path, is below the bar for new friction.
- **Alternatives:** Require sign-in before anonymous redemption (rejected: friction on the common path); send a post-verification security notice "if this wasn't you, reset your password" (not adopted: the verification e-mail already carries the warning and the victim is the one clicking; can be added later as a notification template without a requirement change).
- **Reversibility:** High (one guard on the verify route if ever needed).

### D-UX-030 · 2026-11-12 · ux-designer · G2-C4: US-027 Must makes `/account/security` (sessions section) and S-D08 Must screens; coarse IP shown as the API's CIDR range
- **Decision:** After D-047, S-C07 `/account/security` is a Must route from M1 whose Must content is the "Where you're signed in" section (`SessionList`); the Password and Two-step verification sections stay Should and render only when built. "Security" joins the account nav for every signed-in user. S-D08 My sessions is Must. The coarse IP is shown verbatim as the API returns it (`203.0.113.0/24`, `2001:db8:12::/48`) under a visible "IP address range" header, replacing the earlier `203.0.113.x` rendering. Remote sign-out of one session needs no confirmation; "Sign out of all other devices" keeps its AlertDialog. README §3 recounted: 218 Must = 110 UI + 108 non-UI; Must UI coverage 100 %.
- **Why:** US-027 AC lists the coarse IP "203.0.113.0/24 style" and six states at 360/1280 in three locales; a verbatim range keeps the screen and the test oracle identical and avoids a client-side IP formatter. A Must story cannot live on a Should-only route.
- **Alternatives:** A separate `/account/sessions` web route (rejected: a second security destination; S-E03 already links to `/account/security`); keep the `x` notation (rejected: diverges from the AC example and hides the prefix length for IPv6).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-UX-031 · 2026-11-12 · ux-designer · ReauthDialog (US-291): one shared overlay; proactive at the last step for always-scopes, reactive on 401 for max-age scopes; one automatic retry; TOTP for MFA users
- **Decision:** A `packages/ui` Dialog "Confirm it's you" (W03 §8, F10 §4) used by both apps through the API client (`useReauth().run(scope, request)`). Always-scopes open it proactively **after** the action's own confirmation and immediately before the request, so the 5-minute single-use proof is consumed within seconds; max-age scopes (`org.member.manage`) send the request first and open it on 401 `REAUTH_REQUIRED` (stale variant from `max_age`), then retry once. A second `REAUTH_REQUIRED` re-prompts once ("That took too long"), then stops with an error toast. Password variant (with a hidden `username` field for password managers) or TOTP variant when MFA is on (always for staff). Purpose text is one key per contract scope plus one instruction key per variant (no concatenation). The proof lives only inside the consuming call. No recovery-code path (the contract does not accept one; README §5 item 13).
- **Why:** One component and one interceptor give every Should consumer (US-026/028/083/085/217/270) the same behaviour; asking at the last step avoids proofs expiring in half-filled forms (2.2.1 by restart); reactive handling for max-age scopes avoids client clock decisions on `auth_time`.
- **Alternatives:** Ask at the start of each flow (rejected: proof may expire before submit); client-side `auth_time` check for max-age scopes (rejected: clock skew; server is authoritative); an `alertdialog` (rejected: user-initiated, not urgent).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-UX-032 · 2026-11-12 · ux-designer · Admin idle-timeout `alertdialog` (extends D-UX-025): 28/30 minutes, shared cross-tab clock, "Extend session" label, Escape = extend
- **Decision:** W00 §2.1 / F06 §4. Idle clock shared by all admin tabs through a `localStorage` timestamp plus `BroadcastChannel` (input events only: pointerdown, keydown, touchstart, wheel). At 28:00 an `alertdialog` opens in every tab with the absolute sign-out time in its description, a per-second countdown hidden from assistive technology, initial focus on "Extend session", and one polite announcement at 1 minute left. Extend = immediate refresh; Escape also extends; overlay clicks and other input do not. At 30:00 (or on waking at ≥ 30:00) all tabs stop refresh, call `logoutSession`, clear state and go to `/sign-in?reason=idle&returnUrl=…`, where "You were signed out for inactivity" is shown. The visible label follows the US-033 AC ("Extend session") rather than ADR-0026 am. 8's descriptive "Stay signed in", so the story's test oracle and the UI agree. en/es/fr copy in content §4.11.
- **Why:** WCAG 2.2.1 "Extend" option (120 s notice, one action, unlimited extensions within the 12 h cap); without a shared clock an idle background tab would sign out a user working in another tab, because the server session is shared; a deliberate Extend (not any keystroke) makes the extension explicit and announced.
- **Alternatives:** Per-tab clocks (rejected: false sign-outs); any input closes the dialog (rejected: accidental extensions while a screen reader user explores the dialog); non-modal banner like the 12 h cap (rejected: ADR-0026 am. 8 and US-033 require a modal warning with focus).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-UX-033 · 2026-11-12 · ux-designer · CVC re-collection UX (US-217 Should): predicted from `auth_time`, server flag authoritative; gateway-hosted field; CVC-specific `AUTHENTICATION_FAILED` alert with three recovery actions
- **Decision:** W08 §5 / F03 §2a. When a saved card is selected and the in-memory token's `auth_time` is older than 12 h, the gateway-hosted CVC field mounts at once; `checkoutOrder`'s `cvc_recollection_required` is authoritative (mount after the fact without charging, or unmount). The Platform renders label, hint (4 digits for American Express), notice and errors outside the iframe and never a CVC input of its own. States: not required, required, loading, empty on Pay, incomplete, submitting, check failed, attempts exhausted, field failed to load, offline. A failed check (`AUTHENTICATION_FAILED` on an attempt with re-collection and no 3DS challenge; any `cvc_check` other than `pass`, D-054) shows "We couldn't verify this card's security code. You haven't been charged." with "Enter the code again", "Use a new card" and "Confirm it's you"; the last renders only when the contract provides a re-auth scope for it (README §5 item 12). The message does not distinguish a wrong code from an unavailable check. Mock gateway: a simulated outcome control (pass, fail, unavailable), never free text sent to the API.
- **Why:** Prediction avoids a second Pay press in the common case; server authority keeps it correct across clock skew; PCI SAQ A and REQ-PAY-005 stay intact; neutral wording avoids telling a hijacker whether the issuer checks CVC.
- **Alternatives:** Always wait for the server flag (rejected: two presses of Pay every time); show the CVC field for every saved card (rejected: friction for fresh sessions, contrary to D-046).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-UX-034 · 2026-11-12 · ux-designer · S-W02s route `/q#token=…`; the share fragment is kept, not cleared; one "no longer available" state (D-055)
- **Decision:** S-W02s moves from `/q/{token}` to `/q#token=…` (ADR-0016 am. 6a). The page posts the fragment token to `resolveSharedQuote`. Unlike single-use credentials (D-UX-009: verify, reset, invitation), the fragment is not cleared with `replaceState`, so reload and re-sharing work; the token never enters storage, `history.state`, a query string or logs. Every 404 (unknown, revoked, superseded, expired) and a missing token render one state, "This shared quote is no longer available", with an empty "Get a quote" (the D-055 removal of the prefilled expired view). The share dialog warns that a new link turns off the previous one and handles the 10-per-hour mint cap.
- **Why:** A share link is a deliberately transferable, read-only, personal-data-free bearer link; clearing it would break the one thing it is for. The fragment already keeps it out of server logs and `Referer`.
- **Alternatives:** Clear and keep in `sessionStorage` (rejected: breaks copy-from-address-bar and new-tab opens; adds storage of a token for no gain).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-UX-035 · 2026-11-12 · ux-designer · Staff enrolment e-mail code step detected from the API, not from configuration
- **Decision:** W15 §3 Step 0 / F09 §1. The admin client calls `enrolTotp`; a 403 `MFA_ENROLMENT_CODE_REQUIRED` inserts "Confirm your email" (Step 1 of 4), sends `requestTotpEnrolmentCode` once on entry, offers "Send a new code" after a 60 s cooldown, and maps wrong/expired codes to one inline message; `MFA_LOCKED` and `RATE_LIMITED` use the standard alerts. In local and test the flow keeps 3 steps.
- **Why:** The client does not know `APP_ENV`; reacting to the contract keeps one code path and lets the demo (local) stay at 3 steps without a client flag.
- **Alternatives:** A client configuration flag (rejected: a second source of truth that can drift from the server rule).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-UX-036 · 2026-11-12 · ux-designer · v1.3 copy and state alignments in docs/02-ux
- **Decision:** (a) `SESSION_EXPIRED` joins the signed-out notice (W00 §3.8, W03 §1, F10); admin sign-in shows allow-listed `reason` notices only. (b) Registration `RATE_LIMITED` (D-049) uses the generic alert with no hint about other addresses. (c) Expired unverified accounts (US-292) get the generic `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`; the S-E01 e-mail states the limit with `{days}` from `AUTH_UNVERIFIED_TTL`. (d) `CARRIER_BUSY` (US-293, D-053) has its own neutral per-carrier notice "{carrier} is busy right now. Try again in a few minutes." that never names budgets or quotas. (e) S-E03 security notices link to "Review where you're signed in" (now a Must screen). (f) S-E04 attaches the label and links (never attaches) a `SENSITIVE` customs invoice (D-051). New strings in content-and-i18n §4.2, §4.6, §4.9–§4.13 (en/es/fr; es/fr pending linguistic review per D-023).
- **Why:** Requirements v1.3 and the ADR amendments changed these behaviours; leaving the UX text unchanged would create REQ-vs-UX contradictions at G3.
- **Alternatives:** None considered; each item follows a decided requirement.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-001 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Suite taxonomy, per-suite JUnit files and the manifest location
- **Decision:** Test evidence is organised by 65 named suites (`docs/05-quality/TEST-STRATEGY.md` §7.1). Each lane B suite writes `<suite>.junit.xml` into the run directory; non-Jest tools (k6, Lighthouse, ZAP, Trivy, Gitleaks, Stryker) keep their native JSON and are also converted to JUnit by `quality:heavy`, so verdict recomputation treats every suite alike. This refines the example file names of ADR-0023 amendment B (`integration`, `api`, `generated-suites`, `e2e-<browser>`). The required-suite manifest is drafted at `docs/05-quality/tests/required-suites.json` and is authoritative until Wave 0 task T-QA-01 moves it verbatim to `tools/quality/required-suites.json` (the canonical, code-owned path of ADR-0023 amendment G); the docs path is listed in `tooling_inputs` meanwhile.
- **Why:** One file per suite lets `quality:check-evidence` enforce presence, `min_tests`, zero-skip and `required_cases` per suite; the manifest content is qa-lead's, but no `tools/` tree exists before Wave 0.
- **Alternatives:** Grouped JUnit files per Jest project (rejected: zero-skip and required cases could not be checked per suite); writing directly to `tools/quality/` now (rejected: creates code paths before the scaffold task owns them).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-002 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Test ID scheme and AC-coverage computation
- **Decision:** IDs `AT-US-NNN-nn` (story scenario by 1-based position, outline rows `-<row>`), `NT-<CAT>-NNN-<x>` (NFR test parts), `SEC-TC-NN`, `J-<area>-NN` (journeys). Tests carry the IDs in square brackets in their titles; `quality:trace` (T-QA-02) derives `ac-trace.json`. Must-AC coverage = G3-counted `AT-` IDs whose tests all pass ÷ G3-counted IDs in `docs/05-quality/tests/gherkin-test-map.md` (1,593 at v1.0; the 6 `@live-sandbox` scenarios are excluded as COND-SANDBOX-DEMO). Orphan IDs fail the trace. The REQ-level Test(s) column of `traceability.md` is filled after senior-developer's Task(s) column, to avoid concurrent edits.
- **Why:** Makes "every Must AC has a passing automated test" mechanically checkable at G3 without hand-maintained matrices.
- **Alternatives:** Cucumber-style executable Gherkin (rejected: a second test runner and step glue for 1,599 scenarios, no gain over IDs); per-story rather than per-scenario IDs (rejected: cannot show partial coverage).
- **Reversibility:** High (IDs are text in test titles).

### D-QA-003 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Ledger 95/95 gate in M1 applies to the money-movement code
- **Decision:** No `ledger` module exists in M0/M1 (ADR-0003, ADR-0012 §8). To honour NFR-QUAL-002 ("ledger code is gated whenever it exists; payments and refunds posting in M1"), the 95 % line / 95 % branch threshold applies to `apps/api/src/modules/payments/service/**` and `packages/shared/src/money/**`; `apps/api/src/modules/ledger/**` is pre-declared and vacuous until M4.
- **Why:** Payment authorisation, capture, partial capture, refund amounts and the CVC outcome rule are the money paths of this run (risk QR-03, exposure 15); leaving NFR-QUAL-002's ledger clause vacuous would under-test them.
- **Alternatives:** Vacuous ledger gate until M4, as platform.md §7.2 says (rejected: weaker than the NFR text); 95/95 on all of `orders` (rejected: saga orchestration is covered by the generated fault matrix; a blanket gate there adds cost without proportional risk reduction).
- **Reversibility:** High; product-manager or principal-architect can narrow it with a superseding decision.

### D-QA-004 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Zero-skip set extended; catalog suite named `catalog-c1-c14`; required cases enforced
- **Decision:** Beyond the nine security suites of ADR-0023 amendment G, every suite that holds a SEC-TC case or a PRD §4.3 guardrail is zero-skip (e.g. `identity-session`, `rate-limit`, `payments-webhooks`, `carrier-resilience`, `saga-fault-matrix`, `redaction`, `log-scan`, `e2e-security`, `k6-abuse`, `ci-policy`). The manifest lists all 64 SEC-TC IDs as `required_cases`; a missing, failed or skipped testcase carrying a SEC-TC ID fails the evidence check in any suite. A JUnit file for a `planned` suite fails until the manifest marks it `active`. The catalog suite is `catalog-c1-c14` (SEC-P3-33 item 7; ADR-0023 amendment G is corrected; `platform.md` §7.2 still says C1–C12).
- **Why:** SEC-P3-07's intent (no silently skipped security evidence) applies to every security case, not only to the suites the ADR named.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the ADR's list only (rejected: most of the 64 cases would live in suites where skips pass).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-005 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Playwright projects: 360 and 1280 on all three engines, plus 390 compatibility projects
- **Decision:** Projects `chromium`, `firefox`, `webkit` × mobile 360 × 780 and desktop 1280 × 800, plus `chromium-390` (Pixel 7) and `webkit-390` (iPhone 14). Firefox mobile uses `hasTouch` without `isMobile` (Playwright does not support `isMobile` in Firefox); this limitation is reported, not hidden.
- **Why:** PRD O1 and §6.2 require 360 px and 1280 px on all three engines, which is stricter than NFR-COMPAT-002's 390 px mobile class; the 390 projects keep NFR-COMPAT-001/002 and NFR-A11Y-004 covered.
- **Alternatives:** 390 only on Firefox and WebKit (rejected: contradicts PRD O1).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-006 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Browser-side payment in the default e2e lane uses the Mock gateway hosted-field double
- **Decision:** Without a Stripe key the Stripe Payment Element cannot load in the browser, so the default e2e lane (and the fixture-backed M1 demo journey) pays through the Mock gateway's hosted-field test double (`mock_ok`, `mock_3ds` with a simulated, keyboard-operable challenge frame, `mock_3ds_fail`, `mock_decline`, CVC outcomes). The Stripe adapter is verified fixture-backed at API level (`payments-webhooks`, REQ-PAY-017). The real Payment Element runs only in `@live-sandbox` (COND-SANDBOX-DEMO). Open item OI-QA-01: frontend must confirm the Mock hosted-field component exists for `APP_ENV` in {local, test}.
- **Why:** REQ-PAY-002 makes the Mock gateway available in every non-production environment; the demo must run without external network access or keys.
- **Alternatives:** Stub `js.stripe.com` with a recorded script (rejected: fragile, licence and CSP issues); skip browser payment in e2e (rejected: PRD §6.2 step 6).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-007 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Zero-tolerance flaky-test policy with a narrow quarantine
- **Decision:** Retries (at most 1, lanes B and C only) exist only to detect flakiness; a retried pass is a flaky result. Quarantine (`@quarantine` plus `docs/05-quality/tests/QUARANTINE.md`) is allowed only for tests outside zero-skip suites, outside the DC journeys J-M0-01..04 and J-M1-01, and that are not the sole verification of a Must `AT-` ID; at most 5 at a time; fix within 1 wave or it becomes an S3 (S2 if it covers a Must AC) and blocks the wave exit. G3: 0 flaky in the gate run, flake rate < 1 % over 20 runs, 0 quarantined Must-AC tests.
- **Why:** NFR-QUAL-006 and the evidence-integrity model (a flaky security test must not be quarantined into silence).
- **Alternatives:** Retry until green (rejected); no quarantine at all (rejected: one flaky non-critical test would stop all merges).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-008 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Screen-reader checks use an ARIA-snapshot proxy unless an AT-capable session exists
- **Decision:** The P6 screen-reader spot check of NFR-A11Y-001 is performed as a review of Playwright ARIA snapshots (names, roles, live-region text, reading order) for sign-in, quote and results, wizard, checkout, tracking, staff TOTP and the audit viewer, plus the manual sheet of `accessibility.md` §10 by accessibility-auditor and qa-lead. Real NVDA/VoiceOver checks are done only if an AT-capable interactive session is available; otherwise the verification report states that they were not performed. NFR-A11Y-001 stays PARTIAL either way.
- **Why:** The run is agent-operated on a host without installed assistive technology; claiming AT checks that did not happen would be a silent stub.
- **Alternatives:** Install NVDA on the host (rejected: D-ORCH-001 keeps the host unchanged, and agents cannot operate it interactively).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-009 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Developer tools excluded from the axe route inventory
- **Decision:** Swagger UI (`/api/docs`), the Mailpit UI and the RustFS console are excluded from the axe route-inventory gate. They are third-party developer tools absent from non-local profiles (SEC-TC-34). E-mail templates are checked against `accessibility.md` §10 row S-E01..E04 through the rendered HTML in Mailpit.
- **Why:** NFR-A11Y-002 targets the Platform's own pages; failing the gate on vendor tooling would not improve user accessibility.
- **Alternatives:** Include them and waive findings one by one (rejected: noise).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-010 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Manifest maintenance: activation and min_tests ratchet
- **Decision:** Suites start `planned` and become `active` in the PR that adds their first test (a JUnit file for a `planned` suite fails the check, which forces the flip). At each wave end qa-lead ratchets `min_tests` to the wave run's counts (generated suites: exact cell counts); lowering a value needs a DECISIONS.md entry. At `gate` every suite must be `active` unless listed in `deferred_at_gate` with a decision ID. The manifest is a security-critical tooling input (SEC-P3-31): changes need security-analyst review.
- **Why:** `min_tests` values cannot be known before code exists; a ratchet stops suites from shrinking silently.
- **Alternatives:** Fixed guesses now (rejected: either meaningless or blocking).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-011 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Conditional SEC-TC cases on Should/Could stories assert route absence when the story is not delivered
- **Decision:** SEC-TC-08, 30 and parts of 22, 28, 37, 52, 53 and 59 exercise routes of Should/Could stories (US-165, US-217, US-265, US-083/085, US-084, US-270, US-228, US-252, US-029). If the story ships, the case tests the behaviour; if not, the same test asserts that the route is absent from the route registry and from `openapi.yaml` (or returns 404), and the G3 report records "route absent" with the deferral decision. For SEC-TC-37, missing step-up on the payment-method and membership routes means the test asserts the D-046 fallback `AUTH_REFRESH_TTL=7d`. Consent-record tests of NFR-PRIV-005 follow the same rule.
- **Why:** Security cases must never be skipped (SEC-P3-07), yet Should stories may be deferred by the re-scope ladder (PRD §7.1).
- **Alternatives:** Skip when absent (rejected: violates zero-skip); force the Should stories to Must (rejected: a product decision, not QA's).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-012 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · Risk-elevated coverage targets are reported, not blocking
- **Decision:** `packages/carriers/**`, `apps/api/src/platform/**`, `apps/api/src/modules/identity/**` and `apps/api/src/modules/orders/**` carry a 90 % line / 80 % branch target reported per wave; a shortfall creates a test-engineer task but does not block merges. Blocking gates remain those of COVERAGE_TARGET (85/75; 95/95 on pricing, audit, authz and the D-QA-003 paths).
- **Why:** These T1 areas get their depth mainly from generated, integration and fault-injection suites; a hard line-coverage gate would add friction without matching risk reduction.
- **Alternatives:** Hard gate (rejected: schedule risk for four parallel developers); no target (rejected: T1 risk).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-QA-013 · 2026-11-12 · qa-lead · G3 needs durable lane C evidence
- **Decision:** Hosted artifacts are kept 1 day (ADR-0023 amendment D), so for G3 (and G4) the lane C `release-candidate` run's `inventory.json` and `coverage-summary.json` are committed into a run directory (`profile: lane-c-release-candidate`, same `<stamp>_<sha7>` naming) by devops-engineer, and the verification report records the run URL and conclusion. The G3 checklist (TEST-STRATEGY.md §15) checks these files.
- **Why:** quality-gatekeeper must be able to verify lane C results from the repository after the artifacts expire.
- **Alternatives:** Longer artifact retention (rejected: storage budget); trust the run URL only (rejected: not verifiable after expiry).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-029 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · ECDSA P-256 anchor signature kept with a corrected rationale (SEC-P3-33 item 1; supersedes the rationale sentence of D-ARCH-019 and corrects the counts in D-ARCH-021 and D-ARCH-022)
- **Decision:** Audit anchors stay `ECDSA_P256_SHA256` (ADR-0007 amendment 8 item 5, data-model §5.5). The stated reason "AWS KMS offers no Ed25519 signing" is withdrawn: KMS has supported `ECC_NIST_EDWARDS25519` since 2025-11-07 (verified against the AWS announcement on 2026-11-12). The real reasons are recorded instead: FIPS 186-5 approval; universal verifier support without new code (`openssl dgst -sha256 -verify`, WebCrypto `ECDSA` in every browser of the ADR-0026 §11 matrix, Node 24 `crypto.verify`, every KMS/HSM the platform may move to incl. Azure Key Vault, which has no Ed25519); KMS `ECDSA_SHA_256` with `MessageType = RAW` signing the same digest Node signs, so one verifier serves local and M5; malleability neutralised by the byte-for-byte sink/table comparison (SEC-TC-44); the `algorithm` CHECK enum can gain `ED25519` later. Record-keeping corrections (DECISIONS.md is append-only, so the earlier entries are not edited): D-ARCH-022 says the key register has "20" entries, ADR-0018 lists 24; D-ARCH-021 and ADR-0023 am. G said the security catalog suite is "C1–C12", it is C1–C14 (ADR-0023 am. G and platform.md §7.3 corrected).
- **Why:** Review ruling 2 accepted ECDSA; SEC-P3-33 item 1 found the justification outdated. Switching to Ed25519 would buy nothing the design needs and would cost a second verifier path and a CHECK migration on `audit_anchors`.
- **Alternatives:** Switch to Ed25519 now (rejected: no gain; broad verifier support is the actual requirement); keep the wrong sentence (rejected: an auditor would read it as uninformed).
- **Reversibility:** High before production data; the `algorithm` column is an extensible enum.

### D-ARCH-030 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-28 (MEDIUM, SEC-COND-06): saved-card checkout is `AUTHORISED` only when `cvc_check = 'pass'`; otherwise void + `FAILED/AUTHENTICATION_FAILED` (ADR-0012 am. 4, ADR-0006 am. 12 marker; contract and README amended)
- **Decision:** When `requireCvcRecollection` was set, one shared derivation function (`derivePaymentState`, used by `confirmPayment`, the Mock synchronous path and both webhook processors) treats the authorisation as `AUTHORISED` only if the retrieved charge reports `payment_method_details.card.checks.cvc_check = 'pass'`; `fail`, `unavailable`, `unchecked` or absent → `voidAuthorisation`, payment `FAILED` with `failure_code = AUTHENTICATION_FAILED`, audited `UPDATE payment` (contract `x-audit.additional`), security event `cvc_recollection_failed`, shipments back to `QUOTED`, attempt counted toward the 5/hour cap, no saga. Recovery: another card or `createReauthProof` (advances `auth_time`; the retry needs no CVC). Mock `test_token` gains `mock_cvc_pass | mock_cvc_fail | mock_cvc_unavailable` (behave as `mock_ok` when recollection is not required); the never-implemented `mock_cvc_required` is withdrawn. The Stripe test account has the Radar rule "Block if CVC verification fails" enabled as a backstop (it ignores `unavailable`, which is why the application rule is the control). No new header or extension: the single-mechanism decision of D-ARCH-017 stands.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-28 / CWE-304: collecting the CVC without reading the outcome let a hijacker of a stale session type any three digits at an issuer that does not verify the CVC. Ruling 1 (CVC recollection instead of a conditional re-auth header) was accepted on this condition.
- **Alternatives:** Conditional `X-Reauth-Proof` on checkout (rejected again: second UX path and a conditional extension); rely on Radar alone (rejected: ignores `unavailable`).
- **Reversibility:** High (payments story not built yet).

### D-ARCH-031 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-29 (MEDIUM, SEC-COND-07): unverified users count as anonymous for the carrier budget; `quote-create` rate class; per-organisation daily-quota share (ADR-0010 am. 10, ADR-0016 am. 11 a; contract and README §7/§9/§13 amended)
- **Decision:** (1) Users in `PENDING_VERIFICATION` are treated as anonymous by `createQuote`: per-IP `public-quote` HTTP limits and the whole anonymous carrier budget (≤ 50 % `rates` share, `CARRIER_ANON_CALLS_PER_MIN` bucket, 60 % quota stop); `email_verified_at IS NOT NULL` is the only way out. (2) New class `quote-create` for verified authenticated callers of `createQuote`: `RATE_QUOTE_CREATE_PER_USER_PER_MIN=30`, `RATE_QUOTE_CREATE_PER_USER_PER_HOUR=300`, `RATE_QUOTE_CREATE_PER_ORG_PER_HOUR=1000` (429 `RATE_LIMITED`), replacing the 600/min `authenticated` class on that route; `x-rate-limit-class` stays `public-quote` and the authenticated class is declared in `x-rate-limit-route` (the drift test compares both). (3) `CARRIER_QUOTA_ORG_SHARE_PCT=10`: an organisation may consume at most 10 % of a carrier account's `CARRIER_DAILY_QUOTA` through rates-pool calls; beyond it `carrier_error {code: CARRIER_BUSY}` or cached results for that carrier, other organisations unaffected, security event `carrier_quota_org_share_exhausted` (at most once per account and organisation per minute); transactional calls are never refused. Values match product-manager's D-053 (DR-A22, REQ-CARR-022/023, US-293). Verification SEC-TC-62; NFR-RES-007 rows updated.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-29 / CWE-770: one free account at 600/min could push a carrier's daily quota to 90 % within about a minute and stop rate shopping to that carrier for every customer until 00:00 UTC; the reserve protected bookings but not the quote funnel.
- **Alternatives:** Require e-mail verification before quoting (rejected: breaks the guest-first funnel of D-009 and still leaves verified accounts able to drain); one authenticated bucket per account (rejected: punishes every organisation for one abuser; the per-organisation share isolates them).
- **Reversibility:** High (configuration keys; no code yet).

### D-ARCH-032 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-32 (LOW, ruling 3 conditions): share-link revocation audited, mint cap counted in PostgreSQL, one identical 404 including expired tokens per D-055; `x-audit.additional` added to the contract (ADR-0016 am. 11 b–c; openapi, README §5/§7/§10/§15, data-model §3.10)
- **Decision:** `createQuoteShare` writes `CREATE quote_share` plus `UPDATE quote_share` (`revoked_at` null → value, reason `SUPERSEDED`, actor) in one transaction, declared through the new vendor-extension member `x-audit.additional[] {action, entity, sensitivity, when}` so the audit test generator asserts the second entry (also used by `checkoutOrder` for the CVC-failure `UPDATE payment`). The cap is `QUOTE_SHARE_MINTS_PER_HOUR=10` per quote per rolling hour (429 `RATE_LIMITED`; a refused mint revokes nothing), counted in PostgreSQL inside the minting transaction over the new index `ix_quote_shares__quote_created` and serialised by `ux_quote_shares__quote_active` (no Redis counter, so a race cannot overshoot). `resolveSharedQuote` returns one byte-identical 404 `NOT_FOUND` for unknown, revoked, superseded and expired tokens; `quote_share_resolve` adds `AND expires_at > now()` and loses its `status` column; `SharedQuote.status` is `ACTIVE` only. **Withdrawn the same day:** the principal-architect's first draft kept the v1.3 EC-B29 "expired quote with inputs prefilled" view (200 `EXPIRED`) as a reconciliation with SEC-TC-53; product-manager's D-055 rewrote EC-B29/REQ-QUOTE-015 to the uniform 404, so the design follows the requirement and SEC-TC-53 stands as amended in the review. data-architect's open-conflict note in data-model §3.10 is closed accordingly.
- **Why:** Ruling 3 accepted mint-and-revoke on these three conditions; a uniform 404 stops a holder of an old or leaked link from learning whether a quote still exists, and the owner keeps the full re-quote path (REQ-QUOTE-017).
- **Alternatives:** Redis route counter for the cap (rejected: a second source of truth beside the rows it bounds); keep the expired view (rejected by D-055).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-033 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SEC-P3-27 (MEDIUM, SEC-COND-05): `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp` on every definer and audit function, OID-derived schema guard, `platform_schema_owner` adopted (review item 6), C11/C13/C14 amended, RDS ADMIN-row rule (ADR-0007 am. 8, ADR-0005 am. 8, data-model §4.2/§4.4/§4.5/§4.6/§5.2/§5.3/§5.7/§8.3/§12 A13, platform.md §4.3, risks.md SPK-08)
