- **Decision:** (1) `ensure_audit_partitions`, `audit_forbid_mutation`, `audit_vault_guard`, `audit_ddl_guard`, every §4.5 `platform_definer` function, `app_is_operator` and `job_context` declare `SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp` with schema-qualified relations and `pg_catalog.`-qualified built-ins and casts; every bootstrap script starts with the same `SET search_path` before `SET ROLE`; the application-side advisory-lock calls are qualified too. (2) `audit_ddl_guard` matches the protected schemas by OID derived at call time from the objects owned by `platform_audit_owner` (fail-closed when empty; not baked into the body because `pg_restore` and RDS restores renumber OIDs) and refuses `ALTER SCHEMA … RENAME`/`OWNER TO`/`COMMENT`/`DROP SCHEMA`; `sql_drop` matches by OID with the rename-proof name as fallback. (3) Review item 6 adopted: `NOLOGIN NOINHERIT` no-member role `platform_schema_owner` owns `public` and `platform`; `platform_migrator` gets `USAGE, CREATE`; `platform_definer` gets `CREATE` on `platform` (a missing grant that would have failed the definer migrations at P5); Prisma Migrate compatibility argued on paper, proven by SPK-08. (4) C11 asserts exactly one `proconfig` entry `search_path=pg_catalog, pg_temp` on every `SECURITY DEFINER` function; C14 the same for the three audit trigger/guard functions plus a superuser `ALTER SCHEMA … RENAME` that must raise; C13 allows, on RDS only, exactly one ADMIN-only `pg_auth_members` row per no-member role for the bootstrap master (PostgreSQL 16+ `createrole_self_grant`), treated as break-glass (SEC-P3-33 item 10). (5) Migration-linter rule 13 flags definer functions without the fixed path. Verification SEC-TC-61 (extended with the two `DROP … CASCADE` statements), SPK-08 extended.
- **Why:** SEC-P3-27 / CWE-426 (CVE-2018-1058 class): with `search_path = pg_catalog, public` a better-matching overload planted in migrator-owned `public` would run as `platform_audit_owner` on every daily partition call, giving the widely held migrator credential the superuser-only RR-04 window plus persistent grants. data-architect additionally found that the schema owner could drop the guard function or the `platform` schema with `CASCADE`, which only privilege (item 6) closes.
- **Alternatives:** Bake schema OIDs into the guard (rejected: restores renumber OIDs and the protection would silently vanish); name-based match (rejected: rename denial of service); dedicated `audit` schema now (deferred to M5 as before: renames every audit relation across the Prisma mapping, ADRs and tests).
- **Reversibility:** High (bootstrap files; no production data).

### D-ARCH-034 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · G2 conditions G2-C1, G2-C2 and the design half of G2-C3 closed; SEC-P3-33 documentation drift fixed; CLAUDE.md §2 line left for the orchestrator
- **Decision:** **G2-C1:** NFR-RES-007 row added to `nfr-traceability.md` (mechanism, ADR-0010 am. 1–6/10, ADR-0014 am. 3, ADR-0016 am. 9/11, SEC-TC-40/41/42/62) and to `nfr-strategy.md`; totals 90 → 91 (75 FULL / 10 PARTIAL / 6 DEFERRED), so `traceability.md` "all 91 NFR IDs" is true. **G2-C2:** `traceability.md` v1.4 Design column filled for all 434 REQ rows (explicit per-REQ mapping, Status → Designed; Task/Test untouched); `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6 gains REQ-AUTH-025/026 (identity) and REQ-CARR-022/023 (carriers) and says 434 REQs; "Redis 7"/`redis:7` → Valkey 8 in ARCHITECTURE.md §4/§8.1/§8.2, threat-model.md §1 and the §4 DFD, platform.md §2.2 image cell, §2 diagram and OI-01 (resolved); risks.md R-ARCH-10 closed. **G2-C3 design:** D-ARCH-030..033 above; SEC-P3-30 catalogue (api/README §13, 22 events, SEC-TC-63). **SEC-P3-33:** item 2 — `npm ci` → `npm ci --ignore-scripts` with inline amended markers in ADR-0023 §1 (`ci.yml`), am. A (fast lane), ADR-0002 §Alternatives and `nfr-strategy.md` OPS-002; item 4 — ADR-0014 am. 3 corrected to `DRAFT` at the deadline under `LOCAL_REJECT` (REQ-BOOK-023, D-056); item 5 — README §5 `activateTotp` definitive; item 7 — ADR-0023 am. G and platform.md §7.3 say C1–C14; item 8 — ADR-0023 §1 `release.yml` and §Consequences say provenance attestations are deferred to M5 (OI-10); item 9 — README §5/§8 replay-before-proof rule; item 11 — ADR-0026 am. 8 cross-tab idle clock via `BroadcastChannel` (matches D-UX-032). Item 3 (REQ-AUTH-018) is product-manager's D-056. **Not edited by principal-architect:** `CLAUDE.md` §2 dev-environment line still reads `npm ci`; the orchestrator applies the replacement text given in the P4 handoff report. SEC-P3-31 (`npx` → `npm exec --no`, base-ref gate scripts) stays a Wave-0 CI task for platform-architect/devops-engineer; ADR-0023 am. F still says `npx prisma generate` and is flagged for that task. Incident note: a stale scratch file briefly duplicated D-ARCH-003 rev 2..028 at the end of this file during the append; the duplicate was removed in the same session before commit (no entry text changed).
- **Why:** G2 PASS_WITH_CONDITIONS (gates/G2-attempt-1.md) requires these before P4 exit; quality-gatekeeper verifies them at the next gate check.
- **Alternatives:** Fill the Design column with area-level defaults (rejected: the gate asks for meaningful rows); let the orchestrator fill it (rejected: principal-architect owns Design and no concurrent writer existed on traceability.md).
- **Reversibility:** High (documentation).

### D-PLAN-001 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer · P4 plan shape: 281 tasks, 10 waves, 76 batches of ≤ 4, lock-based overlap control, DC-first with enablers
- **Decision:** `docs/04-plan/PLAN.md` + `tasks/WAVE-00..09.md` + `task-map.csv`. The waves run Wave 0 (foundation) → 1–2 (M0 DC) → 3 (M0 non-DC Must) → ★ `v0.1.0-m0` → 4–6 (M1 DC) → 7 (M1 non-DC Must + NFR tasks) → 8 (Should, cuttable) → 9 (Could, cuttable). Each batch holds at most 4 tasks with disjoint locks. Every dependency sits in an earlier batch. Non-DC Must stories that a DC story depends on (30) are scheduled as enablers inside the DC waves. A back-fill rule lets idle lanes start the next eligible task without changing priority order. Complexity is S/M only (70/211); the starting model is opus for 101 security-critical or complex tasks and sonnet otherwise, escalating to opus from fix cycle 2.
- **Why:** PRD §7.1 (DC first, ladder), CLAUDE.md §3 (MAX_PARALLEL_DEVELOPERS = 4), R-ARCH-11 (generators first). Locks plus discovery-based registries (PLAN.md §5) keep parallel tasks off the same files.
- **Alternatives:** Waves per epic (rejected: the M0 demo needs slices across 12 epics); one task per story without enablers (rejected: dependencies would block DC stories); a central hand-edited route/permission registry (rejected: guaranteed conflicts).
- **Reversibility:** High. The plan is regenerated from structured data; the IDs of existing tasks stay stable.

### D-PLAN-002 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer · Wave 0 carries all generator frameworks, CI lanes and the eight spikes; SPK-01 = TASK-001, SPK-07 = TASK-015
- **Decision:** Wave 0 (37 tasks) contains:
  - the toolchain/workspace (SPK-01 executed as TASK-001, ubuntu half in TASK-007)
  - lanes A/B/C with the evidence schema, `check-evidence` and the required-suite manifest move (TASK-007/011/015; SPK-07 = TASK-015)
  - Prisma, RLS and the audit store (SPK-03 = TASK-008, SPK-08 = TASK-012, SPK-04 = TASK-018)
  - `defineRoute` and the route-metadata test
  - the RBAC-matrix, RLS cross-tenant, audit/immutability and abuse generators
  - the test harness and test-ID tracing (T-QA-02/03/04)
  - Compose and images, and the thin slice
  - SPK-02/05/06 (TASK-009/019/022)

  Each spike ends with GO or FALLBACK in `docs/04-plan/spikes/SPK-0x.md`. A FALLBACK triggers an ADR amendment request before its consumers start.
- **Why:** R-ARCH-11; the G2 conditions and SEC-COND-04 require enforcement from the first `npm ci`; qa-lead T-QA-04 wants generators active by the end of Wave 0.
- **Alternatives:** Separate throwaway spikes for SPK-01/07 (rejected: they would duplicate the scaffold and workflow work); generators in a later wave (rejected: every earlier route would need retrofitting).
- **Reversibility:** Medium (Wave 0 sequencing drives everything after it).

### D-PLAN-003 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer · M1 demo-critical notification core pulled into M0 (US-274, 276, 278, 279, 280, 282, 284)

### D-ARCH-035 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SPK-01 A-1: npm ≥ 11.10 with `engines.npm`, final `.npmrc` (`min-release-age=7`, `audit=false`), `npm run typecheck` = `tsc -b` (ADR-0002 am. items 1–2; ADR-0023 am. I items 1 and 5; platform.md §2.1, Appendix A; nfr-strategy/nfr-traceability MAINT-002)
- **Decision:** (1) The package manager is the npm bundled with Node 24.22+ (11.19.1 on the host and in `node:24` images); root `package.json` declares `"engines": { "node": ">=24 <25", "npm": ">=11.10" }` and "npm 10" wording is superseded everywhere. (2) The committed `.npmrc` is, in full, `ignore-scripts=true`, `engine-strict=true`, `save-exact=true`, `fund=false`, `audit=false`, `update-notifier=false`, `min-release-age=7`; the "once SPK-01 confirms" clause of ADR-0023 am. F item 1 is confirmed (0 of 1,597 lockfile entries were younger than 7 days at generation). (3) `npm run typecheck` is `tsc -b` (emitting into git-ignored `dist/` and `*.tsbuildinfo`), never `tsc -b --noEmit`; `noEmit` stays only on unreferenced leaf projects (Vite apps, `prisma`, `tests/*`, `tools`); empty workspaces use `"files": []` + `include`. (4) CLAUDE.md §2 is not edited by principal-architect; the replacement text for the build line and the lint line is in the SPK-01 handoff for the orchestrator.
- **Why:** The 11.10 floor is the first npm with `min-release-age`; npm 10 (Node 20) is EOL tooling and now fails fast on `EBADENGINE`, which is the intended guard. TypeScript 6.0.3 rejects `--noEmit` in build mode when a referenced project emits (`TS6310`), and emitting costs nothing because `build` reuses the same incremental outputs. Verified in TASK-001's clean-room run (SPK-01 §1).
- **Alternatives:** `tsc --noEmit -p` per project in a loop (16 invocations, no incremental sharing; rejected); keeping `.npmrc` without `min-release-age` and relying on the lane A age script alone (rejected: defence in depth is free).
- **Consequences:** `dist/` contains compiled tests (one tsconfig per workspace) → runtime images strip `**/*.test.*` (D-ARCH-038); ad-hoc `tsc <file>` needs `--ignoreConfig` under TS 6.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-036 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SPK-01 A-2: Jest runs under `node --experimental-vm-modules`; workspace packages resolve through the `@platform/source` export condition in dev/test and through `dist/` in production (ADR-0002 am. items 3–4; ADR-0024 am. items 1–3; ADR-0026 am. 12)
- **Decision:** (1) `test:unit`, `test:integration`, `test:api`, `test:coverage` = `node --experimental-vm-modules --disable-warning=ExperimentalWarning node_modules/jest/bin/jest.js --selectProjects <project>`; workflows and `quality:*` call these scripts, never `jest` directly; the flag is test-only and a Dockerfile/compose test rejects it in images. (2) Every `packages/*/package.json` exports `{ "@platform/source": "./src/index.ts", "types": "./dist/index.d.ts", "default": "./dist/index.js" }` in that order; `tsconfig.base.json` `customConditions`, Jest `customExportConditions`, Vite `resolve.conditions` and the dev runners (`node --conditions=@platform/source`) set the condition. (3) Production never sets it: image `ENTRYPOINT`s and compose commands run `node --enable-source-maps dist/main.js`, so Node resolves `default` → `dist/index.js` built by `tsc -b` project references; runtime images copy `packages/*/dist` + `package.json` only (no `src/`); guards = exports-shape repository test, no-`--conditions` Dockerfile/compose/task-definition test, and the runtime preflight importing every `@platform/*` package. (4) Jest uses explicit `roots` + root-relative `testMatch`; barrels are excluded from coverage; per-path 95/95 thresholds arrive with TASK-015.
- **Why:** `jose` 6, `uuid` 14, `canonicalize` 5, `otplib` 13, `cookie` 2 and `nock` 15 are ESM-only; Jest 30.5's `require(esm)` inside its sandbox needs `vm.SourceTextModule`, which Node 24 gates behind the flag. The export condition keeps tests and dev on sources (no pre-build) while production loads no TypeScript. Brace globs matched 0 files on Windows.
- **Alternatives:** Full native-ESM Jest (rejected: breaks `jest.mock` hoisting, not needed); `moduleNameMapper` to `src/` in Jest + `paths` aliases in tsconfig (rejected: ADR-0002 forbids path aliases so boundaries stay visible to ESLint, and two mapping mechanisms drift); Vitest (fixed stack forbids).
- **Reversibility:** High (flag and condition are configuration).

### D-ARCH-037 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SPK-01 A-3: dependency substitutions verified against the registry — `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y-x` 0.2, `@prometheus-io/client` 0.16 (exact), Stylelint 17.16 + `stylelint-use-logical` 2.1.3, Lighthouse CI as a repository-built tool container, `size-limit` + `@size-limit/file` only, Stryker 10 in an isolated install, `@hookform/resolvers` 5.9.2; `isbot` not added (ADR-0025 SPK-01 table; ADR-0002 am. items 5–7; ADR-0017 am. 1–2; ADR-0024 am. items 4–7; ADR-0026 am. 11)
- **Decision:** As titled. Evidence per candidate (licence, last release, maintainer, peers/engines, `npm audit` on a lockfile-only resolution, rejected alternative) is the table in ADR-0025's SPK-01 amendment, checked 2026-11-12 with `npm view`/`npm audit` under the CLAUDE.md §2 PATH prefix. Highlights: `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` is > 18 months stale and stops at ESLint 9; `prom-client` is deprecated on the registry in favour of the Prometheus-org `@prometheus-io/client` (same API, pinned exact because pre-1.0, confined to the `platform/metrics` module); no logical-CSS Stylelint plugin supports Stylelint 18 yet, so 17.16 is used without any peer override and 18 follows the plugin; `@lhci/cli` carries 9 High advisories in its puppeteer chain and runs only as a tool container (`tools/isolated/lhci/Dockerfile`; advisories accepted for the tool image, reported with the tool register, 90-day review, security-analyst confirms); `@size-limit/preset-app` carries 14 High and is replaced by byte-only `@size-limit/file`; Stryker 10 hoists Babel 8 and lives in `tools/isolated/stryker` with its own lockfile (Jest resolved from the root tree); `@hookform/resolvers` 7.x conflicts with Prisma's `effect` 3 peer. The observability task imports explicit OpenTelemetry instrumentations and the AWS resource detector instead of `auto-instrumentations-node` (removes the GCP chain behind the production closure's only advisories, F-09).
- **Why:** CLAUDE.md §5 dependency rules (allow-list, no Critical/High, release within 18 months) and §9 (new dependencies justified); keep the root lockfile audit-clean and `npm ls` valid so lane A's gates are meaningful from the first PR.
- **Alternatives:** Recorded per row in the ADR-0025 table (upstream a11y plugin; keep `prom-client`; OTel Metrics SDK + Prometheus exporter; Stylelint 18 with a forced peer; `stylelint-plugin-logical-css`; `patrickhulce/lhci-client` image; `--legacy-peer-deps`; `@hookform/resolvers` 7 with an `effect` override; hand-written Zod→RHF bridge; declaring `isbot`).
- **Risks:** R-ARCH-22 (small-org fork and pre-1.0 metrics client).
- **Reversibility:** High for all; medium for `@prometheus-io/client` only if the registry API diverges before 1.0 (mitigated by the module boundary).

### D-ARCH-038 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SPK-01 A-4: runtime images install with `npm ci --ignore-scripts --omit=dev --omit=optional`; the `migrate` image keeps the Prisma CLI but prunes Studio (`elkjs` EPL-2.0, `@visx/*` alpha); runtime preflight and compiled-test stripping (ADR-0022 am. 11–13; ADR-0023 am. I item 2; ADR-0002 am. item 8; platform.md §3.2; nfr-strategy OPS-002)
- **Decision:** (1) `api`/`worker` `prod-deps`: `npm ci --ignore-scripts --omit=dev --omit=optional --workspace=apps/<app>`. (2) `migrate` stage: `--ignore-scripts --omit=optional` (Prisma CLI kept), followed by the allow-listed `tools/docker/prune-migrate-image.mjs` removing `@prisma/studio-core` and `@prisma/dev` with their exclusive subtrees; image test proves `prisma migrate status`/`deploy` and the seed CLI still work; fallback = in-image throwing stub for `@prisma/studio-core`. (3) Image invariants asserted by image tests + Trivy licence scan: `node_modules/prisma` absent from `api`/`worker`; `@prisma/studio-core`, `elkjs` and any `*-alpha.*` absent from every image; no `typescript`, no workspace `src/*.ts`. (4) `RUN node dist/preflight.js` in runtime stages (imports every production module and `@platform/*` package); `tools/docker/prune-dist.mjs` strips `**/*.test.*` and `**/__fixtures__/**` from copied `dist/`. (5) `check-install-flags`: `--omit=dev` in a Dockerfile requires `--omit=optional`; the `migrate` stage must call the prune script.
- **Why:** SPK-01 F-14: npm drops `devOptional` packages only when both `dev` and `optional` are omitted, so `--omit=dev` alone shipped the Prisma CLI, Studio (`elkjs` `EPL-2.0 OR GPL-3.0-or-later`, off the allow-list), `@visx/*` 4.0.x-alpha and `typescript` into runtime images. `prisma` 7.10.1 declares `@prisma/studio-core` as a regular dependency (verified `npm view` 2026-11-12), so the migrate image needs the explicit prune. bcrypt 6 is unaffected (prebuilds inside the package). F-13: `tsc -b` emits compiled tests.
- **Alternatives:** npm `overrides` replacing `@prisma/studio-core` with an empty package (rejected: affects the dev tree, depends on an external "empty" package or a `file:` override that `check-lockfile` forbids); a separate `tsconfig.build.json` per workspace to keep tests out of `dist/` (rejected for now: doubles 16 tsconfigs; stripping at image assembly is deterministic and tested); replacing Prisma Migrate with an in-house runner (rejected: loses `_prisma_migrations` semantics).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-003 rev 3 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · EPL-2.0 admitted in the dev/build closure for unmodified transitive dependencies of sanctioned tooling, never in a shipped image (extends D-ARCH-003 rev 2 items 2 and 4; security-analyst / dependency-auditor to confirm in Wave 0 like rev 2)
- **Decision:** The dev and build closure (rev 2 item 2) additionally admits **EPL-2.0** for *transitive, unmodified* dependencies of sanctioned tooling — today exactly `elkjs` (`EPL-2.0 OR GPL-3.0-or-later`, reached via `prisma` → `@prisma/studio-core`; we elect the EPL-2.0 branch). Conditions: never a direct dependency; never in any shipped image or bundle (enforced by D-ARCH-038's `--omit=dev --omit=optional`, the migrate prune and the Trivy licence scan of every image, rev 2 item 5); listed by name in the licence report with its path. The production closure (rev 2 item 1) is unchanged (EPL-2.0 remains inadmissible there). Rev 2 item 4's "never appear in the npm closure, prod or dev" is narrowed accordingly for EPL-2.0 only; GPL/AGPL/LGPL/SSPL/BUSL/RSAL/Commons Clause remain excluded from both closures.
- **Why:** The Prisma CLI is unavoidable in the dev closure (`migrate dev`, `generate`), and `prisma` 7.10.1 hard-depends on Studio, which pulls `elkjs`; without this rule TASK-007's licence check would fail every lane A run. EPL-2.0 is a weak, file-scoped copyleft comparable to MPL-2.0 (already on LICENSE_ALLOWLIST): obligations attach only to modifying or distributing the EPL'd files, neither of which happens for a dev-time tool dependency that never enters an image.
- **Alternatives:** A per-package waiver file (rejected: same effect with churn each time Prisma's Studio tree changes); removing Studio from the dev tree by `overrides` (rejected, see D-ARCH-038); failing the check (rejected: blocks the toolchain).
- **Reversibility:** High (policy text + licence-check rule).

### D-ARCH-039 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · SPK-01 F-SEC-1 and lane hygiene: custom `app/entry.server.tsx` mandatory in every React Router app; lane A/C and `quality:fastlane` fail when a build mutates the tree; `npx` → `npm exec --no --` everywhere; isolated tool lockfiles covered by the lane A checks (ADR-0026 am. 10; ADR-0023 am. I items 3–4, 6–8; platform.md §3.2, §7.1, §7.2)
- **Decision:** (1) `apps/web` and `apps/admin` commit a minimal `app/entry.server.tsx` without `isbot` (TASK-004); the repository-structure test asserts the file for every workspace with a `react-router.config.ts`; `isbot` is not declared. (2) After `npm run build`, lane A, lane C and `quality:fastlane` run `git status --porcelain` and fail on any output; `quality:heavy` records `build_clean` (false → `INVALID`); a lane A negative fixture (a build script touching `package.json`) proves the check. (3) `npm exec --no -- <bin>` replaces `npx` in `deps:rebuild`, ADR-0023 am. F and platform.md §3.2 (`prisma generate`, `playwright install`); `check-no-npx` rejects `npx` in scripts, workflows, Dockerfiles and compose files (SEC-P3-31, SEC-TC-64, D-ORCH-005 ruling 8). (4) `tools/isolated/{stryker,lhci}` carry their own lockfiles and a byte-identical `.npmrc`; `check-lockfile`, licence/age and `check-install-flags` run over every lockfile; `npm audit --audit-level=high` gates root + stryker; the LHCI lockfile is a container build input (Trivy-reported). (5) `check-lockfile`/licence-check adjustments for `inBundle` entries, npm aliases, legacy `licenses` arrays, the `png-js` LICENSE override and case-insensitive SPDX operators (F-08, F-15). (6) TASK-014 avoids fixed host ports ≥ 49152 (Hyper-V/WinNAT exclusions on the developer host, SPK-01 §4).
- **Why:** `@react-router/dev` 8.5 rewrites `package.json` and runs `npm install isbot` via `execSync` when an app lacks a custom server entry — in the spike that install ran without the committed `.npmrc` (no `ignore-scripts`, no cooldown), bypassing SEC-P3-04; `check-install-flags` cannot see an install issued from inside a dependency, so the tree check is the independent backstop. With `ssr:false` + prerender, bot detection has no purpose, so a dependency forced by an upstream heuristic is not justified (CLAUDE.md §9).
- **Alternatives:** Declaring `isbot` 5 explicitly (Unlicense, maintained, 0 advisories — rejected as unjustified; recorded in ADR-0025); a Semgrep rule for `execSync('npm install')` in `node_modules` (rejected: scanning dependencies' code is noisy and the tree check is deterministic).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK001-01 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-001 implementation decisions (SPK-01 §2 + fix cycle 1)
- `typecheck` = `tsc -b` (TS6310); Jest under `node --experimental-vm-modules`; `@platform/source` export condition for workspace sources; explicit Jest `roots` (Windows glob); a global coverage threshold only until TASK-015 adds per-path 95 %; `--passWithNoTests` on integration/api until the first suites; `@hookform/resolvers` 5.9.2 (effect 3/4 conflict); `deps:rebuild` via `npm exec --no` (all ratified by D-ARCH-035..037).
- Fix cycle 1: `jest-util` 30.5.1 exact dev dep (same matcher as `@jest/transform`; MIT; already in the tree); the `rebuild.mjs` entry guard is `import.meta.main` and fails closed on Node < 24.2; `@platform:registry=https://registry.invalid/` pin (PR1-SEC-01); `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node` removed (PR1-SEC-07; explicit instrumentations per ADR-0017 am. 2 land in TASK-214).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-040 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · PR1-SEC-01 (MEDIUM): the `@platform` npm scope is third-party-owned; `.npmrc` pins it to `https://registry.invalid/`; lockfile `@platform/*` entries must be workspace links; image installs run from the repository root only (ADR-0023 am. J item 1; ADR-0002 §1 note + PR1-SEC am. item 1; ADR-0022 am. 14 a; platform.md §2.1, §3.2, §7.1)
- **Decision:** (1) The committed `.npmrc` gains `@platform:registry=https://registry.invalid/` (RFC 6761 reserved TLD: any registry resolution of a workspace name fails closed with `ENOTFOUND`). The file is now, in full: `ignore-scripts=true`, `engine-strict=true`, `save-exact=true`, `fund=false`, `audit=false`, `update-notifier=false`, `min-release-age=7`, `@platform:registry=https://registry.invalid/` (supersedes the list in D-ARCH-035 (2)). (2) `check-install-flags` asserts the line in the root `.npmrc` and every `tools/isolated/*/.npmrc` copy; `check-lockfile` asserts that every `@platform/*` lockfile entry is a workspace folder entry or a `link: true` entry without `resolved`/`integrity`; a Dockerfile install must sit in a stage that has copied `package-lock.json` and `.npmrc` (root-only installs, `--workspace=apps/<app>`). (3) Workspace names stay `@platform/*`; the pin, not a rename, is the control. (4) SEC-TC-45 gains `.npmrc pins the @platform scope to an unresolvable registry` and `lockfile workspace entries are links only`, plus the out-of-graph negative fixture (`@platform/shared@0.0.0` → `ENOTFOUND registry.invalid`). Lands in PR #1 (`.npmrc` line + assertion); lane A rules in TASK-007, before TASK-014.
- **Why:** `@platform` on npmjs.com is an existing third-party organisation (131 packages on 2026-11-12); none of our 16 names is published, so a publish of `@platform/shared@0.0.0` by the scope owner or an account hijacker would be fetched by any install that resolves workspace names from the registry (a per-app Docker stage, an out-of-graph install such as F-SEC-1, a broken lockfile link) and executed in the API or worker with database and payment credentials. `ignore-scripts` is no defence: the payload is the module. Verified by security-analyst in a scratch copy (`npm ci` 1,505 packages, `npm ls --all` and `npm audit` unchanged; out-of-graph install → `ENOTFOUND registry.invalid` instead of an `E404` from npmjs.com).
- **Alternatives:** Renaming to another scope (rejected: we own no scope either way, same exposure); registering an npm organisation (rejected: an external action outside the guardrails and unnecessary, nothing is ever published); relying on `"private": true` alone (rejected: it prevents publishing, not resolution).
- **Reversibility:** High (one `.npmrc` line and two check rules).

### D-ARCH-041 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · PR1-SEC-02 (MEDIUM): Prisma schema engine pinned by `tools/deps/prisma-engines.json`; `deps:rebuild` step `prisma-engines` is the only fetcher (fixed origin, manifest SHA-256, offline/cached mode); Prisma's downloader and per-user cache are never used; `tools/deps/prisma-cli.mjs` wraps every CLI call; images verify at build and prove `--network none` (ADR-0023 am. F item 1 marker, am. I item 3 correction, am. J item 2; ADR-0002 PR1-SEC am. item 2; ADR-0022 am. 14 b; platform.md §3.2; risks.md R-ARCH-23)
- **Decision:** (1) Fact correction: SPK-01's "`prisma generate` works with no install script" is incomplete — the CLI's `ensureNeededBinariesExist()` downloads the native `schema-engine` (≈ 20 MB) from `binaries.prisma.sh` before `version`/`init`/`migrate`/`db`/`generate`/`validate`/`format`/`telemetry` whenever it is absent from `node_modules/@prisma/engines/` and the per-user cache; SPK-01 passed on a cache entry written 2026-10-19 by another project. Its only integrity check is a same-origin SHA-256, skippable with `PRISMA_ENGINES_CHECKSUM_IGNORE_MISSING`. (2) Manifest `tools/deps/prisma-engines.json` = `enginesVersion` (`d1b3f377ca835d73fd50954a5e22128b9ebdd6b8`; must equal the installed `@prisma/engines-version`, today `8.1.0-1.d1b3f377…` via `prisma` 7.10.1 → `@prisma/engines` 7.10.1), the fixed `source` `https://binaries.prisma.sh/all_commits/`, and the SHA-256 of the gunzipped `schema-engine` per permitted binary target (`debian-openssl-3.0.x` for the `node:24-*-slim` stages and `ubuntu-latest`; `windows` for the developer host; an unknown target fails closed). Hashes are recorded by `node tools/deps/prisma-engines.mjs --record` from two independent environments that must agree byte for byte; a manifest change needs security-analyst review (CODEOWNERS) and changes version and hashes together. (3) `deps:rebuild` step `prisma-engines`, before `prisma generate`: refuses to start if `PRISMA_ENGINES_MIRROR`, `PRISMA_ENGINES_CHECKSUM_IGNORE_MISSING`, `PRISMA_SCHEMA_ENGINE_BINARY`, `PRISMA_QUERY_ENGINE_LIBRARY`, `PRISMA_QUERY_ENGINE_BINARY`, `PRISMA_FMT_BINARY`, `BINARY_DOWNLOAD_VERSION` or `PRISMA_CLI_BINARY_TARGETS` is inherited (TASK-003 completes the list from `@prisma/fetch-engine` at the locked version); checks version equality; reuses an existing `node_modules/@prisma/engines/schema-engine-<target>[.exe]` only if its hash matches (offline/cached mode, no network); otherwise fetches from the origin constant (HTTPS, 60 s timeout, 3 retries with backoff and jitter, same-origin redirects only), verifies the `.gz` against the upstream `.gz.sha256` (transport) and the gunzipped binary against the manifest (trust anchor), writes atomically with mode `0755`; any mismatch deletes partial files and exits 1 leaving no `schema-engine*`. The per-user Prisma cache is never read, written or trusted; the `rebuild.mjs` header names this single permitted origin. (4) Children run with `PRISMA_SCHEMA_ENGINE_BINARY=<verified absolute path>` and `CHECKPOINT_DISABLE=1`, which makes Prisma skip both the download and the cache copy (TASK-003 confirms the short-circuit in `@prisma/fetch-engine`; if it does not hold, the step also re-verifies after every child). Every other CLI call (`db:migrate`, `db:migrate:deploy`, `db:reset`, the migration linter's `prisma validate`, harnesses, the `migrate` image entrypoint) goes through `node tools/deps/prisma-cli.mjs <args>` (same checks, then `npm exec --no -- prisma <args>`); `check-no-raw-prisma` rejects a bare `prisma` in scripts, workflows, Dockerfiles and compose files; `db:seed*` is unaffected. (5) CI caches of the engine are keyed by `enginesVersion` + OS and re-verified on restore; `check-workflow-policy` rejects the variables of (3) and `npm_config_ignore_scripts=false`. (6) `migrate` image: fetch + verify in the build stage, `ENV PRISMA_SCHEMA_ENGINE_BINARY=/app/node_modules/@prisma/engines/schema-engine-debian-openssl-3.0.x`, `--verify-only` in the entrypoint, no runtime egress; `api`/`worker` hold no `@prisma/engines` directory and no `schema-engine*` file. (7) Changing the origin requires an ADR amendment, never an environment variable or configuration key. (8) SEC-TC-64 (i) altered hash → exit 1 and no engine left, (ii) mirror variable set → refused before any network, (iii) empty directory + unreachable origin → fail closed, (iv) poisoned per-user cache ignored, (v) stale manifest `enginesVersion` → exit 1, (vi) image tests (`docker run --network none <migrate> prisma --version` exits 0; no engine in `api`/`worker`); TASK-003's acceptance runs with an empty engine directory and no `PRISMA_*` variables. Owners: TASK-003 (manifest, step, wrapper, tests i–v), TASK-007 (workflow policy, `check-no-raw-prisma`, cache key, workflow `env`), TASK-014 (image rules, test vi), security-analyst (manifest review, design confirmation).
- **Why:** The engine runs DDL as the migrator role; a compromised CDN or an attacker-set variable in CI or BuildKit would yield a malicious engine with full schema control (threat-model "C": RLS and audit integrity). A same-origin SHA-256 is not integrity; an in-repo hash reviewed like a lockfile entry is. The env-override path is the one Prisma short-circuit that disables both the download and the cache copy, which could otherwise overwrite a verified file from a poisoned cache; verify-after alone leaves that hole.
- **Alternatives:** Trust Prisma's same-origin checksum (rejected: no independent anchor); vendor the binary in git or as a release asset (rejected: 20 MB binaries in the repository, invisible to the lockfile tooling — the recorded fallback if the origin proves unreliable, keeping the same manifest hashes); our own mirror via `PRISMA_ENGINES_MIRROR` (rejected until M5: budget 0, and a mirror is a second trusted origin without more integrity); allow `@prisma/engines`' `postinstall` (rejected: same downloader, same hole, re-enables scripts); verify-after only (rejected, see Why).
- **Consequences:** `deps:rebuild` makes exactly one kind of network call, to one fixed origin, and declares it; a Prisma upgrade is a reviewed manifest change; TASK-003 grows by the step, the wrapper and five negative tests; TASK-007 and TASK-014 gain the rules above.
- **Reversibility:** High for the tooling; the invariant "no unverified engine bytes execute" is intended to be permanent.

### D-ARCH-003 rev 4 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · `elkjs` SPDX corrected to `EPL-2.0` exactly; the EPL-2.0 dev-closure admission is implemented as named exceptions; deprecated-transitive rule clarified (gating in production, report-only in dev-only); `tools/ci/maintenance-exceptions.json` register with expiry (corrects the parenthetical of rev 3; clarifies rev 2 item 6; security-analyst to confirm like rev 2 and rev 3; ADR-0023 am. J items 3–4; ADR-0025 PR1-SEC amendment; ADR-0002 PR1-SEC am. item 4; ADR-0022 am. 11 marker)
- **Decision:** (1) Rev 3's "(`EPL-2.0 OR GPL-3.0-or-later` …; we elect the EPL-2.0 branch)" is wrong for the locked version: `elkjs@0.11.1` declares `EPL-2.0` (`npm view elkjs@0.11.1 license`, 2026-11-12; its `LICENSE.md` Exhibit A is unfilled, so no Secondary Licence applies). The `OR` expression belongs to `elkjs@0.12.x`, which is not in the lockfile. The admission stands unchanged in substance (dev/build closure; transitive and unmodified; never in an image). (2) The admission is enforced only through **named entries** in `tools/ci/licence-exceptions.json` (`name`, `version`, `licence`, `via` parent chain, `closure: dev`, decision ID) — today exactly `elkjs@0.11.1` via `prisma` → `@prisma/studio-core`; any other EPL-2.0 package fails (negative fixture), and the entry itself fails if `elkjs` is reached outside the chain, enters the production closure or any image. (3) Deprecated transitives (rev 2 item 6 clarified): **gating in the production closure** (`npm ci --omit=dev --omit=optional`) unless listed in the register below; **report-only in the dev-only closure** unless they carry a High/Critical advisory (already gated by `npm audit --audit-level=high`). Direct dependencies remain gating in both closures. (4) `tools/ci/maintenance-exceptions.json` (sibling of `dependency-overrides.json`): entries `{ name, version, kind: "direct-stale" | "deprecated-transitive", via, reason, decision, review_by, expires, exit_criteria }`; the freshness gate passes an unexpired entry, **fails an expired one**, and fails regardless on a High/Critical advisory; the freshness report lists every entry with days to expiry; a new entry needs a DECISIONS.md ID from principal-architect and security-analyst review (CODEOWNERS). First entries: `bcrypt@6.0.0` (D-ARCH-042) and `node-abort-controller@3.1.1` via `bullmq` (D-ARCH-043). TASK-007's freshness gate goes live only with this register.
- **Why:** security-analyst read the locked package's metadata; I read the registry's `latest`. Rule for the future: licence facts are checked against the **locked** version (`npm view <pkg>@<locked> license`), never `latest`. A named exception keeps the licence check falsifiable (a different EPL-2.0 package must fail), which a licence-wide allowance cannot. Dev-only tooling trees (Jest, jsdom, testcontainers) carry deprecated-by-obsolescence versions (`glob` 10.5.0, `whatwg-encoding` 3.1.1) that process trusted repository inputs only; gating them would fail lane A on upstream tooling we cannot change, while the production closure is where staleness is a risk we ship. Expiry on exceptions forces a re-decision instead of silent drift.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the `OR` text (rejected: false for the locked version); make dev-only deprecated transitives gating too (rejected: lane A would fail on Jest's own tree from day one with no remedy under our control); open-ended exceptions (rejected: the 18-month rule exists to force a decision).
- **Reversibility:** High (policy text, two JSON registers, check rules).

### D-ARCH-042 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · PR1-SEC-06 (LOW): `bcrypt` 6.0.0 kept under a time-boxed maintenance exception (expires 2027-05-11; monthly review from 2026-12-12; four exit criteria); `bcryptjs` 3.x in a `worker_threads` pool behind a `PasswordHasher` port is the recorded exit (ADR-0025 PR1-SEC amendment; ADR-0006 am. 16; ADR-0002 PR1-SEC am. item 3; risks.md R-ARCH-24)
- **Decision:** (1) `bcrypt` 6.0.0 (published 2025-05-11T18:06Z, latest; 18 months old on 2026-11-11; no advisory; N-API prebuilds load on Node 24 under `--ignore-scripts`) stays, as the brief §3 fixed stack names it. (2) Entry `bcrypt@6.0.0` (`direct-stale`) in `tools/ci/maintenance-exceptions.json`, `expires: 2027-05-11` (24 months after the release — the outer bound, not rolling), `review_by` monthly from 2026-12-12 through the freshness report. (3) Exit criteria, any one → a switch task in the following wave: (a) an advisory of severity ≥ Medium against `bcrypt` or its prebuild loader; (b) upstream archived or declared unmaintained; (c) an ADR-0001 Node major on which the 6.0.0 prebuild fails the SEC-TC-45 "bcrypt works" case; (d) 2027-05-11 reached with no release. A new `bcrypt` release removes the entry and the ordinary rule resumes. (4) Exit = `bcryptjs` 3.x (3.0.3, BSD-3-Clause, pure JS, zero dependencies, released 2026-04-24): both libraries verify `$2a$`/`$2b$`, so stored hashes and the data-model `CHECK (password_hash ~ '^\$2[aby]\$')` are untouched; the pure-JS cost-12 hash (≈ 250–400 ms) runs in a `node:worker_threads` pool (`AUTH_HASH_WORKERS`, default 2) so it never blocks the event loop; performance-engineer re-measures the login p95 ≤ 300 ms budget (backlog spike before 2027-05-11). (5) Now, to make the exit a one-module change: a `PasswordHasher` port (`hash`, `verify`, `needsRehash`) holding the SHA-512 pre-hash, the cost floor and the dummy-hash constant-time compare (ADR-0006 am. 16), and an ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule confining `bcrypt`/`bcryptjs` to its implementation module (ADR-0002 PR1-SEC am. item 3). TASK-049 places the module under the ADR-0003 boundaries and applies this decision before first use; TASK-007's freshness gate reads the register.
- **Why:** Fixed stack (CLAUDE.md §2) versus the 18-month rule (§5): the rule exists to keep abandoned code out of what we ship with privileges; bcrypt is a finished primitive whose N-API prebuilds are ABI-stable across Node majors, no advisory exists, and the residual risk is contained by the port plus a dated exit. Switching now would contradict the brief for no security gain and import real work (worker pool, latency re-baseline). security-analyst recommended the exception.
- **Alternatives:** `bcryptjs` now (rejected, above); `@node-rs/bcrypt` (rejected: napi-rs ships platform binaries as `optionalDependencies`, which the `--omit=optional` runtime install of D-ARCH-038 drops; not sanctioned); `argon2` (not sanctioned by the brief).
- **Reversibility:** High (the port makes the swap local).

### D-ARCH-043 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · PR1-SEC-07 (LOW): `node-abort-controller` 3.1.1 accepted as BullMQ's transitive with a watch item (dead code on Node 24); `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node` removed from `apps/api/package.json` before TASK-007's gate (ADR-0013 am. 6; ADR-0025 PR1-SEC amendment; ADR-0023 am. J item 4)
- **Decision:** (1) `node-abort-controller@3.1.1` (MIT; zero dependencies; no install script; no advisory; deprecated 2026-11-10 as "no longer needed on supported Node.js versions") stays as the transitive of `bullmq` 6.7.x (6.7.0 locked, 6.7.5 current; both pin it exactly). Register entry `deprecated-transitive`, `via: ["bullmq"]`, `review_by` 2027-05-12 and at every BullMQ minor bump; exit when BullMQ drops it (entry removed); an advisory against it triggers security-analyst's reachability re-evaluation and a principal-architect ruling within the wave. (2) `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node` leaves `apps/api/package.json` in PR #1 or TASK-007's first lockfile change (nothing imports it; ADR-0017 am. 2 adds the explicit instrumentation packages and the AWS detector in TASK-214), removing `glob` 10.5.0, `minimatch` 9.0.9 and `node-domexception` 1.0.0 from the production closure; expectation afterwards: `npm audit --omit=dev` = 0 and `npm ls gcp-metadata` empty; no exception entries for that chain.
- **Why:** BullMQ 6.7.0's `dist/cjs/classes/abort-controller.js` (read from the `npm pack` tarball, 2026-11-12) `require`s the polyfill and then prefers `globalThis.AbortController` when present — on Node 24 the module loads and is never instantiated. The deprecation signals obsolescence, not a defect; nothing under our control removes it without a `file:`/foreign `override` (which `check-lockfile` forbids) or an upstream issue (which CLAUDE.md §5 forbids).
- **Alternatives:** `overrides` to an empty package (rejected: forbidden source types, for a harmless module); pin BullMQ to a release without it (none exists); fail lane A on it (rejected: blocks the sanctioned queue library for a non-risk).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-044 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Review additions adopted for TASK-004/007/014: workflow-level `NPM_CONFIG_IGNORE_SCRIPTS`/`CHECKPOINT_DISABLE`/`SCARF_ANALYTICS`, empty `git status --porcelain` after build, `isbot`-absence assertion, offline web/admin image builds with manifest checksums, image `ENV` telemetry off, no `npm rebuild` in Dockerfiles, `npm_config_globalconfig` isolation in SEC-TC-45 (PR1-SEC-03 (a)–(d), PR1-SEC-08, PR1-SEC-09; ADR-0023 am. J items 5–6; ADR-0022 am. 14 c–f)
- **Decision:** As titled; the detail is ADR-0023 am. J items 5–6 and ADR-0022 am. 14. `check-workflow-policy` requires the three workflow-level variables and rejects the engine-redirecting variables of D-ARCH-041 and `npm_config_ignore_scripts=false`. ADR-0022 am. 14 (f) records the PR1-SEC-04 (b) caveat: `prisma/build/cli.js` `require`s `@prisma/studio-core/data/*` at module level, so the migrate-image prune of D-ARCH-038 is expected to use its stub fallback; the image test must prove `migrate deploy`/`migrate status` either way.
- **Why:** security-analyst confirmed the in-flight SPK-01 amendments as adequate with these additions; folding them into the ADRs keeps TASK-004/007/014 reading one source. The telemetry variables implement CLAUDE.md §5 (no external communication beyond CI/CD and repository integrations). Workflow-level `NPM_CONFIG_IGNORE_SCRIPTS` is the defence in depth for installs issued from inside dependencies (F-SEC-1), which `check-install-flags` cannot see.
- **Alternatives:** Leave the additions in the review document only (rejected: tasks are planned from the ADRs and `docs/04-plan`, not from review findings).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-045 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 ruling 1 (TASK-003, PR #5): SEC-P3-34 item 5 adopted — `platform_migrator` does not own the database; extensions move to the bootstrap lane; SEC-P3-34 items 1–3 implemented now (data-model §4.4/§4.6 C13/§7.3/§8.6, ADR-0005 am. 9, platform.md §4.3; `docs/03-architecture/rulings/batch-0.2-rulings.md`)
- **Decision:** The database stays owned by the bootstrap role (Compose superuser locally, RDS master at M5). `platform_migrator` holds `CONNECT` on the database and `USAGE, CREATE` on `public`/`platform`, nothing else at database level; `ALTER DATABASE platform OWNER TO platform_migrator` is withdrawn from data-model §4.4. The trusted extensions (`pg_trgm`, `btree_gin`, `btree_gist`) are installed by the bootstrap role in `prisma/bootstrap/01-schemas.sql`; a new extension is a new bootstrap file; the shadow-database init rendering keeps the `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS` lines (the migrator owns the shadow database). SEC-P3-34 items 1 (`ALTER ROLE CURRENT_USER IN DATABASE … SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp` at the end of `00-roles.sql`), 2 (harness/runner/reset connect with `options=-c search_path=pg_catalog,pg_temp`) and 3 (`pg_db_role_setting` assertion: no database-wide row, the bootstrap role's row present) are implemented in TASK-003 and re-asserted by C13, together with `pg_database.datdba ≠ platform_migrator` and `has_database_privilege(migrator, 'CREATE') = false`. New migration-linter rule 14 rejects `CREATE SCHEMA`, `CREATE EXTENSION`, `ALTER DATABASE`, `ALTER SCHEMA … OWNER TO` and role DDL in `prisma/migrations/**`. `npm run db:reset` recreates the database as the bootstrap credential (requires `--yes` and a loopback host). PR #5 needs no code change for this item.
- **Why:** A database owner can set a database-wide `search_path` default that no event trigger sees, create schemas and install extensions — none of which Prisma Migrate needs. TASK-003 verified on Prisma 7.10.1 / PostgreSQL 18 that `migrate deploy`, `migrate status` and `db execute` succeed without database ownership or `CREATE` on the database and emit no `CREATE SCHEMA` (`prisma/README.md`, SPK-08 record 3). PostgreSQL checks `CREATE` on the database *before* the `IF NOT EXISTS` short-circuit of `CREATE SCHEMA` (`schemacmds.c`), so the old §4.4 claim that a generated `CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS "public"` would be a no-op no longer holds — hence rule 14. `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS` on an installed extension is skipped before any privilege check (`extension.c`), but a new one fails by privilege — hence extensions are bootstrap-only. The review rated this LOW now and MEDIUM at M5; closing it at the first bootstrap file costs nothing and avoids a bootstrap-file rewrite later (files are immutable once applied).
- **Alternatives:** Keep the migrator as owner and pin only the privileged sessions (SEC-P3-34 items 1–4 alone; rejected: leaves `ALTER DATABASE … SET` and `CREATE SCHEMA` open to the migrator credential); defer to SPK-08/M5 as the review suggested (rejected: the first bootstrap file is the cheapest moment, and the SPK-08 question "does `migrate deploy` need ownership" is already answered).
- **Reversibility:** Medium (a new bootstrap file can transfer ownership later; the rendering and rule 14 would need the matching change).

### D-ARCH-046 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 ruling 2 (TASK-003, PR #5): default privileges use the global `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE … REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS FROM PUBLIC` form; the `IN SCHEMA … REVOKE` lines and the `TABLES` line are withdrawn; C5/C11 gain `pg_default_acl` assertions (data-model §4.4/§4.6; ADR-0005 am. 9)
- **Decision:** `01-schemas.sql` ends with the three global statements for `platform_migrator`, `platform_definer` and `platform_audit_owner`. C11 asserts exactly three `pg_default_acl` rows (`defaclobjtype = 'f'`, `defaclnamespace = 0`, `<role>=X/<role>`) plus a migrator-created probe function in `platform` and `public` that PUBLIC and `platform_app` cannot execute; C5 asserts no default table/sequence/type/schema privilege row exists, so the grant manifest is provably the only source of table privileges. The statements stay in `01-schemas.sql` (not `00-roles.sql`) because `pg_default_acl` is per-database state carried by `pg_dump`, and the restore rehearsal runs only `00-roles.sql` before `pg_restore`. PR #5 implements exactly this; no code change.
- **Why:** Verified against the PostgreSQL 18 *ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES* reference: "Default privileges that are specified per-schema are added to whatever the global default privileges are for the particular object type. This means you cannot revoke privileges per-schema if they are granted globally … Per-schema `REVOKE` is only useful to reverse the effects of a previous per-schema `GRANT`." The built-in default grants `EXECUTE` on functions to PUBLIC, so only the global form removes it; TASK-003 confirmed the per-schema form writes no `pg_default_acl` row. PostgreSQL grants PUBLIC nothing on tables by default, so the `REVOKE ALL ON TABLES FROM PUBLIC` line changed nothing and is dropped rather than carried as dead text. The wider scope (also `public`) is intended: every `platform_app` `EXECUTE` is an explicit grant already.
- **Alternatives:** Per-schema `REVOKE` (the original text; a no-op); revoking `EXECUTE` function-by-function in each migration (rejected: forgettable, and C11 would be the only guard).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-047 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 ruling 3 (TASK-003, PR #5): data-model §8.4 step 2 sets `_prisma_migrations.rolled_back_at` directly (test/dev only) instead of `prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back`, which is defined for failed migrations only (P3012)
- **Decision:** The up → down → up driver (`tools/db/up-down-up.ts`) runs `prisma db execute --file down.sql`, then an injected `markRolledBack(name)` that executes, as the migrator, `UPDATE public._prisma_migrations SET rolled_back_at = now() WHERE migration_name = $1 AND finished_at IS NOT NULL AND rolled_back_at IS NULL` (exactly one row), then `migrate deploy`. The driver additionally snapshots S0 before the first `deploy` and requires `S_down = S0`, stopping before re-apply when leftovers exist. The direct write is confined to the Testcontainers suite and local `db:reset`-class flows; production rollback remains roll-forward or restore-based (§8.2, CLAUDE.md §5). `[NT-DATA-004-a]` empty-database half is claimed by `prisma/tests/migrations/up-down-up.int.test.ts`; the seeded half waits for TASK-023. No code change to PR #5.
- **Why:** Prisma's docs state `migrate resolve` "can only be used on failed migrations"; on 7.10.1 a successful migration yields P3012 "Migration `{name}` cannot be rolled back because it is not in a failed state" (TASK-003, PostgreSQL 18). The original §8.4 copied the down-migration recipe whose context is a failed `up`. Prisma has no supported "mark a successful migration rolled back" command; the history-table write is the smallest faithful equivalent, and the S0/S_down comparison makes the test stronger than the original (it proves each `down.sql` is complete, not just that `deploy` runs again).
- **Alternatives:** Drop the history row instead of setting `rolled_back_at` (rejected: loses the audit of the test's own actions and diverges from Prisma's own state model); `migrate reset` on the test database (rejected: needs database ownership, D-ARCH-045, and does not exercise `down.sql` at all).
- **Reversibility:** High (if a later Prisma adds a supported command, `markRolledBack` is a one-line swap).

### D-ARCH-048 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 ruling 4 (TASK-003, PR #5): bootstrap files are `prisma/bootstrap/00-roles.sql` and `01-schemas.sql` (`NN-<name>.sql`); the plan sheet's `0001_roles_schemas.sql` and data-model's `infra/compose/postgres/initdb/00-roles.sql` path are withdrawn (SEC-P3-37 item 4; data-model §4.4/§8.1; plan-data.mjs TASK-003 regenerated)
- **Decision:** As titled. `00-roles.sql` holds cluster- and database-level statements only (the single file the restore rehearsal runs before `pg_restore`, §8.6); `01-schemas.sql` holds schema-scoped statements, extensions and default privileges; `10-audit.sql`/`90-audit-ddl-guard.sql` follow in TASK-021. The TASK-003 sheet (`docs/04-plan/generator/plan-data.mjs`, regenerated with `gen.mjs`, 0 errors) now names both files.
- **Why:** The data-model names were the designed ones and §8.6's restore rule depends on roles and schemas being separate files; a single `0001_roles_schemas.sql` would force `01-schemas.sql` content before `pg_restore`, which fails `--exit-on-error` on `CREATE SCHEMA platform`. The compose `initdb` path predates the bootstrap lane (A2) and was the SEC-P3-37 item 4 drift.
- **Alternatives:** Rename the code to the plan sheet (rejected for the §8.6 reason).
- **Reversibility:** Low once applied (bootstrap files are immutable by ledger hash), which is why it is decided now.

### D-ARCH-049 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 ruling 5: migration-linter rules 3–9, 11 and the new rule 14 are owned by TASK-016 (Wave 0 batch 0.5); they unlock `[SEC-TC-33]` (rules 7, 8, 9, 12, 14) and `[NT-AVAIL-003-a]` (rules 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 11); rule 12's `platform_audit_owner` wording made precise (data-model §8.3; plan-data.mjs TASK-016 regenerated)
- **Decision:** TASK-003 delivered rules 1, 2, 10, 12, 13 and the contract-reversibility check with negative fixtures, exactly what its sheet demanded, and correctly claimed neither SEC-TC-33 nor NT-AVAIL-003-a. TASK-016 (database-engineer, opus; adds `prisma-core` to its locks, `NFR-AVAIL-003`/`NFR-DATA-004` to its NFRs, `tools/db/lint-migrations.ts` + fixtures to its files) implements the remaining rules with one negative fixture per statement class and claims the two test-case IDs. It also answers rule 5's open question by running a one-statement `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` migration through `migrate deploy` in the harness and recording the result in `prisma/README.md`. Rule 12 precision: in the bootstrap lane the marker is required for `SET ROLE`, `OWNER TO` and **membership** grants of `platform_audit_owner`; its own role attributes, schema privilege grants and default-privilege row need none (the TASK-003 implementation; security-analyst confirms in the PR #5 review).
- **Why:** The `migration-lint` required suite activates with US-074 (`required-suites.json`), and US-074 closes in TASK-016, which also writes the first real migration (policies, definer functions, grants) — the statements rules 7–9 police. Splitting the rules across the two tasks that ship the first two migrations keeps each PR reviewable and puts the security rules next to the objects they protect. Rule 14 exists because D-ARCH-045 made schema/extension/database DDL fail by privilege at deploy; the linter is the early warning on the PR.
- **Alternatives:** A dedicated follow-up task (rejected: adds a batch for one file; no free specialist slot in batch 0.3/0.4 without displacing demo-critical work); leave the rules to TASK-021/TASK-034 (rejected: later than the first policy-bearing migration).
- **Reversibility:** High (plan data only).

### D-ARCH-050 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 ruling 6 (TASK-004, PR #4): `npm run typecheck` = `npm run typegen && tsc -b` — React Router typegen precedes the type-check (ADR-0002 batch-0.2 amendment; ADR-0026 am. 12 note + am. 13; CLAUDE.md §2 wording handed to the orchestrator)
- **Decision:** Accepted as implemented. Root `typegen` = `npm run typegen --workspaces --if-present`; each React Router app has `"typegen": "react-router typegen"`; `typecheck` = `npm run typegen && tsc -b`. `.react-router/` stays git-ignored; `root-scripts.test.ts` asserts the `tsc -b` tail; workflows call `npm run typecheck`, never `tsc -b` directly for the apps. The SPK-01 amendment item 2 statement is refined (tsc -b remains the type-check; typegen is its generated-input step), not reversed.
- **Why:** Route modules import `./+types/<route>` declarations that exist only after `react-router typegen`; `tsc -b` alone fails on a clean checkout. No alternative satisfies "type errors in any workspace fail `typecheck`" (AT-US-001-04) without generating those declarations first. The supply-chain concern of typegen (F-SEC-1's `isbot` install) is already closed by the mandatory `entry.server.tsx` (D-ARCH-039) and the workflow-level `NPM_CONFIG_IGNORE_SCRIPTS` (D-ARCH-044).
- **Alternatives:** Commit `.react-router/` (rejected: generated files in git, drift); exclude route modules from `tsc -b` (rejected: they are product code); make `typegen` part of `build` only (rejected: `typecheck` is the DoD gate and must stand alone).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-051 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 rulings 7–8: the ADR-0018 key register has 25 rows (authoritative; "24" in the security review/dispatch brief is stale) and SPK-01 §2.5 records the m4 choice (barrel re-export lint rule kept with the Jest barrel exclusion) (ADR-0018 am. 5; SPK-01 §2 item 5)
- **Decision:** (1) The ADR-0018 amendment-1 table is authoritative with 25 rows; `packages/config/src/keys.ts` (TASK-005, PR #3) holds 25 register entries with unique `key_id`s and its test asserts the count and two-way variable parity with the schema; no plan-sheet change was needed (no `docs/04-plan` artefact says 24 — the "24 entries" wording lives in the P3 security review's SEC-P3-08 line and in the batch-0.2 dispatch brief; the review predates the four §12 "register gap" keys). Row names are a reviewer check whenever either side changes. (2) SPK-01 §2.5 now records the PR #1 review m4 outcome: TASK-002's local ESLint rule `barrel-reexport-only` enforces re-export-only `packages/*/src/index.ts`, and the `collectCoverageFrom` barrel exclusion is kept (hides nothing by construction; keeps never-imported entry files out of the denominator). No code change to PR #3 or PR #2.
- **Why:** The code implemented the ADR, which is the source of truth; the stale count was in secondary documents. The m4 record closes a tracked follow-up (STATUS.md FU-TASK001) in the place the reviewer asked for.
- **Alternatives:** Remove the barrel exclusion (v8 counts executed re-export lines; rejected as above).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-006 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · Batch 0.2 review routing: module composition root, fix-in-PR policy for security mediums, PR #4 merge hold
- **Decision:** (1) The module `index.ts` is the composition root (DI wiring); `routes.ts` is not, so TASK-002's routes-layer boundaries policy (PR #2 M1) forbids routes → service/repository imports without exception (code-reviewer recommendation; ADR-0003 silent). (2) Security MEDIUM findings that are ≤ ~30 lines in files the PR owns are fixed in the PR's own fix cycle rather than deferred: PR3-SEC-01/02 (debug-flag and transport guard parsing), PR5-SEC-01 (role passwords in the PostgreSQL log — a run guardrail held at 0), and the PR5 rule-12 deny-by-default / db:reset guard LOWs that protect audit tamper-resistance. (3) PR #4 (TASK-004) does NOT merge before its Playwright + axe harness commit lands and passes round-2 code review and the security R2 delta review; no AC split. The cooldown clears at 2026-11-12T20:18Z (playwright-core 1.65.0 publish + 7 days, PR4-SEC-03), not 18:30Z; no cooldown override. (4) PR #3 M2: backend config validation uses Joi, the web subset Zod, sharing dependency-free limits/patterns with a parity test (brief §3.3 / ADR-0001 fixed stack); no Zod at runtime in the backend schema. (5) Merge order stays row order 002 → 003 → 004 → 005 (PR #2 → #5 → #4 → #3); the later-merging PR of #5/#3 does the DB config-key reconciliation (TASK-005, since it merges last).
- **Why:** Small, owned-file security fixes are cheapest now and keep the "secrets in logs = 0" guardrail intact; merging a Must AC without its acceptance test would breach the DoD (CLAUDE.md §8); the fixed stack is a project rule.
- **Alternatives:** Defer mediums to later tasks (rejected: drift risk, guardrail breach window); split TASK-004 AC into TASK-004a (rejected: no schedule gain — PR #4 must rebase onto PR #2 first anyway); allow Zod in backend config (rejected: contradicts §3.3).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK002-01 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-002 implementation decisions (PR #2)
- **Decision:** Negative fixtures are linted in a child process (`spawnSync(process.execPath)`, no shell) because ESLint/Stylelint are ESM; fixture linting turns off `import-x/no-unresolved` and `no-extraneous-dependencies` with resolution covered by a separate test; bcrypt/bcryptjs imports are confined to `apps/api/src/modules/identity/service/bcrypt-password-hasher.ts` (TASK-049 creates exactly that path) via a subpath-blocking pattern; `lint` runs Stylelint with `--allow-empty-input`; the existing `.prettierrc` is kept; new dev deps eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y-x 0.2.0 (MIT), stylelint 17.16.0 (MIT), stylelint-use-logical 2.1.3 (CC0-1.0) — security-approved (PR-0002 §4).
- **Why/Alternatives/Reversibility:** see PR #2 body and the round-1 reviews; all High reversibility.

### D-TASK003-01 · 2026-11-12 · database-engineer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-003 implementation decisions (PR #5)
- **Decision:** Deviations 1–4 and the linter-rule ownership are ruled in D-ARCH-045..049 (all accepted). Additionally: `prisma/tests` is type-checked through `tools/tsconfig.json`; the SEC-TC-64 test seam is function parameters only (no env seam); `db:reset` requires `--yes` and a loopback host; `--passWithNoTests` is removed from `test:integration` (integration lane now requires Docker); engine per-target hashes recorded on Windows and in `node:24-bookworm-slim` and independently recomputed by security-analyst (PR-0005).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK005-01 · 2026-11-12 · backend-developer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-005 implementation decisions (PR #3)
- **Decision:** Key register = 25 rows (ratified D-ARCH-051); secret providers env, file and aws-secrets-manager with a 2 s budget (enforcement fixed in fix cycle 1, PR #3 M1); errors name keys never values; `zod` declared in packages/config for the web subset only (backend Joi per D-ORCH-006 item 4).

### D-TASK002-02 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-002 fix cycle 1 decisions (PR #2 @6ba7649)
- **Decision:** (1) bcrypt confinement uses two controls: an eslint-plugin-boundaries policy (catches `import()`, `require`/.cjs, `module.require`, `createRequire(...)(...)`, family `bcrypt*`/`@*/bcrypt*`, re-exports from the hasher) plus `no-restricted-imports` kept as the static-import net (double report on static imports accepted); the hasher re-declares the shared pattern list minus bcrypt instead of turning the rule off; aliased `createRequire` is left to the TASK-007 Semgrep backstop. (2) `SERVER_SOURCES` = `packages/*` minus browser packages (default-covered). (3) Prisma allowed at exact planned paths only (`packages/audit/src/store/prisma-store.ts`, `apps/api/src/platform/outbox/writer.ts`). (4) `@platform/*` web/admin confinement proven by in-repo `lintText` probes (fixture roots cannot classify locally resolved imports).
- **Why:** Close PR #2 M1/m1/m3/m9(a) and PR2-SEC-02 with fixture-proven policies; narrow allowances over globs.
