### D-TASK003-02 · 2026-11-12 · database-engineer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-003 fix cycle 1 (PR #5 @4574a14)
- **Decision:** Bootstrap role passwords are sent only as client-side SCRAM-SHA-256 verifiers (tools/db/scram.ts, RFC 7677 vector) and must be ≥ 24 printable ASCII characters; rule 12 in the bootstrap lane is deny-by-default (tightening allow-list + the 00-roles creation DO block pinned by SHA-256); rule 13 scans code-only text anywhere; db:reset resolves the effective host via pg-connection-string and refuses host/hostaddr params; bootstrap/reset/deploy carry connect, lock and statement timeouts; engine gunzip output capped at 256 MiB. 00-roles.sql, 01-schemas.sql and prisma-engines.json are byte-unchanged.
- **Why:** Close PR5-SEC-01 (guardrail: secrets in logs = 0), PR5-SEC-02/03/06 and CR m1–m4/m6.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK005-02 · 2026-11-12 · backend-developer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-005 fix cycle 1 behaviours (PR #3 @875409d)
- **Decision:** `DB_POOL_MAX` config bound 1..100 (factory allows 200; defaults api 10 / worker 5); tooling keys (`DATABASE_MIGRATOR_URL`, `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_BOOTSTRAP_URL`, `BOOTSTRAP_{MIGRATOR,APP}_PASSWORD[_FILE]`) live in a separate tooling set, never reach runtime `Config`, and are refused by api/worker in hardened environments; staging/production require `NODE_ENV=production`; Valkey URLs carry no query string outside local/test and pg URLs need exactly one `sslmode=verify-full`; BRAND URLs ≤ 2048 chars; BRAND_* rules live in a dependency-free module consumed by Joi (backend) and Zod (web) with a parity test; secret resolution races providers against an overall 4.5 s deadline with one jittered retry and value-free failure codes; `file:` refs confined to /run/secrets with realpath confinement.
- **Why:** Close PR #3 M1/M2/m1–m3/m7/m9 and PR3-SEC-01..04/07; D-ORCH-006 item 4.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-SEC-PR5-01 · 2026-11-12 · security-analyst (logged by orchestrator) · SCRAM verifier in PostgreSQL DDL log is acceptable; PR #5 routing corrections
- **Decision:** (1) A SCRAM-SHA-256 verifier appearing in the PostgreSQL log (from `ALTER/CREATE ROLE … PASSWORD 'SCRAM-SHA-256$…'`) is acceptable: `ALTER ROLE` cannot take bind parameters, psql `\password` / libpq `PQencryptPasswordConn` send the same form, and a logged verifier exposes no more than read access to `pg_authid`. Conditions: machine-generated passwords (≥ 32 chars recommended for M5 secret generation, TASK-014), DB logs access-controlled at `pg_authid` level, TLS verify-full at M5; DDL logging is NOT suppressed (pgaudit at M5 needs it). (2) Routing corrections: PR5-SEC-07 workflow-policy half → TASK-007 (the `prismaChildEnv` NODE_OPTIONS hardening → next task touching `tools/deps`); PR5-SEC-11 (rule 13: `ALTER ROUTINE` and a second `SET search_path`) + PR5-SEC-12 + PR5-SEC-09 extension (predefined `pg_*` role memberships) → TASK-016, binding before the first SECURITY DEFINER migration; PR5-SEC-13 (filter bootstrap vars from the `migrate deploy` child env) → TASK-034.
- **Why:** Plaintext never reaches logs (PR5-SEC-01 closed); the verifier is the standard PostgreSQL wire form.
- **Alternatives:** Disable DDL logging around bootstrap (rejected: conflicts with pgaudit/forensics).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-052 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 part 2 ruling 9 (TASK-003 PR #5 @4574a14, TASK-005 PR #3 @875409d): bootstrap passwords keep the Docker `<NAME>_FILE` idiom as a register exception (holder `bootstrap`); `*_REF` is not adopted for the tooling set; `readSecret` hardening parity → TASK-016 (ADR-0018 am. 6)
- **Decision:** ADR-0018 §3 "code holds references (`*_REF`)" is clarified as the rule for *external credentials* (sensitivity `secret-ref`: Stripe, carrier, storage, mail refs); *platform-held material* (key rows, `DATABASE_URL`, `REDIS_URL`, `SEED_*`) is a direct value in the environment (sensitivity `secret`), as amendment 1 already prescribes. The tooling set (`DATABASE_MIGRATOR_URL`, `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_BOOTSTRAP_URL`, `BOOTSTRAP_{MIGRATOR,APP}_PASSWORD`; `packages/config/src/tooling-keys.ts`) is of the second class; for the two bootstrap passwords the `_FILE` variant (`/run/secrets/<name>`) is admitted as a register exception for holder `bootstrap` only. No code change to PR #5 or PR #3 (they already agree). Binding condition: `tools/db/bootstrap.ts#readSecret` reaches the `file` provider's bar (path lexically under `/run/secrets`, realpath confinement, regular file, ≤ 64 KiB, trailing newline stripped, empty = unset, exactly one of `NAME`/`NAME_FILE`, errors name the variable only) — owner TASK-016 (holds `prisma-core`, batch 0.5), before TASK-014 mounts Compose secrets; TASK-014 references only `/run/secrets/<name>` paths. D-ORCH-006 item 5's config-key reconciliation is closed at PR #3 @875409d.
- **Why:** The bootstrap passwords are the values embedded in `DATABASE_URL`/`DATABASE_MIGRATOR_URL`, so their class follows those keys; the bootstrap tool holds the cluster-admin credential and must keep the smallest import closure (`pg` + `node:*`; no `@platform/config` graph, no AWS SDK) — the same isolation that keeps the ORM out of it; Compose delivers secrets as files and the PostgreSQL image uses `POSTGRES_PASSWORD_FILE`; at M5 the one-shot job receives the value as an injected variable, so `aws-secrets-manager:` resolution buys nothing there.
- **Alternatives:** `BOOTSTRAP_*_PASSWORD_REF` resolved by `parseSecretRef` (rejected: pulls `@platform/config` into the most privileged tool and requires the built package or the source condition in the bootstrap one-shot; the only gain is a provider the job never needs); implement `_FILE` once in `packages/config` and import it from `tools/db` (rejected for the same closure reason — parity is enforced by a requirement list and tests instead); leave `readSecret` as is (rejected: it reads any path and follows symlinks).
- **Reversibility:** High (two variable names and one reader; nothing stored).

### D-ARCH-053 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 part 2 ruling 10 (TASK-003 PR #5 @4574a14): data-model §8.3 rule 12 bootstrap lane is deny-by-default (three tightening forms + SHA-pinned role-creation block); rule 13 evaluates code-only text anywhere in the statement
- **Decision:** The Part 1 wording ("marker for `SET ROLE`, `OWNER TO`, membership grants; attributes, schema grants and default-privilege rows need none") is superseded in place. Bootstrap lane: any statement mentioning `platform_audit_owner` or an audit object needs the marker except `ALTER ROLE … <NO* attributes only>`, `GRANT USAGE|CREATE ON SCHEMA … TO platform_audit_owner` (sole grantee, no grant option), `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE platform_audit_owner … REVOKE …`, and the `00-roles.sql` role-creation `DO` block identified by the SHA-256 of its collapsed text (`AUDIT_OWNER_CREATION_BLOCK`). Rule 13: `SECURITY DEFINER` and the `SET search_path` clause are matched in the statement's code-only text (strings, dollar bodies, comments blanked) wherever they appear, not in a "header up to AS"; a path set only inside the body does not count; `ALTER FUNCTION … SECURITY DEFINER | RESET search_path | RESET ALL | SET search_path <other>` fail.
- **Why:** PR5-SEC-02 showed the allow-by-default reading let `REASSIGN OWNED`, `DROP OWNED`, `ALTER ROLE … LOGIN`, `DROP ROLE`, default-privilege `GRANT`s and multi-grantee membership grants through without the marker; code-review m2 showed `… AS $$ … $$ LANGUAGE sql SECURITY DEFINER` escaped rule 13. The SHA pin is sound because applied bootstrap files are immutable through the ledger. The code is right; the spec follows it.
- **Alternatives:** Enumerate denied forms (rejected: an open list is the bug being fixed); exempt `00-roles.sql`/`01-schemas.sql` by file name (rejected: a renamed or new file would inherit the exemption; the hash pins content, not a name).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-054 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 part 2 ruling 11 (TASK-005 PR #3 @875409d, TASK-004 PR #4 @9f5ea5e; PR #3 m8, PR #4 m5, PR3-SEC-08, PR4-SEC-01): `@platform/config/web` stays in `packages/config`; web/admin may import the subpath as a value from `vite.config.ts`/`react-router.config.ts` and as types only from `app/**`; `VITE_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` joins the schema; owner TASK-022 (ADR-0018 am. 7, ADR-0003 part-2 am. item 2, lint spec L1–L4)
- **Decision:** The Zod web subset is a build-time module: `apps/{web,admin}/vite.config.ts` call `loadWebBuildConfig(loadEnv(mode, <repo root>, ''))` and set `define: webBuildDefine(config)`; application code reads `import.meta.env.<KEY>` and imports only types (`import type { WebBuildConfig }` for the `ImportMetaEnv` augmentation). Once wired: `envPrefix` = a prefix no variable carries; `loadWebBuildConfig` rejects unknown `VITE_*` keys; `VITE_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` is a required absolute http(s) origin in `webConfigSchema` (no localhost fail-open), passed as a build arg by TASK-014; canary and no-source-map assertions in the prerender test. Interim: PR #4's `envPrefix: 'VITE_PUBLIC_'` is accepted; PR #4 does not wire the module. Lint (`lint` lock; routed by the orchestrator, default owner TASK-022): categories `app-build-config` and `config-web`; allow policies for the build-config files (value) and for `app/**` with `dependency: { kind: 'type' }` (eslint-plugin-boundaries 7.4.1 accepts `kind`); root `@platform/config` and `./testing` stay forbidden. TASK-022 gains dependency TASK-005, the wiring files and locks `web-core`, `admin-core`, `pkg-config`, `lint`.
- **Why:** PR #2 encodes "web/admin import shared and ui only" over every file under `apps/{web,admin}/**`, which ADR-0018 §1's "consumed by Vite's `define`" never anticipated; the consumer is the build, not the bundle. `packages/config` must stay the single owner of every variable (parity tests, "no web key is a backend secret" test, shared `brand-rules.ts`), and moving the subset to `packages/shared` would put `zod` into the api/worker closure. TASK-022 is the first web-build task after TASK-004/005/014 merge and extracts CSP hashes from the same build.
- **Alternatives:** Move the web subset to `packages/shared` (rejected, above); allow `@platform/config/web` as a value import from `app/**` (rejected: a runtime import path from browser code into the config package is exactly the leak the policy prevents, even if the module is browser-safe today); hand-written `env.d.ts` keys with a parity test instead of the type import (rejected: duplication for no boundary gain — the type import is erased at build); wire in PR #4 (rejected: scope and lock creep in a PR that is in round 2).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-055 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 part 2 ruling 12 (TASK-002 PR #2 code review n4): ADR-0002 §6 `npm run lint` wording set to the implemented script; `--allow-empty-input` removed by TASK-004 (PR #4) at rebase (ADR-0002 batch-0.2 amendment item 2)
- **Decision:** ADR-0002 §6 reads `eslint . --max-warnings 0 && stylelint "**/*.css" --max-warnings 0 --allow-empty-input`, with `.stylelintignore` anchored to the root and workspace output folders and the three Stylelint report options; verified on `main` @2720891. PR #4 drops `--allow-empty-input` when it rebases (it adds the first CSS; PR2-SEC-03 item 3 already names TASK-004); the wiring test (`/^stylelint .*--max-warnings 0/`) is unaffected.
- **Why:** Text drift between an Accepted ADR and `main`; the implemented form is a superset of the ADR's globs and closes the "linted nothing" hole once the flag goes.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the ADR globs (rejected: `packages/*` other than `ui` and `tests/**` CSS would be unlinted).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-056 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Batch 0.2 part 2 ruling 13 (PR #2 code review m2/m3, D-TASK002-02 item 3): ADR-0003 names the exact Prisma/driver importers (`PLANNED_PRISMA_FILES`); the typed Axios client lives in `packages/ui/src/api/**`, `packages/shared` holds no HTTP library; api/worker never import `@platform/ui` (ADR-0003 part-2 am. items 1 and 3, ADR-0026 am. 14, lint spec L5)
- **Decision:** Prisma (`@prisma/client`, `@prisma/adapter-pg`, `prisma/generated/**`) and `pg`/`pg-pool` are imported by module repositories, `apps/*/src/platform/db/**`, exactly `packages/audit/src/store/prisma-store.ts` (TASK-025) and `apps/api/src/platform/outbox/writer.ts` (TASK-043), and the `prisma/**`, `tools/db/**` and test categories; any further importer needs a lint config edit and an ADR-0003 amendment (NEEDS-ADR otherwise). The Axios client of ADR-0026 §4 is created in `packages/ui/src/api/client.ts` by TASK-026 (first API consumer; lock `ui-api`); `packages/shared` declares no `axios`/`undici`/`node-fetch`/`got`/`ky` (structure test) and stays under `platform/outbound-http-via-client`; `apps/{api,worker}` → `@platform/ui` is forbidden (policy L5). No change to the outbound-HTTP rule scope.
- **Why:** The two stores are repositories by function that cannot sit in a module's `repository/` folder (the audit writer lives in a package; the outbox writer is platform plumbing) — the ADR's one-liner was a rule of thumb, the exact-path encoding is the narrowest control. `packages/shared` is in the api/worker runtime closure: an HTTP library there would be a second egress library installed beside the single `OutboundHttpClient` of ADR-0016 §13; `packages/ui` is already the browser-only package (TASK-065's interceptor) and never reaches a server image. The brief's package list is fixed, so no `packages/api-client`.
- **Alternatives:** Exempt `packages/shared/src/api/**` from the outbound rule plus a policy forbidding api/worker from importing it (rejected: the barrel re-exports everything and `axios` would still be installed in the server closure); a new `packages/api-client` (rejected: brief §4.1 layout is fixed); the client inside each app (rejected: web and admin would duplicate the interceptor and conventions).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-007 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · PR #3 fix cycle 2 scope; PR3-SEC-03 default; lint L1–L5 owner
- **Decision:** (1) PR #3 (TASK-005) gets fix cycle 2 before merge: PR3-SEC-12 (MEDIUM, Valkey URL without `//` downgrades to plaintext — require raw `rediss://`, non-empty host, no query/fragment, refuse unparsable URLs), code-review R2-m1 (Joi/Zod parity by shared predicates `isBrandUrl`/`brandNameLength` + fast-check differential test; AT-US-002-04 "rejected identically"), PR3-SEC-15 (error class name from prototype, Error-shaped only), and PR3-SEC-03 with the security-recommended option (b): `APP_ENV=local|test` is refused when ECS task-metadata variables (`ECS_CONTAINER_METADATA_URI`, `ECS_CONTAINER_METADATA_URI_V4`) are present. (2) PR3-SEC-14 (`checkToolingEnv` for tooling URLs) → TASK-014/TASK-016 callers, helper in packages/config by TASK-014; PR3-SEC-13 → TASK-014 entrypoint + IaC Checkov rule; code-review R2-m2 → TASK-014. (3) Lint rules L1–L5 (config/web consumption, D-ARCH-054) are owned by TASK-022 (architect default applied). (4) PR #4 at merge-main/harness: drop `--allow-empty-input` (PR2-SEC-03 item 3), fix R2-m1 (unnecessary cast) and R2-m2 (`lint` runs `typegen` first), and must NOT wire `@platform/config/web`.
- **Why:** Fix-in-PR policy for small mediums (D-ORCH-006 item 2); AT-US-002-04 must hold literally; option (b) is a cheap second signal that fails closed on AWS without new config.
- **Alternatives:** PR3-SEC-03 option (a) (require an explicit second env flag) — rejected as more config surface.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-008 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · Pull dependency-satisfied tasks forward while the batch 0.2 tail completes
- **Decision:** While PR #3 (TASK-005, fix cycle 2) and PR #4 (TASK-004, Playwright harness after the 2026-11-12T20:18Z cooldown) finish, dispatch the tasks whose dependencies are already merged on main and whose locks are free: TASK-008 (SPK-03, 0.3, deps TASK-003), TASK-009 (SPK-02, 0.3, deps TASK-001) and TASK-013 (0.4, deps TASK-003, lock pkg-authz). TASK-006/TASK-007 (need TASK-005) and TASK-012 (0.4) follow as capacity frees. Docker/Testcontainers runs are serialized host-wide because the host is memory-constrained (~4 GB free; one gate run was reaped).
- **Why:** Keeps MAX_PARALLEL_DEVELOPERS utilisation without breaking the DAG or batch locks; batches are a scheduling aid, dependencies and locks are the constraint.
- **Alternatives:** Wait for batch 0.2 to close (rejected: ~6 h idle until the cooldown clears).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK005-03 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-005 fix cycle 2 behaviours (PR #3 @0e48a2a)
- **Decision:** (1) Brand URLs: non-ASCII hosts must be punycode (`xn--…`); credentials in brand URLs refused (would land in the public bundle); non-RFC 3986 characters refused on both Joi and Zod sides via shared predicates `isBrandName`/`isBrandUrl`/`brandNameLength` (code points); same validator for `VITE_API_BASE_URL` and `VITE_FEATURE_FLAGS_ENDPOINT`. (2) Postgres URL query parameters in staging/production are allow-listed: `sslmode`, `sslrootcert`, `sslnegotiation`, `uselibpqcompat`, `application_name`, `connect_timeout`; host must be non-empty without `%`. (3) Valkey URL must start with raw `rediss://`, non-empty host, no `?`/`#`; unparsable URLs refused. (4) Transport guard skips keys already rejected by the schema (no double report; startup still fails). (5) Permanent provider errors (`SECRET_FILE_OUTSIDE_ROOT`, `_NOT_REGULAR`, `_TOO_LARGE`, `EACCES`, `EPERM`) are not retried; if the deadline passes during the retry sleep, the first diagnosis is kept. (6) `APP_ENV=local|test` refused when ECS task-metadata variables are set (D-ORCH-007).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-009 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · Batch 0.2 merge order change: PR #3 (TASK-005) before PR #4 (TASK-004)
- **Decision:** PR #3 merges as soon as its round-3 reviews and a merge-main gate pass, ahead of PR #4, which cannot complete before its Playwright harness lands after 2026-11-12T20:18Z. Supersedes D-ORCH-006 item 5's row order for #4/#3; TASK-005 still performs the TASK-003 key reconciliation (already present since fix cycle 1) at its merge-main.
- **Why:** TASK-004 and TASK-005 are independent; TASK-006 and TASK-007 (batch 0.3, critical path) depend on TASK-005, not TASK-004.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-010 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · Host memory pressure: concurrency cap and post-merge verification by tree equality
- **Decision:** (1) Until lane A/C run hosted (TASK-007/TASK-015), at most 3 build agents run concurrently and heavy commands (npm ci, builds, full suites, Docker) are serialized host-wide; new dispatches wait for a running batch to report if the host is under pressure. (2) When an orchestrator post-merge rerun on main is reaped by the host, post-merge verification is satisfied if `git diff <gated PR head> <main squash> -- . ':!docs' ':!.claude'` is empty and the PR head's full gate is recorded (applied to TASK-005: main b1597fd ≡ 3e390e0). The reaped run is not restarted automatically.
- **Why:** Claude Code reaped three background gate runs for low memory; repeated restarts would compound the pressure. Squash merges of an up-to-date branch produce the same code tree that was gated.
- **Alternatives:** Re-run full gates on main after every merge regardless (rejected while memory-constrained; resumes once hosted CI lanes exist).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK008-01 · 2026-11-12 · database-engineer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · SPK-03 verdict GO (PR #7)
- **Decision:** ADR-0005 `withTenantTx` = Prisma 7.10.1 interactive transaction whose first statement is a parameterised `set_config(…, true)`; raw-`pg` fallback not needed. Exit item (d) asserted as the `set_config` server cost (p95 0.087 ms; 0.484 ms p95 above `SELECT 1`); end-to-end per-transaction delta (one extra round trip, 1.9–3.6 ms p95 on Docker Desktop) recorded, not asserted — condition C-1: re-measure on ubuntu-latest (TASK-016 / performance-engineer). Findings F-01..F-13 bind TASK-016 (nested `$transaction` = SAVEPOINT keeps context; root client inside tx silently returns 0 rows; maxWait 2 s / timeout 5 s → P2028; `SET LOCAL` takes no bind param; RLS violation = P2010/42501, never echo the message; reset value `''` not NULL; guest hash lowercase hex; definer migrations grant EXECUTE before owner change). ADR-0005 wording notes W-1/W-2 → architect.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK009-01 · 2026-11-12 · backend-developer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · SPK-02 verdict: UPS GO; FedEx/DHL FALLBACK for named operations (PR #6)
- **Decision:** Where official carrier docs were unreadable, secondary sources are labelled "confirmed (secondary)". SPK-02 §4 raises an ADR-0010 amendment request (DHL `cancelShipment` → `CAPABILITY_NOT_SUPPORTED`; UPS GIF→PDF conversion in the adapter; "diffed by a person with portal access" line for FedEx/DHL READMEs; host allow-list incl. FedEx ETD hosts; no hard-coded token lifetimes) — due before TASK-135/139/140.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK013-01 · 2026-11-12 · backend-developer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-013 permission catalogue decisions D-T013-01..12 (PR #8)
- **Decision:** 40-permission typed catalogue in six context modules; 17 roles (8 operator, 5 customer, 4 partner) seeded by two generated expand-only migrations with deterministic UUIDv7 IDs (sheet/US-073 over data-model §3.3/§9.2 reference seed — data-architect to align); Super Admin holds every role-assignable permission, the two webhook permissions are `signatureOnly` and held by no role (architect to confirm); reserved operator-only seams `shipment:read:any_org`, `org:read:any_org`; every non-reserved permission is held by some non-Super-Admin role (operator Admin gets `featureflag:manage`); least-privilege where requirements are silent (Viewer lacks `session:manage`/`notification_pref:manage` — product-manager to confirm); read-only = verb `read`; Prisma `@@index` cannot express `INCLUDE` (possible `migrate diff` drift noted); sheet file names win over ADR-0008's; `RoleScope` lives in authz until a shared enums module exists. Seed migration declared `data-changing: no` (INSERT into tables created by the previous migration) — security to validate.
- **Reversibility:** High (role grants change via a v2 migration).

### D-058 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · Role-permission matrix ruling for TASK-013 (PR #8, D-T013-05): the self-service set `session:manage`, `notification_pref:manage`, `notification:read` is held by every seeded role; four roles corrected, the rest confirmed
- **Decision:** (a) **Self-service set.** `session:manage`, `notification_pref:manage` and `notification:read` are flagged `selfService` in the catalogue and granted to **all 17 seeded roles**. They guard only `user`-tenancy `/me/*` routes (own sessions, own preferences, own notification log; data-model `USER_OWNED` tables with RLS `user_id = app_actor_id()`), so "manage" here never reaches another user; cross-user session or preference management is M2 under distinct operator-only `:any_user`/`:any_org` permissions. The REQ-RBAC-004 invariants "Viewer holds no create/update/delete/manage" and "Compliance Auditor holds only read and audit" carve out exactly this set, and a new positive invariant asserts the set on every role. (b) **Grant changes TASK-013 must apply** (in PR #8's fix cycle while the v1 seed is unmerged; otherwise as a v2 generated migration with `CATALOGUE_VERSION` bump): CUSTOMER_VIEWER + `session:manage`, + `notification_pref:manage` (9 → 11); CUSTOMER_FINANCE + `notification_pref:manage`, + `order:read` (6 → 8); CUSTOMER_ADMIN + `privacy:delete` (29 → 30); OPERATOR_COMPLIANCE_AUDITOR + `session:manage`, + `notification_pref:manage` (3 → 5). No other cell changes; Super Admin stays at 38 (the two `signature-only` webhook permissions belong to no role — REQ-RBAC-004 now reads "every role-assignable permission", confirming D-T013-02 on the requirement side; ADR-0008 wording remains the architect's). (c) **Confirmed as seeded:** Viewer without `payment:read`, `document:download`, `document:email`, `privacy:export`, `privacy:delete`; Shipper without `org:update`, `org:member:invite`, `org:member:manage`, `payment_method:manage`, `payment:read`; operator roles other than Super Admin hold self-service plus `carrier:status:read` / `org:read` / `featureflag:manage` as seeded; partner roles hold `org:*` plus self-service only; D-T013-04 dead-permission rule and D-T013-06 verb rule accepted (`selfService` is a flag, not a verb exception, so the verb rule stays literal). (d) **Requirement side:** REQUIREMENTS 1.5 (REQ-RBAC-004 wording, REQ-RBAC-014 note, REQ-ACCT scope note, DR-A07), US-066 notes and AC (guard scenarios for the set and for `privacy:delete`), A-074 updated, A-103/A-104 added, PRD change log 1.5. No REQ/US IDs, priorities, milestones or counts change; traceability.md untouched.
- **Why:** Permissions resolve from the *active* membership (REQ-RBAC-008, ADR-0008 §4). A Viewer in a business organisation, or a Compliance Auditor in the operator organisation, who lacked `session:manage` could not see or revoke their own sessions while that membership is active — yet REQ-AUTH-021/022 are **Must** for every user (ASVS 4.0.3 V3.3.4, D-047), US-027 says "customer or staff user", REQ-ADMIN-005/007 put "My sessions" in front of every staff user, and the PRD Super Admin persona lists "manage own sessions". The least-privilege reading of the Viewer invariant was correct against the letter of REQ-RBAC-004 and wrong against the product: the invariant exists to stop Viewer touching organisation data, not their own device list. The personal-organisation switch (REQ-ORG-006, Should) would be a workaround, not a design. `notification_pref:manage` follows the same logic (own consent records, REQ-ACCT-012). Customer Finance without `order:read` could call `GET /orders/{id}/payment` but never list or open the order; customer Admin without `privacy:delete` while Shipper had it was an inconsistency in the scope note, not a rule. Viewer keeps no `payment:read`: card brand/last four and payment identifiers are what Finance is for, and the shipment detail already shows charge lines from the snapshot.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the literal invariant and tell Viewers to switch to their personal organisation (rejected: hides a Must behind a Should and breaks when a user has no personal org in later milestones); rename to `session:manage:self` / `notification_pref:manage:self` (rejected: churn across the catalogue, OpenAPI `x-permission` on five routes, PR #8 and REQ-RBAC-014 for no behavioural gain — the `user` tenancy already makes them self-scoped); grant `payment:read` to Viewer (rejected for now, telemetry-gated in A-104); treat `privacy:export`/`privacy:delete` as self-service for Viewer too (rejected: D-051 denies `privacy:export` for bulk-exfiltration reasons and REQ-ACCT-016/017 are Could; a Viewer's data-subject requests go through the operator until M4/M5).
- **Reversibility:** High (grants change by a generated seed migration; invariant text in one test). Follow-ups: principal-architect to mirror the carve-out in ADR-0008 §3 and to confirm that `privacy:export` scopes shipments and quotes to the active organisation (otherwise the personal-org switch would bypass the Viewer exclusion); security-analyst to re-check the Viewer and Compliance Auditor rows in the PR #8 review.

### D-SEC-PR8-01 · 2026-11-12 · security-analyst (logged by orchestrator) · Insert-only generated seeds are not data-changing; released seed versions are immutable
- **Decision:** (1) A generated seed migration consisting only of `INSERT … VALUES … ON CONFLICT ("id") DO NOTHING` into tables created by a prior migration is `data-changing: no`; any `DELETE`, `UPDATE`, upsert (`ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE`) or `MERGE` makes it `data-changing: yes` and triggers the backup/restore-rehearsal guardrail (linter rule 10 to be extended for upserts — PR8-SEC-03). (2) Once a catalogue version's seed directory is merged, it is pinned by SHA-256 and never rewritten; grant changes ship as a new versioned migration (PR8-SEC-01, code-review PR #8 M2). (3) D-058 grant changes are applied to v1 in PR #8's fix cycle because v1 is not yet merged; v1 is pinned at merge.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-011 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · Wave 0 PRs #6/#7/#8 fix-cycle scope and routing
- **Decision:** Fix cycle 1: PR #6 (TASK-009) — M1 neutral UPS label option set (Label Recovery PDF / GIF decode in the documents module with a justified decoder / ZPL-GIF + PM decision; REQ-DOC-002 cited), m1, m2 (pin source SHAs), m3/PR6-SEC-01 and m4/PR6-SEC-02/03/06 as additions to the SPK-02 §4 request, NITs incl. gitleaks line and `transactionSrc` from BRAND_*; PR #7 (TASK-008) — M1 timing benchmark opt-in (`SPK03_BENCH=1`), m1, m2, m6, doc rewording m3/m4/m5 + F-findings strengthened per PR7-SEC-01/02/03/05, NITs; PR #8 (TASK-013) — M1 key-based Auditor rule + category/key consistency, M2/PR8-SEC-01 seed version pinning + refuse overwrite + throw for version > 1 until a delta renderer exists, D-058 grants + selfService flag + invariants (PR8-SEC-04), PR8-SEC-02 guard hardening, PR8-SEC-03 linter rule 10 upserts, m1–m6, full test:integration + test:coverage re-run. Binding TASK-016 acceptance: PR7-SEC-01/02/05/07 items, F-01..F-13, C-1 (due before G3), port-then-delete of the spike file; TASK-020 merge condition: PR8-SEC-05 route-binding check; TASK-051: PR7-SEC-04 de-claim trigger; TASK-006/010: PR7-SEC-03 items 2–3 (`errorFormat: 'minimal'`, P2039/originalCode), PR7-SEC-06 item 3; TASK-231/235: PR8-SEC-06 role rank table; TASK-060: PR8-SEC-07; TASK-221: PR8-SEC-09 baseline reconciler; TASK-113: PR6-SEC-01/03/04; TASK-131/132/139: PR6-SEC-05/07; TASK-135/139/140: PR6-SEC-02/04/06; TASK-261: PR6-SEC-08; qa-lead: SEC-TC-66/67/68 + PR7/PR8 SEC-TC additions. principal-architect folds the PR7-SEC §4a deltas into ADR-0005 am. 10, PR6-SEC-01/05 + UPS label options into ADR-0010, PR8 items into ADR-0008/data-model.
