### D-ARCH-057 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 ruling 1 (SPK-02, TASK-009 PR #6 @4e5d05f): ADR-0010 amendment 11 — DHL `cancelShipment` capability-gated (`CAPABILITY_NOT_SUPPORTED`, Platform-side void), UPS GIF→PDF conversion inside `packages/carriers` with an in-house GIF decoder (no new dependency), README line "Diffed against official spec by a person", derived host allow-list (no env override; DHL path-based environment separation), token TTL from `expires_in` with configured skew/cap, quota seeds
- **Decision:** (1) SPK-02 §1 and the README "re-verify" sections are the authoritative status; the 13 corrections of SPK-02 §2 are adopted (UPS void `DELETE`, label recovery, versions v2/v3, string token fields and token path outside `/api`, DHL two tracking forms, FedEx 200-with-`errors[]`, FedEx 1,400/10 s + 10-minute 403 penalty, `pushTracking=false` ×3, `maxTrackingNumbersPerCall` 1/30/200). (2) `capabilities().operations.cancelShipment: boolean`; DHL declares `false`; the REQ-CARR-003 guard covers it with no I/O; cancellation without a carrier void = shipment `CANCELLED`, pickup cancelled where present, `carrier_void = UNSUPPORTED` (terminal, no retry, never `EXCEPTION`), label `VOIDED` Platform-side, refund proceeds (D-017 unchanged); contract case `cancel-capability-not-supported-no-io`. (3) UPS adapter requests GIF (+ ZPL), converts to `PDF_4X6`/`PDF_A4`/`PDF_LETTER` in `packages/carriers/src/labels/{gif-decode,png-encode,raster-label-pdf}.ts` (in-house LZW GIF decoder, PNG via `node:zlib`, `pdfkit` imported only there), declares `labelFormats` as what it returns, GIF kept only as a raw-payload `$blob`; determinism + size + property tests; new risk R-ARCH-25 with fallback (ZPL only). (4) REQ-CARR-019 README fields gain `Diffed against official spec by a person: yes (date, role) | no`, enforced by TASK-191; `no` allowed, listed under COND-SANDBOX-DEMO; synthesised fixtures counted in evidence. (5) `ttl = clamp(Number(expires_in) − CARRIER_TOKEN_REFRESH_SKEW_S, 0, CARRIER_TOKEN_TTL_MAX_S)` (60/3600, config keys, TASK-131); missing/non-numeric `expires_in` → uncacheable token, never a default lifetime. (6) Outbound allow-list = union over enabled carrier accounts of the adapter's static `HOSTS[account.environment]` + Stripe; no configuration key adds a host; UPS `wwwcie.ups.com`/`onlinetools.ups.com`, FedEx `apis-sandbox.fedex.com`/`apis.fedex.com` + ETD hosts only while `uploadCustomsDocuments` is supported, DHL `express.api.dhl.com` with path separation `/mydhlapi/test` vs `/mydhlapi` (unit test); production accounts refused unless `APP_ENV=production`. (7) Quota seeds: DHL 500, FedEx/UPS 0. Plan: `carrierif` (TASK-075), `oauth` (TASK-131), `dhl`/`fedex`/`ups` (TASK-135/139/140), `fixtures` (TASK-191) acceptance lines; risks.md R-ARCH-02 result + R-ARCH-25.
- **Why:** No sandbox access exists in this run, so an unverifiable DHL cancel operation would ship as dead code with a retrying saga behind it; REQ-DOC-002 requires PDF labels and `pdfkit` (baseline) embeds only JPEG/PNG while every npm GIF decoder fails the 18-month rule (`omggif` 2022-06, `gifwrap` 2022-06, `@jimp/gif` 2024-02, `gifuct-js` 2025-01) or is a toolkit (`jimp`); an env-settable host list would widen the SSRF surface; hard-coded lifetimes already proved wrong (UPS 4 h → 1 h on 2026-04-01).
- **Alternatives:** DHL option (b) "keep and prove live" (rejected: nothing can prove it this run); UPS labels GIF-only (rejected: fails REQ-DOC-002 for every UPS booking); conversion in the `documents` module (rejected: leaks a wire fact into the saga and makes `labelFormats` untruthful); `jimp`/`sharp` (rejected: maintenance rule / native `optionalDependencies` dropped by `--omit=optional`); `OUTBOUND_EXTRA_HOSTS` env key (rejected: SSRF widening).
- **Demo-critical impact:** none — US-196/216/229/261 are Should non-DC; US-237 (Must non-DC) still works (DHL pickup cancel exists). product-manager flags: REQ-BOOK-029/REQ-DOC-013/REQ-CARR-003/REQ-CARR-019 wording; ASSUMPTIONS.md entry "DHL does not invoice a never-handed-over shipment".
- **Reversibility:** High (capability flag flips when the official spec shows an operation; converter is one module with a declared fallback). Code change required in PR #6: none.

### D-ARCH-058 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 ruling 2 (SPK-03, TASK-008 PR #7 @985a552): ADR-0005 amendment 10 — W-1/W-2 wording, findings F-01..F-13 binding on TASK-016/051/276, condition C-1 owned by TASK-016 (hosted `security-integration` run on ubuntu-latest), verified by performance-engineer at P6
- **Decision:** (1) W-1: "one extra database round trip per transaction plus ~0.1 ms server-side" replaces "~0.2 ms"; the end-to-end delta is recorded, never asserted. (2) W-2: the nested-transaction caveat belongs to the query-extension pattern; nested `tx.$transaction` = SAVEPOINT on the same connection with context kept; the hazard is the root client inside the callback. (3) Binding rules: `TenantDb = Omit<Prisma.TransactionClient, '$transaction' | '$queryRawUnsafe' | '$executeRawUnsafe'>`; root client private + no-repository-import check; explicit `transactionOptions` (maxWait ≤ pool checkout budget, timeout ≥ `statement_timeout`), `P2028` → retryable 503; `set_config` only, lint against `SET LOCAL`/unsafe raw in platform/db and repositories; extension wraps the client; `P2010`+`42501` → 403 `AUTHZ_DENIED` + `rls_write_rejected`, message never echoed; `55P03` → retryable 503, claim path treats it like 0 rows; `''` ≡ NULL; lowercase-hex guest hash computed server-side; definer migrations GRANT/REVOKE **before** `OWNER TO platform_definer` (data-model §4.5 example reordered); `$executeRaw` for void functions; Jest path before `--selectProjects`; F-13 harness → TASK-276. (4) C-1: TASK-016 keeps `prisma/tests/spikes/spk03-rls-tx.int.test.ts` in the integration project until its PR's hosted `security-integration` job (D-ARCH-021) has run it on ubuntu-latest with `SPK03_REPORT` as an artifact; the delta is recorded in SPK-03 §4 and ADR-0005 am. 10; performance-engineer verifies at P6 and re-measures on RDS at M5; the permanent suite asserts only the server-side cost (≤ 1 ms p95). Plan: `rls` (TASK-016) x + acceptance; risks.md R-ARCH-03 result.
- **Why:** The spike measured the statement at 0.087 ms server-side; the literal "with vs without" wording cannot be met on any host whose RTT exceeds 1 ms and would turn a network property into a flaky assertion; the hosted job already exists for `prisma/**` PRs, so C-1 costs no extra lane and no new owner; the migrator's `INHERIT FALSE` membership makes the grant-after-owner order fail on PostgreSQL 16+.
- **Alternatives:** performance-engineer owns C-1 in a separate hosted run (rejected: hosted minutes, D-ORCH-001); assert the end-to-end delta with a generous bound (rejected: still host-dependent); expose savepoints on `TenantDb` now (rejected: no use case, hides the root-client hazard less well).
- **Reversibility:** High. Code change required in PR #7: none (optional: relax the client-observed assertion to recorded).

### D-ARCH-059 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 rulings 3 and 5 (TASK-013 PR #8 @efe23df; D-T013-02/03/04/06/07/11/12; product-manager D-058): ADR-0008 amendment 2 — Super Admin = every role-assignable permission, `signatureOnly` webhooks held by no role, `selfService` carve-out mirrored, reserved `:any_org` seams, dead-permission and verb rules, layout/file names and table shape aligned to the code; `privacy:export` scoping confirmed by construction
- **Decision:** (1) CONFIRMED D-T013-02: Super Admin holds the catalogue minus `webhook:stripe:receive` and `webhook:mock_gateway:receive` (`signatureOnly`, held by no role) — REQ-RBAC-004 says the same since D-058. (2) D-058 (a) mirrored: `session:manage`, `notification_pref:manage`, `notification:read` flagged `selfService`, held by all 17 roles, carved out of the Compliance Auditor ("⊆ read ∪ audit ∪ selfService") and Viewer ("no create/update/delete/manage except selfService; never download/email/export/delete") invariants; positive invariant on every role. (3) `privacy:export` (REQ-ACCT-016, Could) is RLS-bound to the active organisation and `app_actor_id()` inside `withTenantTx`, so the Viewer exclusion cannot be bypassed via the personal-organisation switch — confirmed, test added by that story's task. (4) Adopted: reserved operator-only `shipment:read:any_org`, `org:read:any_org`; dead-permission rule "held by ≥ 1 role besides Super Admin" (`featureflag:manage` → Operator Admin); verb = last segment unless a scope qualifier; deterministic UUIDv7 seed ids; `RoleScope` in `packages/authz` for now. (5) File names: `permissions/<context>.ts` + `permissions/index.ts`, `roles.ts`, `guard.ts`, `authorise.ts`, `routes.ts`, `matrix-doc.ts`, `seed-sql.ts`, `tools/authz/generate.ts`, `docs/03-architecture/generated/role-permission-matrix.md`; ADR-0008 §2's sketch table shape replaced by data-model §3.3's. (6) **Required in PR #8 before merge (D-058 b):** Viewer +`session:manage`,+`notification_pref:manage`; Finance +`notification_pref:manage`,+`order:read`; Admin +`privacy:delete`; Compliance Auditor +`session:manage`,+`notification_pref:manage`; `selfService` flag; guard carve-outs + `SELF_SERVICE_MISSING`; regenerated matrix and v1 seed; D-058 scenarios of US-066 as tests. Super Admin stays at 38.
- **Why:** At `efe23df` the Viewer (9) and the Compliance Auditor (3) lack the self-service set that REQ-RBAC-004 (Must, DC story US-066) now mandates, so the PR fails its own acceptance as reworded; a webhook permission held by a human role would let a user forge gateway events; the `:any_org` seams give DR-A07's rule a subject so the escalation test is not vacuous.
- **Alternatives:** keep "full catalogue" literally and seed the webhook permissions to Super Admin (rejected: a human must never pass the signature-only gate); `:self` qualifiers (rejected by D-058: churn without behavioural gain); keep ADR-0008's P3 file names (rejected: the sheet and the code agree and nothing else referenced them).
- **Reversibility:** High (grants change by a generated migration; ADR text only).

### D-ARCH-060 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 ruling 4 (TASK-013 PR #8 D-T013-08/09): roles and permissions are populated by the generated versioned migration, not by the reference seed — data-model §3.3, §9.2, §8.3 rule 10, §1.7, §7.3, §12 A17 aligned; reference seed = parity check; TASK-221 writes the `CONFIG_CHANGE` entry per `CATALOGUE_VERSION`
- **Decision:** `prisma/migrations/<ts>_authz_seed_v<N>/` (rendered by `seed-sql.ts`, deterministic ids, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, `created_by` = SYSTEM actor, `data-changing: no`) is the only writer of `permissions`, `roles`, `role_permissions`; a grant change bumps `CATALOGUE_VERSION` and ships a new migration (narrowing/`UPDATE` migrations are `data-changing: yes`). The reference seed (TASK-023) never writes these tables; its `authz` step verifies catalogue ⇔ database parity (drift fails) and the US-073 task (TASK-221, new dependency on TASK-023) writes one `CONFIG_CHANGE` audit entry by the SYSTEM actor per catalogue version, idempotent through `reference_dataset_versions(dataset='authz_matrix', version, content_sha256)`. Linter rule 10 clarified: insert-only generated seed = `no`. Covering indexes (`INCLUDE`) are raw SQL; `migrate diff` drift on them is tolerated by name in the TASK-016 drift check. `role_scope` enum source note (`RoleScope` in `packages/authz`).
- **Why:** US-073 and REQ-RBAC-013 name the migration as the mechanism; REQ-RBAC-002's "never widens without a migration" is provable only with a single writer; applied migrations are immutable while a seed upsert is a second idempotency logic to get wrong; the audit entry needs the audit writer and the SYSTEM actor, which exist in the seed path, not in SQL migrations (audit tables are owned by `platform_audit_owner`).
- **Alternatives:** reference seed upserts roles from the catalogue as §9.2 said (rejected: two writers); write the audit entry from the migration (rejected: no audit writer in SQL, no hash chain); skip the audit entry for migration-driven changes (rejected: REQ-RBAC-013).
- **Reversibility:** High. Code change required in PR #8: none for this item.

### D-ARCH-061 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 ruling 6 (PR #3 security review round 3 R3.5, PR3-SEC-03; D-ORCH-007 item 1, D-TASK005-03 item 6): ADR-0018 amendment 8 — hardened behaviour keys on `APP_ENV` (Mock refusal at `APP_ENV=production`), `NODE_ENV=production` is a staging/production deployment requirement enforced by the images and the TASK-014 preflight, ECS task-metadata second signal recorded, `APP_ENV=benchmark` vocabulary closed
- **Decision:** §3 corrected to `APP_ENV=production` for the Mock carrier/gateway refusal (staging allowed behind the gateway HMAC, ADR-0010 am. 9, SEC-TC-24); every refusal keys on `APP_ENV`, none on `NODE_ENV`. §4: staging and production require `NODE_ENV=production`, set by the runtime images and refused by the TASK-014 entrypoint preflight when `APP_ENV ∈ {staging, production}` and `NODE_ENV ≠ production` (with the NODE_OPTIONS allow-list and `PG*` refusals; exit 78 ≤ 5 s; container test); option (a) stays rejected (D-ORCH-007). Second signal: `APP_ENV ∈ {local, test}` refused when `ECS_CONTAINER_METADATA_URI` / `_V4` is present and non-empty (`ECS_METADATA_KEYS`); optional third signal `AWS_EXECUTION_ENV=AWS_ECS_*`; residual (hardened non-ECS host) → M5 IaC `app_env` validation. `APP_ENV` has no `benchmark` value: the `-bench` image refuses unless `APP_ENV ∈ {local, test}` (threat-model R-12 / SEC-TC-18 text → security-analyst before TASK-018). Plan: `compose` (TASK-014) preflight acceptance line.
- **Why:** The code (`guards.ts`) has keyed on `APP_ENV` since PR #3 and is consistent with ADR-0010 am. 9 and SEC-TC-24; a `NODE_ENV`-keyed guard would be weakened by a mis-set library switch; the preflight is the one place that already refuses process-level misconfiguration before application code runs.
- **Alternatives:** a `packages/config` guard on `NODE_ENV` (rejected: `config` loads after Node has applied `NODE_ENV`, and the preflight already exists); leave `NODE_ENV` unconstrained (rejected: Express/React production behaviours are part of the hardened posture).
- **Reversibility:** High. Code change required: none (PR #3 merged; TASK-014 sheet amended).

### D-ARCH-062 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 revisions after the PR #6/#7/#8 reviews and D-058/D-SEC-PR8-01/D-ORCH-011 landed mid-batch: D-ARCH-057 item (3) superseded — UPS PDF labels via the official Label Recovery API and a new optional `recoverLabel` operation, no PDF code in `packages/carriers`; PR6-SEC-01/05/06 + code-review m3/m4/n6, PR7-SEC §4a, PR8-SEC-01/02/03/08/09 + code-review M1/M2 folded into ADR-0010 am. 11, ADR-0005 am. 10, ADR-0008 am. 2 and data-model
- **Decision:** (1) **Supersedes D-ARCH-057 item (3).** Verified in the pinned official spec (`UPS-API/api-documentation@fd57ca9`, `Shipping.yaml:11251-11263`, `:13479-13496`): Ship `LabelImageFormat.Code` = GIF|ZPL|EPL|SPL; Label Recovery `LabelImageFormat.Code` = GIF|**PDF**|ZPL|EPL|SPL. `CarrierAdapter` gains the optional `recoverLabel(ref, formats, ctx)` (idempotent, retryable, `transactional` pool, capability-gated; UPS and Mock `true`, FedEx and DHL `false`; admitted under A-057). The UPS adapter ships with GIF/ZPL, returns `labels: []` for PDF (GIF only as a raw-payload `$blob`, never a document), declares `labelFormats = [PDF_4X6, ZPL]`; the saga document step (ADR-0014 step 4) calls `recoverLabel` for offered formats missing from the create result with step-4 retry/exception semantics, never a second `createShipment`. **No `pdfkit`, raster decoding or PDF rendering in `packages/carriers`** (ADR-0003/0019: the documents module owns PDF). Contingency (R-ARCH-25 rewritten): raster → PDF converter in the documents module with an in-house GIF decoder; last resort ZPL-only with a product-manager re-scope. (2) **ADR-0010 am. 11 items 5, 6, 8, 9 (PR6-SEC-01/05/06/07, m3, m4, n6):** egress rules are `EgressRule { origin, pathPrefixes, tier }` — exact origins, no wildcards, path prefixes per environment (DHL `/mydhlapi/test/` vs `/mydhlapi/`; UPS `/api/` + `/security/v1/oauth/token`; FedEx ETD only while supported and verified), derived from enabled accounts, no env override, production rules only under `APP_ENV=production`; carrier-supplied URLs never dereferenced (inline label bytes; `URL_ONLY` negative fixture); token-endpoint failure handling (negative cache `CARRIER_TOKEN_FAIL_TTL_S=60`, per-carrier token bucket `CARRIER_TOKEN_REQUESTS_PER_S`, 403 → carrier-wide cool-down `CARRIER_TOKEN_PENALTY_COOLDOWN_S=600`, SEC-TC-68); identifiers URL-encoded on fixed templates, query strings stripped from logged URLs, README "PII and redaction" subsections; ambiguous DHL creates reconciled via tracking lookup. (3) **ADR-0005 am. 10 revised per PR7-SEC §4a:** run-time `TenantDb` Proxy + `set_config`/`app.*` token ban + `prosrc` scan (PR7-SEC-05); AsyncLocalStorage re-entrance guard `NESTED_TENANT_TX`, run-time throw for AT-US-075-04, SYSTEM-table writers on `TenantDb`, W-2 names nested `withTenantTx` (PR7-SEC-01); literal `true`, validated strings only, `PGOPTIONS` refusal, fresh-connection probe, session-scope negative control, C13 `app.%` rows (PR7-SEC-02); `mapDbError` on `driverAdapterError.cause.originalCode`, `errorFormat: 'minimal'`, retryable 40001/40P01/57014 (PR7-SEC-03); renderer predicate allow-list + C3 token scan (PR7-SEC-07); definer functions under `SET ROLE platform_definer`, linter rule 15 mandatory (PR7-SEC-09); GUEST_CAPABLE no-de-claim trigger (PR7-SEC-04; data-model §4.3, TASK-051); per-transaction timeouts + readiness drift check (PR7-SEC-06); poolers outside the GO (PR7-SEC-08); the spike's client-observed assertion behind `SPK03_BENCH=1` (PR7-SEC-10, D-ORCH-011). (4) **ADR-0008 am. 2 / data-model revised per PR8:** `isAuditPermission` keyed on the resource segment, `category` derived from the resource with an explicit map, `signatureOnly` only on `webhook:*` (M1, PR8-SEC-02); released seed directories pinned by SHA-256, `--check`/write refuse changes to a pinned version, `artefacts()` throws for `CATALOGUE_VERSION > 1` until a delta renderer exists, generic migration-immutability check → TASK-016/TASK-007 (M2, PR8-SEC-01); `ON CONFLICT ("id") DO NOTHING`, optional DR-A07 CHECK and database-side assertions (PR8-SEC-08); rule 10 counts `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE`/`MERGE` as data-changing (PR8-SEC-03, D-SEC-PR8-01); the REQ-RBAC-013 `CONFIG_CHANGE` mechanism is a post-migrate **reconciler** as `SYSTEM_JOB` (digest of the three tables vs last audited digest in `reference_dataset_versions('authz_matrix')`, baseline back-fill ∅ → current, nothing when unchanged; TASK-221; G3 residual note if cut) (PR8-SEC-09). Plan: `ups`, `label` (TASK-155), `carrierif` (TASK-075), `oauth` (TASK-131), `dhl`, `fedex`, `rls`, `rolesmig` sheets updated; regenerated, 0 errors.
- **Why:** The first draft of item (3) assumed `pdfkit` could embed GIF and placed PDF generation in the carrier package; the PR #6 code review proved both wrong and pointed to the official PDF path, which is preferable to hand-written bit-level code; the security reviews' deltas are tighter forms of rules the amendments already stated and were requested as text changes because security-analyst does not edit ADRs; DECISIONS.md is append-only, so the supersession is recorded here rather than by editing D-ARCH-057.
- **Alternatives:** keep the in-house decoder as the primary path (rejected: an official API exists; module boundary); put `recoverLabel` inside `createShipment` as a hidden second call (rejected: `createShipment` is non-retryable while recovery is idempotent; the saga's step 4 already owns label persistence and retries); treat PR7/PR8 deltas as TASK-016/TASK-013 acceptance only without ADR text (rejected: conformance reviews judge against the ADRs).
- **Reversibility:** High. Code change required: PR #6 — none from the architecture (the code review's M1 rewrite cites am. 11 item 3); PR #7 — none from the architecture; PR #8 — D-058 (b) + M1 + M2/PR8-SEC-01 + PR8-SEC-02/03 as routed by D-ORCH-011.

### D-059 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · Requirement-side alignment with the wave-0 batch-3 rulings (D-ARCH-057..062, ADR-0010 am. 11, ADR-0008 am. 2): Platform-side void when the carrier cannot void, capability guard on every declared-unsupported operation, `recoverLabel`, token-endpoint failure path, README "diffed by a person" field, no carrier push scheme this run; demo-critical impact confirmed none
- **Decision:** (1) **Cancellation when the adapter cannot void** (DHL Express `operations.cancelShipment = false`): REQ-BOOK-029 reads "void the carrier shipment where the adapter supports it (otherwise record a Platform-side void)"; outcome `carrier_void = UNSUPPORTED` (terminal, no retry, never EXCEPTION), pickup cancelled where one exists (REQ-PICK-008 unchanged), label VOIDED Platform-side at once (REQ-DOC-013), refund proceeds (DR-B21 extended; D-017 unchanged); REQ-BOOK-030 states that `CAPABILITY_NOT_SUPPORTED` is not a void failure. Customer-facing outcome identical to a confirmed void. US-196, US-215 (note), US-216, US-229 gain the scenario. (2) **Capability guard:** REQ-CARR-003 covers "an operation the adapter declares unsupported" (optional operations and `cancelShipment`); REQ-CARR-002 adds `operations.cancelShipment: boolean` and the SPK-02 seeds (DHL false; `recoverLabel` UPS/Mock only; `pushTracking` false ×3); REQ-CARR-001 lists the optional `recoverLabel`; REQ-CARR-017, DR-B32 and NFR-RES-003 put `recoverLabel` in the retryable set and the `transactional` pool (US-121 and US-130 AC). (3) **Token endpoint** (PR6-SEC-05/07): REQ-CARR-016 gains the failure-path sentence — negative cache `CARRIER_TOKEN_FAIL_TTL_S=60` on 400/401 failing fast with `AUTH_FAILED` and counting toward the breaker, per-carrier bucket `CARRIER_TOKEN_REQUESTS_PER_S` (FedEx 1/s burst 3), 403 → carrier-wide cool-down `CARRIER_TOKEN_PENALTY_COOLDOWN_S=600` failing fast with `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE` plus a security event, TTL from `expires_in` with skew/cap and uncacheable when absent (US-129 AC; SEC-TC-68). (4) **README field:** REQ-CARR-019 adds `Diffed against official spec by a person: yes (date, role) | no`, the spec commit SHA and the "PII and redaction" subsection; `no` allowed and reported under COND-SANDBOX-DEMO where it becomes a release condition (US-137 AC). (5) **Push tracking:** REQ-TRACK-006 notes that no official DHL/FedEx/UPS specification documents an authenticated push scheme, `pushTracking = false` for all three, verification is the Platform's HMAC shared-secret contract, an unconfigured carrier gets 401, and TASK-261 invents no carrier scheme (US-252 AC; live scenario conditioned on a documented scheme, skipped this run). (6) **DR-A07:** already "every role-assignable permission" since REQUIREMENTS 1.5 (D-058); no edit — the D-ARCH-059 hand-on is closed. (7) **UPS labels:** REQ-DOC-002 is satisfied as worded — UPS declares `labelFormats = [PDF_4X6, ZPL]` and the PDF arrives through `recoverLabel` (official Label Recovery API) in the saga's document step, "only that format is offered" applies (4x6 only); R-ARCH-25 noted in REQ-DOC-002 and US-222 (notes, recovery and recovery-failure scenarios). (8) **ASSUMPTIONS:** A-105 "DHL Express does not invoice a shipment never handed over" (Medium; COND-SANDBOX-DEMO cancel-and-check, M5 invoice reconciliation), A-106 UPS Label Recovery unverified in CIE (Medium; R-ARCH-25 contingency, last resort ZPL-only re-scope by product-manager), A-107 no push scheme (High). (9) **Demo-critical impact: none, confirmed.** Of the touched stories only US-121 is DC; its existing scenarios are unchanged and the added scenario is a no-I/O unit test. US-196/US-215/US-216/US-229/US-252 are Should non-DC; US-129/US-130/US-137/US-222 are Must non-DC and the M1 exit demo runs on Mock (`cancelShipment` true, `recoverLabel` true, both label formats returned by `createShipment`), so no demo path changes. Versions: REQUIREMENTS 1.6, PRD 1.6, NFR 1.5 (NFR-RES-003 pool list only); traceability.md untouched. No REQ/US IDs, priorities, milestones, DC tags or counts changed (434 / 91 / 293 / 134).
- **Why:** G2-style conformance reviews judge code against the REQs and the ADRs together, so a REQ that still says "void the carrier shipment" would make the accepted DHL design (D-ARCH-057) fail its own acceptance and would push TASK-247 toward a retrying saga behind an operation the carrier does not expose. The refund still proceeds because D-017's promise ("a mistake costs nothing") is the product commitment and the carrier-cost exposure is bounded and testable (A-105) rather than a reason to withhold money from the customer. `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE` for the 403 cool-down reuses the open-breaker code so callers and the saga need no new branch (a cool-down is an operator-side outage from the caller's view). `recoverLabel` is listed in the REQ interface because REQ-DOC-002 is Must and the only way UPS meets it is through that operation; leaving it ADR-only would leave a Must requirement satisfied by an undocumented contract.
- **Alternatives:** Withhold the refund or make DHL cancellation a staff action until A-105 is proven (rejected: breaks D-017 for one carrier, with no evidence of a charge; A-105's validation re-opens this on the first contrary invoice line); keep REQ-CARR-003 limited to optional operations and special-case `cancelShipment` in the saga (rejected: two guards for one behaviour); re-scope REQ-DOC-002 for UPS to ZPL now (rejected: the official spec documents PDF via Label Recovery; re-scope stays the last resort in A-106); invent a per-carrier push verifier from community examples (rejected: PR6-SEC-08 — nothing official to verify against).
- **Reversibility:** High (wording and assumptions; capability flags flip when a carrier documents an operation). Follow-ups: senior-developer briefs for TASK-075 (guard on `operations.cancelShipment`), TASK-131 (US-129 new scenarios), TASK-140/155 (US-222 recovery scenarios), TASK-191 (US-137 README line), TASK-247/252 (US-196/US-229 unsupported-void scenarios), TASK-261 (US-252 unconfigured-carrier scenario); qa-lead maps SEC-TC-68 to US-129.

### D-TASK013-02 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-013 fix cycle 1 (PR #8 @498e3a5)
- **Decision:** Released authz seed versions pinned by SHA-256 of raw bytes in `tools/authz/generate.ts` (`.gitattributes eol=lf`); `MAX_FULL_RENDER_VERSION = 1` (later versions throw until a delta renderer exists); `checkRoleInvariants` runs `checkCatalogue`; category/key exception map = `payment_method→payment`, `notification_pref→notification` only; `canAssignRole`/`ASSIGNABLE_BY` implemented in `packages/authz/src/assign.ts` (only an Owner assigns Owner) — PR8-SEC-06 closed here, consumed by TASK-231/235; `effectivePermissions` requires `roles`; D-058 grants applied (189 role-permission links; Super Admin 38).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK008-02 · 2026-11-12 · database-engineer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · SPK-03 fix cycle 1 (PR #7 @065aa3e)
- **Decision:** The (d) benchmark runs only with `SPK03_BENCH=1` and asserts the server-side `set_config` cost only; client-observed deltas are recorded. `setContext` sets all six data-model §4.1 settings; a negative control proves session-level `set_config(…, false)` leaks across pooled transactions while the transaction-local form does not; F-09 (55P03) is proven by test.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK009-02 · 2026-11-12 · backend-developer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · SPK-02 fix cycle 1 (PR #6 @f0387d4)
- **Decision:** SPK-02 §4 and the carrier READMEs point to ADR-0010 am. 11 (UPS PDF via Label Recovery = option (a)); every source pinned to a commit SHA or retrieval date; F11a–e URLs reconstructed by re-running the searches on 2026-11-12; FedEx PII field names remain unverified (portal access needed; COND-SANDBOX-DEMO). Each README carries "Diffed against official spec by a person: no".
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-063 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 ruling 7 (PR #7 round 2: code review R2-m1/R2-n1/R2-n2 (i), security review PR7-SEC-12 a/b/f): ADR-0005 am. 10 text corrections — the negative control proves a session-level `app.*` LEAKS (PostgreSQL cannot deny a USERSET placeholder), "(existing lint rule)" withdrawn, the spike's client-observed assertion is deleted and the (d) block is opt-in, C-1 line gains `SPK03_BENCH=1` + due before G3
- **Decision:** ADR-0005 am. 10 item 4 now reads: a session-scope negative control proves that `set_config('app.*', …, false)` by `platform_app` leaks to the next checkout on the same backend (adapter-pg releases without reset) and a later `withTenantTx` masks it only for its own transaction; the guards are the token ban + `prosrc` scan (item 1), the private client (item 2), the fresh-connection probe and the item-16 detector; C13 proves the deniable part (no `app.%` row in `pg_db_role_setting`/`pg_parameter_acl`). Item 2: "(lint rule added by TASK-016)" — no such rule exists on `main`. W-1/item 13: the client-observed assertion was deleted (recorded only), the whole (d) block incl. the server-side check is behind `SPK03_BENCH=1`, so TASK-016's hosted run sets the flag (else `SPK03_REPORT` has no `d_overhead_ms`), C-1 is due before G3, and the ported suite asserts the server-side p95 ≤ 1 ms without the flag (TASK-016 C-1 sheet line reworded, R2-m2).
- **Why:** Placeholder `app.*` GUCs are `USERSET`; `GRANT/REVOKE SET ON PARAMETER` has no effect on them, the `SET` statement cannot be revoked and `set_config` must stay executable for the transaction-local form — a TASK-016 developer following the old text would write a test that cannot pass or weaken it until it passes vacuously (CWE-668 residual documented instead). SPK-03 F-02/F-04 and the `518d281` test are the accurate record.
- **Alternatives:** keep the sentence and let TASK-016 discover it (rejected: the sheet inherited the ambiguity); drop the negative control (rejected: it documents the residual the static controls exist for).
- **Reversibility:** High (text).

### D-ARCH-064 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 ruling 8 (PR #7 R2-n2 ii/iii, PR7-SEC-12 c, PR7-SEC-06): `platform_app` has no role-level `statement_timeout` — a 30 s backstop is added in a new bootstrap file `02-role-backstops.sql`, and data-model §4.1 becomes the eight-setting context statement with `DB_TX_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS` / `DB_TX_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` / `DB_TX_IDLE_IN_TRANSACTION_TIMEOUT_MS`; TASK-016 gains the `pkg-config` and `lint` locks
- **Decision:** (a) `prisma/bootstrap/02-role-backstops.sql` (TASK-016): `ALTER ROLE platform_app SET statement_timeout = '30s'`, C13 row, shadow-DB rendering includes it; a new `NN-name` file because applied bootstrap files are immutable by ledger hash (data-model §8.1). (b) The context statement sets `statement_timeout`, `lock_timeout` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` per transaction (`set_config(…, true)`) from three new `packages/config` keys (defaults 5000 / 2000 / 15000 ms, `.env.example` parity); §4.1 and the §7.7 sample show eight settings; ADR-0005 am. 10 item 12 corrected ("`00-roles.sql` sets `search_path`, `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`, `lock_timeout` only"). (c) Readiness (TASK-006) compares `pg_db_role_setting` for `current_user` with one shared expected-set constant that TASK-016 extends; drift → not-ready + `db_role_setting_drift`. (d) TASK-016 locks + `pkg-config`, `lint` (no other holder in batch 0.5).
- **Why:** The spike baseline reads `statement_timeout = 0`, so the readiness probe and any system path outside `withTenantTx` had no timeout (T-83 pool exhaustion); the backstop closes that at zero code cost, while the per-transaction values are what make item 12's drift argument true (any role may `ALTER ROLE <self> SET` its own USERSET defaults, so role defaults cannot be relied on). The §4.1 list had drifted from item 12 (six vs eight settings); one statement is the single source.
- **Alternatives:** text-only correction requiring every system path to run the context statement (rejected alone: the readiness probe deliberately runs outside a tenant transaction); hard-code the three values in the factory (rejected: ADR-0018 "configuration over hard-coding"; the pool keys already live in `packages/config`).
- **Reversibility:** High (bootstrap file is additive; keys have defaults).

### D-ARCH-065 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 ruling 9 (PR7-SEC-11 LOW, PR7-SEC-12 d/e): the `pg_proc.prosrc` scan is by FORM across every non-system schema (`platform` included) and becomes migration-linter rule 16; rule 15 written into data-model §8.3; GUEST_CAPABLE trigger conditions use `IS DISTINCT FROM`; §4.5 `job_context` block rewritten under `SET ROLE platform_definer`
- **Decision:** ADR-0005 am. 10 item 1: C-series scans `prosrc` of every function in `public`, `platform` and any later schema; write forms (`set_config(` with an `app.` key, `SET`/`SET SESSION`/`SET LOCAL … app.`, `RESET`, `DISCARD`) are forbidden everywhere; reads of the four security keys only in the §4.2 helpers (by `regprocedure`); `app.correlation_id` reads allowed; the same rule is linter rule 16 for `prisma/migrations/**`; three negative fixtures. Rule 15 (GRANT/REVOKE after `OWNER TO platform_definer` fails; `SECURITY DEFINER` outside a `SET ROLE platform_definer` block flagged) is now in §8.3 with four fixtures; both owned by TASK-016. Item 11 / §4.3: the trigger raises when `OLD.org_id IS NOT NULL AND NEW.org_id IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.org_id` and when `NEW.guest_token_hash IS NOT NULL AND NEW.guest_token_hash IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.guest_token_hash` (`GUEST_DECLAIM_FORBIDDEN`, `23514` kept); TASK-051 tests the four forbidden transitions as the superuser. §4.5 `platform.job_context`: `SET ROLE platform_definer; CREATE FUNCTION …; REVOKE …; GRANT …; RESET ROLE;`.
- **Why:** "Non-platform functions" exempted exactly the schema that holds every `SECURITY DEFINER` function, where a `set_config('app.org_id', …)` would switch tenant mid-transaction (T-35) or leak session-wide; rule 15 was named by am. 10 item 9 but never listed in §8.3; the first trigger draft blocked org → NULL and NULL → hash only, not org A → B or H1 → H2, and only a superuser test proves the trigger independently of RLS; the `job_context` block was the one remaining example of the `OWNER TO`-before-`GRANT` order that fails for the `INHERIT FALSE` migrator.
- **Alternatives:** per-schema exemption list (rejected: form-based rules do not need maintenance when schemas are added); `42501` for the trigger as PR7-SEC-04 proposed (rejected: `mapDbError` keys the `rls_write_rejected` event on 42501 + message prefix; a distinct class avoids conflation).
- **Reversibility:** High (tests, text, migration form).

### D-ARCH-066 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 ruling 10 (PR7-SEC-13 INFO, PR7-SEC-14 INFO/optional): ADR-0005 am. 10 item 15 — state-transition `UPDATE`/`DELETE` assert the affected-row count (`expectAffected`); item 16 — the in-statement session-leak detector is adopted as BINDING (`MATERIALIZED` CTE reads prior `app.*` values in the same round trip; `DB_SESSION_CONTEXT_LEAK`, event, process not-ready)
- **Decision:** Item 15: F-02 ported as "INSERT: 42501; UPDATE/DELETE: 0 rows silently"; `expectAffected(result, 1)` in `apps/api/src/platform/db` (TASK-016) throws `NOT_FOUND` on 0 and `CONFLICT` on > 1; first consumer TASK-051's claim repository; ESLint flags unchecked `updateMany`/`deleteMany` results in repositories. Item 16: the §4.1 statement is `WITH prev AS MATERIALIZED (SELECT current_setting('app.org_id', true), …) SELECT prev.*, set_config(…) … FROM prev` via `$queryRaw`; any prior value other than NULL/`''` throws `DB_SESSION_CONTEXT_LEAK` before `fn`, emits `db_session_context_leak`, increments a counter and marks the process not-ready until restart; the ported negative control asserts the detector fires after `set_config('app.org_id', A, false)`.
- **Why:** An out-of-context UPDATE/DELETE commits silently with the business transaction (a lost label void or refund marker) — the row-count check is the same one optimistic concurrency needs. The detector is the only run-time control that sees a mid-life leak (the fresh-connection probe runs once per connection; the token ban and `prosrc` scan are static), costs no extra round trip (W-1 unchanged) and its test already exists in the spike; not-ready is the correct response because the leaked backend lives in the process-scoped pool and only recycling the process replaces every connection.
- **Alternatives:** leave PR7-SEC-14 optional (rejected: a cheap control for the core O3 property); heal with a session-level `RESET` inside the transaction (rejected: discarded by the rollback the throw causes, and it needs the banned token in product code); fail only the request and keep serving (rejected: every later checkout of that backend would read a foreign tenant's context).
- **Reversibility:** High (one statement and a helper).

### D-ARCH-067 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 ruling 11a (PR #6 security review PR6-SEC-11 LOW/MEDIUM-from-M3, PR6-SEC-12 g): ADR-0010 am. 11 item 5 — token-endpoint 403 handled by a static per-adapter `TOKEN_ENDPOINT_POLICY { on403 }` (FedEx `carrier-cooldown`, UPS `account-negative-cache`, DHL none); negative-cache fail-fast code is the canonical `AUTH_FAILED` (REQ-CARR-016 confirmed), cool-down fail-fast is `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE`; `CARRIER_TOKEN_BLOCKED_TTL_S = 600`; SEC-TC-68 gains the UPS case
- **Decision:** Each OAuth adapter exports `TOKEN_ENDPOINT_POLICY` next to `EGRESS`. FedEx `'carrier-cooldown'`: 403 → carrier-wide `CARRIER_TOKEN_PENALTY_COOLDOWN_S = 600` for all FedEx accounts on the egress, fail fast `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE`, no retries, one probe after, event `carrier_token_penalty`. UPS `'account-negative-cache'`: 403 = "Blocked Merchant" → that account's negative cache for `CARRIER_TOKEN_BLOCKED_TTL_S = 600`, fail fast `AUTH_FAILED`, its breaker opens, ops event; other UPS accounts unaffected, no carrier-wide key. DHL Express (HTTP Basic) declares no policy. 400/401 negative cache fails fast with `AUTH_FAILED` (+ `carrier_token_auth_failed`), strict `expires_in` parse, token bodies never logged or captured. SEC-TC-68 UPS case: "403 on account A leaves B and C working; no carrier-wide key". M3 re-check at COND-SANDBOX-DEMO records FedEx's penalty body code. Owners TASK-131 (cache + policy application, four new config keys), TASK-132 (gateway fail-fast order), TASK-139/140 (declarations). product-manager scopes the REQ-CARR-016 403 sentence per carrier.
- **Why:** The first text generalised FedEx's per-IP penalty: one blocked UPS account would have denied UPS to every account for 600 s and re-triggered on each probe (one carrier lost for all users; MEDIUM once partner-owned accounts exist, ADR-0011). `AUTH_FAILED` is a canonical DR-B30 carrier code meaning the carrier rejected our credentials, so REQ-CARR-016 was right and the ADR is aligned to it; an operator reading `GET /carriers/status` can then tell "fix the credentials" from "wait for the cool-down".
- **Alternatives:** classify by response body (rejected: FedEx's penalty body code is unverified until a live run); configuration-driven policy (rejected: the policy is part of the wire contract like `EGRESS`, changed by ADR amendment); `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE` for both (rejected: hides the actionable cause).
- **Reversibility:** High (static declaration per adapter).

### D-ARCH-068 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 ruling 11b (PR #6 code review R2-n1..n6 + R2.4, security review PR6-SEC-12 a–f/h, R2.4 inventory): ADR-0010 am. 11 items 3/4/6/8 corrected and items 10–11 added — npm RELEASE dates, canonical README heading "PII and redaction" + Docs URL SHA, path-segment-boundary matching with the UPS token path as an EXACT path and `rule.tier === ctx.environment`, route-template logging (never `url.full`), `carrierPath`/`carrierUrl` helper owned by TASK-132, per-carrier `sensitive-fields.ts` with whole-container redaction and the residual fields, UPS Label Recovery `Code` length quirk (R-ARCH-25 v1.5)
- **Decision:** (1) Item 3 / R-ARCH-25: release dates verified with `npm view <pkg> time` — `omggif` 1.0.10 2019-07-16, `gifwrap` 0.10.1 2022-03-17, `gifuct-js` 2.1.2 2021-11-04, `@jimp/gif` 0.22.12 2024-02-23, `@jimp/js-gif` 1.6.1 2026-04-07 (in-window, depends on the first two); conclusion unchanged. (2) Item 4: canonical heading `PII and redaction`; TASK-191 matches a heading that starts with it (case-insensitive, suffix allowed); Docs URL row carries `@ <sha>` for repository sources; PR #6 READMEs renamed by TASK-135/139/140. (3) Item 6: entries ending in `/` match at a segment boundary, entries without a trailing `/` are exact paths (UPS `/security/v1/oauth/token`; `tokenX` refused); `rule.tier === ctx.environment` per request adopted now; encoded separators refused; redirect hops re-checked; SEC-TC-66 cases listed; rules come from static `EGRESS` only. (4) Item 8: logs/spans carry `server.address`, method and route template, never `url.full`/`url.path`/`url.query`/`http.url`/`http.target` (TASK-113, TASK-214); bodies never logged; `carrierPath` (per-carrier identifier allow-pattern, throws with no I/O) and `carrierUrl` (origin/prefix escape check) in `packages/carriers/src/http/carrier-url.ts`, owner TASK-132, SEC-TC-67; batch tracking maps by requested number; per-carrier `sensitive-fields.ts`, whole party containers redacted, residual fields named (DHL `shipmentNotification[].receiverId`, `mobilePhone`, `customerReferences[].value`; UPS `FaxNumber`, `PhoneNumber`, `InternationalForms.Contacts` parties, `AlternateDeliveryAddress`; FedEx `child_id`, `phoneExtension`, `faxNumber`, notification `emailAddress`), completeness test; minimisation defaults incl. `LabelLinksIndicator` never set. (5) Item 10: Label Recovery `LabelImageFormat.Code` `minLength/maxLength 4` contradicts its three-character examples — TASK-140 does not validate the `Code` length against the published schema; settled in CIE. (6) Item 11 records the round-2 corrections. R2-n4/n5/n6 → TASK-135/139 sheets.
- **Why:** `time.modified` is registry metadata, not a release; a naive `startsWith` on a prefix without a trailing slash admits `/security/v1/oauth/tokenX`; `encodeURIComponent` leaves `.`/`..` unreserved, so a tracking number `..` would reach a different operation inside the allowed prefix; `url.full` with the query stripped still carries INT identifiers in paths; a helper in `apps/api` is unreachable from `packages/carriers`, and TASK-132 is the gateway task every adapter already depends on; a generic leaf deny-list would never match `receiverId`; a validator generated from the published schema would reject `PDF`.
- **Alternatives:** keep `url.full` minus query (rejected: PR6-SEC-12 c); one generic sensitive-field list (rejected: PascalCase/camelCase and carrier-specific keys); put `carrierPath` in TASK-075 (rejected: TASK-075 is the contract task; the helper needs the egress base shape from TASK-113, which TASK-132 depends on).
- **Reversibility:** High (text; helpers internal to `packages/carriers`).

### D-ARCH-069 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 ruling 12 (PR #8 round 2: code review R2-m1/R2-n1/R2-n2/R2-n3, security review PR8-SEC-05/06/07/10/13/14): ADR-0008 amendment 3 — Compliance Auditor narrowed to read ∪ {`audit:export`} ∪ selfService; `SUB_RESOURCES` middle-segment allow-list; TASK-020 owns both plus the PR8-SEC-05 route-binding merge condition (lock `pkg-authz`); PR8-SEC-06 → TASK-231/235, PR8-SEC-07 → TASK-060, PR8-SEC-10/14 → TASK-016/028, PR8-SEC-13 + CODEOWNERS → TASK-007 (fallback TASK-016)
- **Decision:** (1) Auditor invariant: every permission has verb `read`, or is exactly `audit:export`, or is `selfService`; further auditor-grantable `audit:<verb>`s are explicit ADR additions; destructive audit verbs never auditor-grantable; no grant change → no `CATALOGUE_VERSION` bump. (2) `SUB_RESOURCES = ['member', 'status']` + webhook providers; `checkKeyShape` rejects other three-segment keys whose qualifier is not a scope qualifier; P3/P3b as mutated-catalogue tests. (3) TASK-020 gains lock `pkg-authz` and files `packages/authz/src/{routes,guard}.ts`, `permissions/index.ts`; `DeclaredRoute` gains `tenancy`/`auth`; `findRouteBindingViolations` (a)–(d) is a merge condition. (4) PR8-SEC-06 (i)–(iv) + R2-n2 assigner-subset property test → TASK-231; UI consequences → TASK-235. (5) PR8-SEC-07 scope-filtered effective-permission query, `expectedScope` → TASK-060. (6) PR8-SEC-10: GLOBAL manifest + `grants.manifest` → TASK-016; classification INT except four PUB text columns → TASK-028. PR8-SEC-14: backstop keyed on the key rule + planted-row control → TASK-016. (7) PR8-SEC-13 / R2-n3: base-ref `tools/ci/check-migration-immutability.mjs` (A-only on `prisma/migrations`, two fixtures) + `.github/CODEOWNERS` (`packages/authz`, `tools/authz`, `prisma/migrations`, `prisma/bootstrap`, lint-control files) → TASK-007 preferred (in flight; orchestrator forwards), TASK-016 fallback.
- **Why:** P4 proved `audit:purge` on the Auditor passes the "any `audit:*`" rule — least privilege at the catalogue level is cheaper than relying on O4 immutability for a future verb; P3/P3b proved a verb hidden in the action segment classifies as read (same family as round-1 M1); TASK-013 is merged and TASK-020 is the next task on route ↔ permission binding, so grouping three small guard edits there avoids a fourth critical-path task; the seed pin lives beside the generator, so one PR can rewrite v1 and its pin together — only a base-ref CI check and CODEOWNERS stop that (LOW now, MEDIUM at M5).
- **Alternatives:** keep "any `audit:*`" until M2 (rejected: a one-line guard change now, with no grant change); a new Wave-0 task for the guard edits (rejected: critical-path cost); let `ASSIGNABLE_BY` stand without the subset property (rejected: a future grant change would silently let an Admin assign a role holding more than they do).
- **Reversibility:** High (guard code and CI tooling).

### D-ARCH-070 · 2026-11-12 · principal-architect · Wave-0 batch-3 Part 2: plan-sheet routing of every ROUTED-W0-B3 / ROUTED-0.2 acceptance item (22 `plan-data.mjs` blocks; regenerated with 0 errors; task IDs and dependencies unchanged; locks changed for TASK-016 (+`pkg-config`, `lint`) and TASK-020 (+`pkg-authz`) only); in-flight TASK-006 and TASK-007 sheets carry their items for the orchestrator to forward
- **Decision:** Blocks edited: `rls` (TASK-016), `route` (TASK-020), `ci-a` (TASK-007, in flight), `api` (TASK-006, in flight), `logging` (TASK-010), `rlsgen` (TASK-024), `redact` (TASK-028), `guestclaim` (TASK-051), `authzmw` (TASK-060), `ssrf` (TASK-113), `oauth` (TASK-131), `resilience` (TASK-132), `dhl` (TASK-135), `fedex` (TASK-139), `ups` (TASK-140), `rawpayload` (TASK-188), `fixtures` (TASK-191), `obs` (TASK-214), `rolesmig` (TASK-221), `members` (TASK-231), `teamui` (TASK-235), `push` (TASK-261) — acceptance lines cite the ADR item and the review finding; `x` refs carry the finding IDs so `task-map.csv` shows the routing. TASK-006 items: PR7-SEC-03 items 2 and 4, PR7-SEC-06 item 3 + PR7-SEC-02 item 2 (readiness drift and fresh-connection checks). TASK-007 items: PR8-SEC-13 immutability check, CODEOWNERS paths. The `rulings/wave-0-batch-3-rulings.md` Part 2 table lists every block.
- **Why:** The reviews' routed items were recorded only in D-ORCH-011 and the review files; a developer works from the sheet (R2-m2 showed the C-1 line missing `SPK03_BENCH=1`), and TASK-016 is next on the critical path. Keeping IDs and dependencies stable avoids re-planning; the two lock additions are required by the files those tasks now touch and conflict with nothing in their batches.
- **Alternatives:** separate tasks for the authz guard changes and the config keys (rejected: critical-path cost, batch-0.5/0.6 locks are free); leave in-flight sheets untouched and rely on the orchestrator's brief (rejected: the sheet is the record the reviewers check against).
- **Reversibility:** High (generated documents).

### D-060 · 2026-11-12 · product-manager · Requirement-side alignment with the wave-0 batch-3 Part 2 rulings (D-ARCH-067, D-ARCH-069; ADR-0010 am. 11 item 5(c), ADR-0008 am. 3 item 1): token-endpoint 403 scoped per carrier in REQ-CARR-016/DR-B32 and US-129; Compliance Auditor invariant narrowed to read ∪ `audit:export` ∪ self-service in REQ-RBAC-004/DR-A07 and US-066; demo-critical impact confirmed none
- **Decision:** (1) **REQ-CARR-016** no longer says "on a token-endpoint 403 apply a carrier-wide cool-down"; it says the 403 is handled by the adapter's static token-endpoint policy: FedEx — carrier-wide cool-down `CARRIER_TOKEN_PENALTY_COOLDOWN_S` (600 s), no FedEx token request, every FedEx account fails fast with `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE`, security event `carrier_token_penalty`, one probe after; UPS — that account only is negative-cached for `CARRIER_TOKEN_BLOCKED_TTL_S` (600 s; 403 = "Blocked Merchant"), fails fast with `AUTH_FAILED`, its breaker opens, ops event, every other UPS account unaffected and no carrier-wide key; DHL Express — no token endpoint, no policy. The existing 400/401 negative-cache wording (`AUTH_FAILED`, counts toward the breaker) is **confirmed unchanged** — the ADR was aligned to the REQ (PR6-SEC-12 g), not the reverse. The note records the error-code rule (`AUTH_FAILED` = "fix the credentials", `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE` = "wait", visible in the carrier status listing), that the policy is a static declaration and never configuration, and the M3 re-check at COND-SANDBOX-DEMO (record FedEx's penalty body code). DR-B32's token sentence carries the same per-carrier split. US-129: notes updated, the FedEx 403 scenario names the policy, the event and the single probe, and a UPS scenario is added ("403 on account A leaves accounts B and C working; no carrier-wide key" — the SEC-TC-68 UPS case). (2) **REQ-RBAC-004** Compliance Auditor clause reads "holds only read permissions (action `read`), `audit:export` and the self-service set"; the note records the invariant `verb = read` ∪ {`audit:export`} ∪ selfService, that any other non-read `audit:<verb>` on the Auditor fails CI naming role and permission, that a further auditor-grantable audit verb is an explicit ADR addition, and that no seeded grant changes (no `CATALOGUE_VERSION` bump). DR-A07 aligned. US-066: notes updated, the guard-invariant scenario reworded, a Scenario Outline rejects `audit:purge`, `audit:anchor` and `audit:manage` on the Auditor, and a positive scenario keeps `audit:read` + `audit:export`. (3) **Demo-critical impact: none, confirmed.** US-129 is Must non-DC (the M1 exit demo runs on Mock, which has no token endpoint). US-066 is DC, but its existing scenarios are untouched, the new scenarios are build-time guard tests, and no seeded grant changes, so the M0 demo path (seed, matrix, guard passing) is identical. (4) Versions: REQUIREMENTS 1.7, PRD 1.7; NFR.md stays 1.5 (no NFR carries the 403 sentence or the Auditor clause); traceability.md untouched. No REQ/US IDs, priorities, milestones, DC tags or counts changed (434 / 91 / 293 / 134). No new assumption: the per-carrier 403 semantics are the architect's ruling from the carriers' published documentation, and the FedEx body-code uncertainty is already tracked as the M3 re-check in D-ARCH-067.
- **Why:** The 1.6 wording generalised FedEx's per-IP penalty to every OAuth carrier; implemented literally (TASK-131) it would have blacked out UPS for every account for 600 s on one blocked merchant account and re-triggered on each probe — a one-carrier outage for all users, and MEDIUM once partner-owned accounts exist (ADR-0011). Conformance reviews judge TASK-131/132/139/140 against the REQ and the ADR together, so the REQ had to carry the same per-carrier split or the accepted design would fail its own acceptance. Keeping `AUTH_FAILED` for the credential paths and `CARRIER_UNAVAILABLE` for the penalty path gives operators the actionable distinction. For the Auditor, the P4 test in PR #8 proved "any `audit:*`" lets `audit:purge` through: least privilege at the catalogue level is a one-line guard change today, whereas relying on O4 immutability would make a future destructive audit verb a runtime question instead of a CI failure.
- **Alternatives:** Leave REQ-CARR-016 at "carrier-wide" and let the ADR carry the UPS exception (rejected: a Must REQ would contradict the accepted ADR and the TASK-131 acceptance lines); say "per carrier policy" without naming the policies (rejected: untestable — the UPS scenario needs the account-scoped behaviour stated); make the Auditor clause "read permissions and `audit:export`" without the self-service carve-out (rejected: contradicts D-058, under which every role holds the self-service set); list the Auditor's permitted audit verbs in the REQ as an open set (rejected: the ADR makes additions explicit by design).
- **Reversibility:** High (wording only; the FedEx/UPS policies are static adapter declarations changed by ADR amendment). Follow-ups: senior-developer briefs TASK-131 (US-129 FedEx/UPS scenarios, four config keys), TASK-139/140 (policy declarations) and TASK-020 (US-066 Auditor outline); qa-lead maps the SEC-TC-68 UPS case to US-129 and adopts the US-066 `audit:purge` outline as a guard test.

### D-TASK006-01 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-006 runtime skeleton decisions (PR #9 @374166d)
- **Decision:** (01) the worker imports apps/api only via `@platform/api/runtime` and `@platform/api/modules/<m>` (lint policy + probes); (02) config key holders refused in staging/production, warning in local while compose.env is shared (until TASK-014); (03) pool `statement_timeout` default 25 s, `query_timeout` = +1 s; (04) readiness uses the OpenAPI `HealthReady` names; `object_storage` and audit checks are pending stubs reporting `ok` until TASK-019/TASK-021 (reviewer focus; MEDIUM ops risk until then); (05) problem `type` base `https://docs.platform.invalid/problems/` until a docs-domain key exists; (06) session-integrity guard runs lazily from readiness, re-checks every 5 min, requires NULL `app.*` at start-up and accepts `''` later, emits `db_role_setting_drift` / `db_session_default_tampered`; (07) specialists ran on opus instead of the sheet's sonnet.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK007-01 · 2026-11-12 · devops-engineer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-007 lane A and supply-chain decisions (PR #10 @28442b6)
- **Decision:** (01) stale transitive dependencies are report-only (D-ARCH-003 rev 2/4); (02) a High with no available fix needs a dated waiver; (03) cooldown age = min(now, lockfile commit time) measured against the full packument; (04) PR5-SEC-04 security-review record rule, bootstrap `--report-only` until merged; (05) register dates; (06) osv-scanner v2.7.0 pinned; (07) fixture-folder ignore rules; (08) lane A commitlint gates the **PR title** (= squash-merge header) on `pull_request` and the pushed range on push to main; a `commit-msg` hook keeps per-commit linting locally; merges use "Squash and merge"; lane A does not re-run on title edits (no `edited` trigger, minutes budget); (09) `check-workflow-policy` asserts CODEOWNERS covers the sheet's path list. Lane A measured 2 m 36 s warm / 4 m 02 s cold (≈ 5–7 billable job-min per code PR).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-012 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · Squash-only merge discipline as a procedural control (PR #10 review M3)
- **Decision:** Changing the repository merge settings (disable merge commits/rebase merges; squash title = PR_TITLE) was denied by the session's safety policy, so it is NOT applied. Safe alternative applied: (1) the orchestrator merges every PR only with `gh pr merge <n> --squash --subject "<subject>"` (explicit subject, never the default), (2) before merging, the orchestrator lints that exact subject with the repo's commitlint config (`npm exec --no -- commitlint` on stdin) and re-checks that lane A is green on the exact head SHA (PR #10 review m6 — gating is procedural; no branch protection on this plan), (3) no agent other than the orchestrator merges PRs. TASK-007's D-TASK007-08 stays (lane A lints the PR title). The settings change remains recommended for the repository owner and is listed in the run summary.
- **Why:** Keeps the commit-history guarantee without modifying shared repository settings from an agent session.
- **Alternatives:** Apply the settings change (denied); rely on the PR-title check alone (rejected: titles edited after a green run are not re-checked).
- **Reversibility:** High.

Server-side `set_config` p95 asserted ≤ 1 ms in the permanent suite (measured 0.106–0.118 ms). **C-1 (hosted ubuntu-latest `SPK03_BENCH=1` measurement) is NOT done** — no hosted `security-integration` job exists yet; re-owned to **TASK-015** (which creates that job), due before G3; the spike file is deleted when the C-1 artifact is recorded. PR8-SEC-13 fallback not shipped (TASK-007/PR #10 owns it). Whichever of PR #9 (TASK-006) and PR #11 merges second adds `statement_timeout=30s` to `EXPECTED_ROLE_SETTINGS`, the drift assertion on the lock_timeout RESET test, and wires `createWithTenantTx` into the composition root. ### D-ORCH-013 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · PR #10 security rulings; merge procedure tightened (PR10-SEC-05)
- **Decision:** (1) PR10-SEC-02 rated LOW (private repo, single maintainer, tampering visible in diff; matches SEC-P3-31). (2) D-TASK007-01..09 accepted per security review (02 subject to a waiver-span cap). (3) Repository-setting hardening recommended by PR10-SEC-05 (`sha_pinning_required`, selected actions only, squash-only merges) is NOT applied from the agent session (the settings change was denied by the session safety policy, D-ORCH-012); it is listed for the repository owner in the run summary. ### D-ORCH-014 · 2026-11-12 · sdlc-orchestrator · Merge order #10 → #9 → #11 and the TASK-006/TASK-016 reconciliation owner
- **Decision:** PR #10 (TASK-007) merges first, then PR #9 (TASK-006), then PR #11 (TASK-016). PR #11 is therefore the second-merging PR and owns the full reconciliation list from the PR #11 code review §7 (a)–(f): (a) `EXPECTED_ROLE_SETTINGS` gains `statement_timeout=30s` and PR #9's pin test parses every bootstrap file; (b) the lock_timeout-RESET test asserts readiness drift; (c) composition-root wiring of `createWithTenantTx` (event sink, counter, `markNotReady` latch, fresh-connection probe first); (d) composition-root placement: adopt the reviewer's recommendation — a `platform/db/runtime.ts` inside the database platform exports the runtime DB wiring, so `platform/runtime/**` and health tests import it rather than `client.ts` (no boundary carve-out); (e) tenant-context-ban hits in `session-integrity.int.test.ts` and `problem-handler.api.test.ts` resolved without inline disables; (f) textual merges in `.env.example` and `packages/config/src/{index,schema}.ts`.

- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK006-02 · 2026-11-12 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-006 fix cycle 1 (PR #9 @05855b1)
- **Decision:** (1) D-TASK006-06 superseded: the session-integrity start-up check runs eagerly at boot on a dedicated `pg.Client` (api before `listen`, worker before its health server and processors), requires NULL there; pooled checks afterwards accept NULL or `''`; only passing results are cached (5 min); failures re-check every call; an unref'd 5-minute monitor re-checks without readiness traffic (PR9-SEC-05). (2) Pending audit readiness checks report `degraded` (HTTP 200) per OpenAPI `HealthReady`; a real stack's `/health/ready` reads `status: degraded` until TASK-019/TASK-021 land (tripwire tests enforce replacement). (3) pg TLS: every TLS sslmode is verify-full (`rejectUnauthorized: true`, `sslrootcert` → `ca`), `no-verify` refused, URL query stripped so URL/PGSSLMODE/NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED cannot override; `tlsVerificationGuard` exits 78 if `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED` ≠ 1 in staging/production. (4) JSON bodies UTF-8 only (415 otherwise); corrupt encodings → 400; problem responses `Cache-Control: no-store`. (5) Prisma children run via `npm exec --node-options=` with `NPM_CONFIG_NODE_OPTIONS` dropped; residual (other `.npmrc` settings such as `script-shell`) routed to TASK-007/tools-deps follow-up (spawn the Prisma CLI entry with `process.execPath`).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK007-02 · 2026-11-13 · devops-engineer / senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-007 fix cycle 1 (PR #10 @26fc08d)
- **Decision:** (10) A parallel isolated `scans` job (own runner, no npm/node_modules/workspace scripts/setup-node cache) runs gitleaks (only `.git` mounted, `--ignore-gitleaks-allow`, `--diff-merges=first-parent`, `--network none`), licences, signatures and audit before `deps:rebuild`; gate scripts come from the base ref (from head git objects only while the base lacks them — bootstrap). (11) `CI_REQUIRED_GATES` (20 gate steps + `ci-gate needs [fast-lane, scans]`) enforced by `check-workflow-policy`; draft PRs get a red `ci-gate`, not a skipped one; `ci-gate` runs on `!cancelled()` and fails if a lane was skipped; the separate `changes` job is folded into fast-lane (≈ 6 billable job-min per run). (12) Waivers require `granted` (not in the future), ≤ 90 days to `expires`, and a decision ID `^D-[A-Z0-9]+-\d+$`. (13) Security-review record contract once PR #10 merges: file `docs/05-quality/security/PR-<nnnn>-*.md` for that PR number; only the LAST `Verdict` row counts (PASS / PASS_WITH_FOLLOW-UPS / PASS_WITH_CONDITIONS); head SHAs read only from `Branch / head` and `Head` rows; `--no-renames`; main changes brought in by a merge do not invalidate a record; not run on push. Install-command detection covers every npm 11.19.1 install/rebuild alias and every `ignore-scripts` override form (drift test against installed npm). YAML subset parser rejects CR/NEL/U+2028/U+2029/C0/BOM.
- **Reversibility:** High.

CREATE EXTENSION works; RDS event-trigger behaviour confirmed by desk research (re-check at M5). Text-only amendments to data-model §7.3 (F-01, F-02) → architect before TASK-021. F-01..F-10, A-1..A-7 and RDS items routed to TASK-016/021/033/034/116, principal-architect and COND-G4-PROD per SPK-08 §7–§9. F-04: TASK-013's `INCLUDE` index makes every `migrate diff` report drift → TASK-016 / authz owner.

### D-ARCH-071 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · TASK-016 PR #11 rulings part 1 (PR decisions 3 and 4; code review §4 rulings 3–4, m4/m6; security review PR11-SEC-02/03/07, §5 rulings 1 and 4): migration files are NOT atomic → data-model §8.3 rule 5 verified result, rule 9 a/b, rule 11 session-form-only, rule 15 `SET ROLE … RESET ROLE` as the ONLY definer form (ADR-0005 am. 10 item 9 amended), rule 16 a/b (forms list, operator-atom exemption inside `CREATE POLICY`), new rule 17 (`down.sql` `IF EXISTS`), §8.4 prefix reversibility; `GRANT EXECUTE ON platform.app_actor_id() TO platform_definer` accepted (§4.4); R-ARCH-26
- **Decision:** (1) TASK-016's rule-5 probe is adopted as fact: Prisma 7.10.1 `migrate deploy` runs a file's statements one at a time outside any transaction block; a failure after statement k leaves 1..k applied with `finished_at` NULL. Rule 5 keeps "one `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` per file" for isolation, not for `25001`. Ordering principle written into §8.3: every prefix of a file leaves the schema safe (CREATE TABLE → ENABLE + FORCE → policies → GRANT; definer functions inside one `SET ROLE` block; data after structure; no file ends inside `SET ROLE`). (2) Linter: rule 11 accepts `SET [SESSION] lock_timeout` only (`SET LOCAL` is a warning-only no-op outside a transaction block) and requires `RESET lock_timeout` when a file raises the value above the migrator role default; rule 9 a requires every `CREATE POLICY` in `prisma/migrations/**` to equal the renderer output for its table (no exemption file) and rule 9 b requires `GRANT … TO platform_app` on an RLS-class table after ENABLE + FORCE (+ policies) in the same file; rule 15 accepts only the `SET ROLE platform_definer; … RESET ROLE;` block — the "grants before `OWNER TO`" alternative of ADR-0005 am. 10 item 9 is withdrawn, `ALTER … OWNER TO platform_definer` in migrations fails, an unreset definer role at end of file fails, the linter's `handedOver` branch is removed (fixture `definer-grants-before-owner-to` flips to failing; new `definer-role-not-reset`, `set-local-lock-timeout`, `grant-before-force`); rule 16 a reads "any write form" literally with one pattern list shared by `scans.ts` and the linter (concatenated/quoted keys, `format('%I')`, spaced/quoted GUC names, dynamic `current_setting(k)`; every evaded form becomes an executed C-series fixture) and rule 16 b exempts exactly the normalised operator atom only inside `CREATE POLICY`; new rule 17: `down.sql` carries `IF EXISTS` on every DROP form. (3) §8.4: a failed deploy is a prefix; `down.sql` → `migrate resolve --rolled-back` (legitimate for a FAILED migration) → redeploy; the up→down→up driver gains a prefix-reversibility check (owner TASK-024). (4) `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION platform.app_actor_id() TO platform_definer`, issued by the migrator before `SET ROLE` in the `app_is_operator` migration (template `app-is-operator.sql`), is accepted and recorded in §4.4; C11 expects the extra grantee once TASK-037 applies the template. (5) risks.md v1.6 adds R-ARCH-26.
- **Why:** The reviews proved that three linter assumptions rested on file atomicity: a rule-11-compliant `SET LOCAL` left `ALTER TABLE` with no lock timeout (NFR-AVAIL-003 pile-up), the accepted "grants before OWNER TO" form could leave a migrator-owned SECURITY DEFINER function executable by `platform_app` as a durable state (the migrator owns the SYSTEM/GLOBAL tables without RLS), and a grant placed before FORCE could leave a granted table without RLS. The renderer's own operator policy failed rule 16 (a false positive that would block TASK-037's first migration), while a hand-written tautology passed rule 9 and C3 — so the allow-list must be enforced in the linter and the catalog, not only in the renderer. The helper grant is needed because `PUBLIC` holds no EXECUTE on the helpers and the definer body calls `app_actor_id()`.
- **Alternatives:** keep both definer forms and only add the end-of-file check (rejected: the window is real and durable); wrap each migration in an explicit transaction (rejected: `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` cannot run inside one, and Prisma does not offer per-file transactions); loosen rule 16 for all policy statements (rejected: opens the raw `current_setting` hole PR7-SEC-07 closed); a SHA-keyed exemption file for policies (rejected here, see D-ARCH-072).
- **Reversibility:** High (linter rules, fixtures, text); the helper grant is one revocable statement.

### D-ARCH-072 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · TASK-016 PR #11 rulings part 2 (PR decision 5; code review §4 ruling 5; security review PR11-SEC-10 requirements 1–5, §5 ruling 5): DR-A14 special policies for `users`/`memberships`/`organisations` are three NAMED RENDERER TEMPLATES selected by the manifest field `policySet`, with positive status lists (`IN ('INVITED','ACTIVE','SUSPENDED')` replaces `<> 'REMOVED'`) and exact `EXISTS` atoms; C3 derives the expected set from manifests + renderer and asserts keyed atoms and recursion invariants; tooling owner TASK-024, application TASK-037 (memberships, organisations, `app_is_operator`) and TASK-049 (users); security-analyst sign-off is a merge condition of both
- **Decision:** `tools/db/table-classes.ts` gains `policySet: 'dr-a14-users' | 'dr-a14-memberships' | 'dr-a14-organisations'`, accepted only on the table of the same name and class (`users` USER_OWNED, `memberships` TENANT, `organisations` TENANT). `blocksFor` renders `enable-force` + the set (replacing `user-owned` for users and `tenant` for organisations — neither has the keyed column; adding `own_read` to `tenant` for memberships) + `operator` (+ `definer-read`). `validateTemplate` admits, for these templates only, the exact atoms `id = platform.app_actor_id()` (users), `id = platform.app_org_id()` (organisations) and the two exact `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.memberships m WHERE …)` atoms; every forbidden token stays forbidden inside the EXISTS; every context reference inside it is `col = platform.app_*()`; `IN (…)` only on `m.status` and only positive. `p_users__org_member_read` uses `m.status IN ('INVITED','ACTIVE','SUSPENDED')`; `p_organisations__member_read` keeps `IN ('ACTIVE','SUSPENDED')`. Rule 9 a compares every `CREATE POLICY` with the renderer output; C3 renders every manifest entry on a rolled-back probe, deparses it and requires the live `pg_policies` rows to equal it (the hand-edited snapshot is retired), asserts one of the four keyed helpers in every `TO platform_app` predicate, and asserts that `memberships` policies contain no subquery and `users`/`organisations` subqueries reference `public.memberships` only. No exemption file exists. TASK-024 ships the templates, `policySet`, rule 9 a, rule 17, C3 derivation and the C11 converse (locks + `prisma-rls`, `prisma-core`); TASK-037 depends on TASK-024 and applies the memberships/organisations sets with the `app_is_operator` template; TASK-049 applies the users set; both carry a security-analyst sign-off merge condition and a prerequisites check (BLOCKED, never a local linter loosening, if the tooling is missing). data-model §4.3 and ADR-0005 am. 10 item 7 (round-3 note) record it; §12 A19.
- **Why:** The renderer correctly refused the hand-written shapes. An exemption "by policy name" would have permanently removed the three tables from C3 and from rule 9 and let later predicate edits go unchecked (PR11-SEC-10); a SHA-keyed exemption file would work but introduces a second reviewed source of truth next to the renderer, which already is the reviewed source. `<> 'REMOVED'` was fail-open on enum growth: any future `membership_status` value would have made a user's row readable by fellow members by default. Putting the tooling on TASK-024 (database-engineer, batch 0.7, between TASK-016 and TASK-037) keeps the DC module task TASK-037 free of renderer/linter/catalog work and lands the tooling one wave earlier; a new `late` task was possible without renumbering but would have disturbed the orchestrator's dispatch state.
- **Alternatives:** SHA-256-keyed exemption file read by rule 9 and C3 under CODEOWNERS (rejected: second source of truth); new table classes `IDENTITY_USER`/`MEMBERSHIP`/`ORGANISATION` (rejected: the tables are TENANT/USER_OWNED in every other respect — grants, C1/C2, definer flags — and a class would duplicate that); generic grammar support for `EXISTS` (rejected: opens arbitrary subqueries; exact strings are reviewable); putting the templates on TASK-037 (rejected: load and lock spread on a DC task); `('ACTIVE','SUSPENDED')` for users too (rejected: changes the member-list semantics the original predicate intended; INVITED rows are non-removed memberships of existing users).
- **Reversibility:** High (templates and manifest field; policies are migrations).

### D-ARCH-073 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · TASK-016 PR #11 rulings part 3 (security review PR11-SEC-06, PR11-SEC-11 a–h; code review n2): §4.2 helper bodies qualified (`pg_catalog.current_setting`, `OPERATOR(pg_catalog.~*)`, `pg_catalog.decode`, `::pg_catalog.uuid`) and adopted in the UNMERGED helpers migration; `lc_messages = 'C'` pinned for `platform_app` in the unmerged `02-role-backstops.sql` (five C13 rows; fallback `03-role-locale.sql` by TASK-021); C13 case folding + `proconfig ILIKE 'app.%'`; C11 converse ownership set (TASK-024); trigger-event names (TASK-021/051); `readSecret` open-once (PR #11 or TASK-034); C1/rule 9 schema scope and `runtimeReadOnly` flag (TASK-024)
- **Decision:** (a) The plain SQL helpers qualify every built-in and operator; PR #11 amends `20261112020000_platform_rls_helpers` before merge (unmerged → free; afterwards an immutable-migration follow-up). (b) `ALTER ROLE platform_app SET lc_messages = 'C'` joins the role backstops: `mapDbError` distinguishes an RLS `WITH CHECK` rejection (403 `AUTHZ_DENIED`) from a privilege error (500) by the English message prefix, so a non-English server would silently downgrade every RLS rejection to 500; C13 expects five `platform_app` rows and `EXPECTED_ROLE_SETTINGS` gains the row (already reconciled under D-ORCH-014 (a)); SPK-08 records whether the RDS master may set a `SUSET` parameter per role. (c) C13 compares `lower(…) LIKE 'app.%'` and asserts no `pg_proc.proconfig` entry `ILIKE 'app.%'`. (d) C11 asserts `platform_definer` owns exactly the allow-listed definer functions and no other function, relation, type or schema (P3 §15 deviation 7 condition, D-ORCH-005 (7)) with planted view/invoker-function controls — TASK-024. (e) Items c, d, e, f, g routed as in `rulings/wave-0-task-016-rulings.md`.
- **Why:** Inlined SQL helpers resolve names through the caller's `search_path`; qualification is free defence in depth and does not stop inlining. The locale dependency is a correctness dependency of the 403/500 split, cheaper to pin than to document. The converse ownership assertion was the explicit condition under which deviation 7 (`platform_definer` holds `CREATE` on `platform`) was accepted: a definer-owned view in `platform` would read every tenant's rows under `USING (true)`.
- **Alternatives:** document the `lc_messages` dependency only (rejected: silent fail-safe downgrade of a security signal); set `lc_messages` in the pool startup parameters instead of the role (rejected: non-pool sessions would differ, and C13 can assert a role row); qualify the helpers in a later `CREATE OR REPLACE` migration (rejected: the migration is still unmerged).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-074 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · TASK-016 PR #11 rulings part 4: plan-sheet routing (12 `plan-data.mjs` blocks; regenerated 0 errors / 0 warnings; task IDs and titles unchanged; dependency change TASK-037 (+TASK-024) only; locks TASK-020 (+`lint`), TASK-024 (+`prisma-rls`, `prisma-core`); `part: US-012` on TASK-019/021) — T-83 lint owner TASK-020; C6/C7/C14 → TASK-021 (C7 via manifest flag `immutable`), C8/C9 → PR #11 else TASK-024, C10 → TASK-028; PR11-SEC-09 items 1–6 + D-ORCH-014 (a)–(f) on TASK-016 as merge conditions, verified at TASK-020's `tx` slot; TASK-025/043 SYSTEM writers take `TenantDb` only; TASK-019/021 replace the readiness stubs (PR #9 m2); TASK-015 carries C-1 with `SPK03_BENCH=1` + spike-file and lint-exemption deletion in one commit; platform.md statement_timeout layering (PR #9 n3)
- **Decision:** Blocks edited: `lanec` (TASK-015), `rls` (TASK-016), `spk05` (TASK-019), `route` (TASK-020), `auditdb` (TASK-021), `rlsgen` (TASK-024), `auditcore` (TASK-025), `redact` (TASK-028), `orgs` (TASK-037), `outbox` (TASK-043), `register` (TASK-049), `ssrf` (TASK-113); every line cites the review finding and the decision, and the `x` refs carry the finding IDs so `task-map.csv` shows the routing. T-83: ESLint rule `platform/no-external-calls-in-tenant-tx` (lexical inside the `withTenantTx` callback: `fetch`, imports from `undici`/`node:http(s)`/`bullmq`/`ioredis`/`@aws-sdk/*`/`stripe`/`nodemailer`, configurable member-call names, `setTimeout` sleeps) owned by TASK-020, list extended by TASK-113; a Semgrep twin is optional. platform.md's RDS paragraph now describes the three statement_timeout layers (role backstop 30 s outside the pool; pool startup 25 s `DB_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS`; per-transaction 5 s `DB_TX_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS`). TASK-037's dod flags the `memberships.user_id → users(id)` FK ordering (TASK-049 creates `users` three batches later) for senior-developer's dispatch decision.
- **Why:** A developer works from the sheet, and the orchestrator's D-ORCH-014 made PR #11 the reconciler: the wiring list existed only in DECISIONS and the review. T-83 belongs where the only per-request `withTenantTx` entry is created and before wave-1 modules can violate it; TASK-113 would gate too late. The C6–C10 omissions were silent in traceability ("C1–C12 green"), so each check now has a named owner and the suite header must list deferrals. The readiness stubs would otherwise have let US-012 (DC) read as complete on fakes.
- **Alternatives:** a new `late` task for the RLS tooling (rejected: dispatch-state churn; TASK-024 is the right specialist and batch); T-83 as review-only (rejected: the threat model's mitigation says lint and a lexical rule is cheap); leave readiness stubs tracked only in the review (rejected: PR #9 m2 asked for sheet lines).
- **Reversibility:** High (generated documents).

### D-ORCH-016 · 2026-11-13 · sdlc-orchestrator · Lane A live; security-record workflow; PR #13 rulings and routing

From now on each PR that changes security-owned paths needs its security record `docs/05-quality/security/PR-<nnnn>-*.md` ON THE PR BRANCH, naming the head in a `Head`/`Branch / head` row with the last `Verdict` row PASS*; the orchestrator (or the fix-cycle developer) copies the committed record from main onto the branch, then lane A must be green on that exact head before `gh pr merge --squash --match-head-commit`. (4) US-049 AT-02/05/09/10 re-scope → product-manager (code review M1).
