### D-061 · 2026-11-13 · product-manager · US-049 delivery split (PR #13 code review M1, D-ORCH-016 (4)): TASK-023 delivers the framework (AT-US-049-01/03/04/06/07/08), TASK-014 owns AT-US-049-02 (Compose runs the seed), TASK-089 owns AT-US-049-05/09/10 (users provisioned; staging set-password link in Mailpit); US-049 stays Must and demo-critical, no story is split or deferred
- **Decision:** (1) US-049 is **not** completed by TASK-023. The plan sheets change from "TASK-023 completes US-049" to "TASK-023 partial; completed by TASK-014, TASK-089" (CLAUDE.md §7 step 3 form, applied to task ownership only — the story's MoSCoW, milestone and DC tag are unchanged). (2) Scenario ownership: AT-US-049-01, -03, -04 (×3), -06, -07, -08 → TASK-023 (met at framework level, PR #13; AT-01/-08 on probe tables is accepted for a framework task, the first real dataset is TASK-026's countries). AT-US-049-02 → **TASK-014**: the Compose seed one-shot runs `db:seed` then `db:seed:demo` after `migrate`, with `NODE_ENV=development` and `APP_ENV=local` (D-TASK023-01 (02) refuses `NODE_ENV=production` with a local/test `APP_ENV`; code review n7), and the test asserts that after `docker compose up --wait` every reference dataset registered at that time is present with one `reference_dataset_versions` row each; the "demo data are present" clause of AT-02 closes with TASK-089 together with US-003 AT-01, exactly as US-003 is already split between the two tasks. AT-US-049-05, -09, -10-1, -10-2 → **TASK-089**: the real `DemoUserProvisioner` replaces TASK-023's `DEFERRED_PROVISIONER` (D-TASK023-01 (04), accepted as temporary in D-ORCH-016 (2) and gated by PR13-SEC-02); local/test create users with the seeded passwords and send no link; staging creates staff without a password and sends one SET_PASSWORD link per staff user through the reset flow, visible in Mailpit, having read no `SEED_*` variable; the summary reports `deferred = 0`; no password or token value in stdout or logs (TASK-023's fast-check secrecy properties are extended to the real provider). The TASK-023 test that asserts `created: 0, deferred: 14` is replaced by TASK-089, not kept alongside. (3) Traceability: REQ-DATA-001/002 carry Task(s) `TASK-023, TASK-014, TASK-089` with status "Planned (partial: TASK-023 framework; completes with TASK-014 (AT-02) and TASK-089 (AT-05/09/10))"; US-049 reads as Implemented only when all three are merged. (4) PR #13 body: AT-05 and AT-10 relabelled **Partial** (the orchestrator or fix-cycle developer edits the body; the test titles already say "deferred until TASK-089"). (5) Demo coverage confirmed: PRD §6.1 Background ("seeds have run") and the two sign-in steps need AT-02 and AT-05/-10 end to end; TASK-089 (batch 2.4) precedes TASK-121 M0 exit-demo journeys (batch 3.7, depends on TASK-089), so R0 still demonstrates M0. PRD §6.2 does not depend on the demo users (the guest registers); its seed dependencies (Mock services, pricing, packs) are other EPIC-17 stories. No PRD, REQUIREMENTS or NFR text changes; counts 434 / 91 / 293 / DC 134 unchanged.
- **Why:** The framework is real (idempotency proven by `xmin`, refusals before connecting, secrecy properties), but four of the ten scenarios describe *users existing* and *Compose running the seed*, which the framework cannot do before TASK-089 (users table, bcrypt, reset flow — TASK-037/049/079/080) and TASK-014 (Compose skeleton) exist. Marking US-049 complete would have let a DC story read as done while the §6.1 sign-in steps had no seeded users behind them — the silent-drop the review flagged. Splitting the story into a new Should (ladder rung 4) is wrong here: nothing is being deferred, every scenario is still Must and still lands in R0; only the owning task changes. TASK-014 rather than TASK-026 for AT-02 because the seed job, its env and its image are TASK-014's Compose artefacts, TASK-014 is not yet dispatched (its sheet can still absorb the line), review n7 already sits there, and TASK-026 depends on TASK-014 so it re-proves the chain on real data without owning the scenario. Keeping AT-05/-09/-10 on TASK-089 instead of pulling user provisioning into PR #13 keeps the `prisma-seed` lock and PR #13 small, and respects the dependency order (users do not exist in batch 0.7).
- **Alternatives:** Keep "TASK-023 completes US-049" and rely on the PR body (rejected: traceability and the gate read the sheets, not PR bodies). Pull a minimal user provisioner into PR #13 (rejected: `users` lands in TASK-049, batch 1; it would couple a wave-0 PR to wave-1 schema). Ladder rung 4 — split US-049 into a smaller DC Must plus a Should (rejected: no scope is leaving R0; a Should would wrongly signal that user provisioning may slip). AT-02 on TASK-026 (rejected: TASK-026 is a consumer of the Compose seed job, not its owner; it already carries three partial stories). AT-02 wholly on TASK-089 (rejected: the mechanism would then be unverified for two batches while TASK-026 and every wave-1 seed task use it).
- **Reversibility:** High (sheet lines, a story note, two traceability rows; `plan-data.mjs` folded by the architect batch).

### D-TASK006-03 · 2026-11-13 · senior-developer (logged by orchestrator) · TASK-006 fix cycle 2 (PR #9 @6331aeb)
- **Decision:** (1) JSON Content-Type check: linear grammar (whitespace after `;` inside the optional parameter group), `MAX_CONTENT_TYPE_LENGTH = 256` → 415 before any regex, bounded check runs before `req.is` (closes R2-B1 / PR9-SEC-14 HIGH; measured 9.6 s → ≤ 0.16 ms). (2) Refines D-TASK006-02 item 3: single `RUNTIME_DATABASE_URL_PARAMS = {sslmode, sslrootcert, sslnegotiation}` exported from `@platform/config`; `DATABASE_URL` no longer accepts `application_name`, `connect_timeout` or `uselibpqcompat` in ANY environment; unlisted/repeated params → `invalid / databaseUrl.queryParameter` (exit 78); staging/production additionally require exactly one `sslmode=verify-full`. (3) `sslrootcert` must be an absolute path to a regular file ≤ 1 MiB; `system` refused. (4) Readiness stubs branded via `isPendingCheck` WeakSet; tripwires assert replacement implies no stub.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-SEC-PR12-01 · 2026-11-13 · security-analyst (logged by orchestrator) · SPK-08 security rulings

### D-ORCH-017 · 2026-11-13 · sdlc-orchestrator · PR #13 gitleaks hit on a pushed synthetic test literal: supersede the branch instead of allowlisting
- **Decision:** Commit `e6ccf60` on PR #13 contains a synthetic test literal (a synthetic 13-character test password literal assigned to a PASSWORD constant) that lane A's gitleaks flags (`generic-api-key`); it was replaced in `5fcf177` but stays in the PR's commit range, and history is never rewritten. Instead of adding a commit-scoped allowlist entry to `.gitleaks.toml` on main (weakens the control, needs its own reviewed PR), PR #13's reconciliation pass (after PR #11 merges, D-ORCH-015) creates a NEW branch from main containing the final reviewed tree as fresh commits (no `e6ccf60` in its range), opens a superseding PR that links #13, and closes #13 unmerged. Round-2 reviews of PR #13 happen now by SHA; the superseding PR gets a delta check proving its tree equals the reviewed tree plus the reconciliation commits, and its own security record per D-ORCH-016. D-TASK023-01 item 05 is superseded (copied `readSecret` removed); D-TASK023-03 amended: `CONFIG_CHANGE` (drift-restored) also fires on an audited drift restore.
- **Why:** Keeps the secret-scanning control unweakened and auditable; no force push or history rewrite.
- **Alternatives:** Narrow commit-scoped allowlist (rejected: permanent exception in a security control for a test artefact).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-SEC-PR13-02 · 2026-11-13 · security-analyst (logged by orchestrator) · PR #13 gitleaks hit is a false positive; D-ORCH-017 endorsed; literal scrubbed from docs
- **Decision:** (1) The gitleaks `generic-api-key` hit in PR #13 commit `e6ccf60` is a synthetic test value (CWE-798 does not apply; nothing to rotate). (2) D-ORCH-017 endorsed; rejected alternatives: allowlist entry, `.gitleaksignore`, inline allow marker, merging with red scans, branch rewrite. The superseding PR must: not have `e6ccf60` or `5fcf177` as ancestors; differ in tree from `e42e368` only by the reconciliation changes (+ code-review R2-m1/R2-n1, optional PR13-SEC-10); leave `.gitleaks.toml`/`.gitleaksignore` unchanged; be green on lane A; carry a record naming the new head. (3) PR13-SEC-11 (process): the literal was quoted verbatim in D-ORCH-017, a code-review record and a senior-developer memory file on main, which would turn lane A gitleaks red for every PR that merges main. Remediated by the orchestrator in place on 2026-11-13 (the only edit made inside an existing DECISIONS entry; wording replaced by a description, no meaning changed); `git grep -nE "pw-[0-9]{10}"` on main is empty. Rule going forward: records never quote secret-shaped literals, even synthetic ones. (4) PR13-SEC-09 (seed TLS value rules: `sslmode=no-verify`, `PGSSLMODE`, unchecked `sslrootcert`, `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED`) → TASK-014 (move `tlsConfig`/`readRootCertificate`/`withoutQuery` into `@platform/config`; `checkToolingEnv`).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-018 · 2026-11-13 · sdlc-orchestrator · PR #11 lane A blockers: supersede branch (gitleaks) + base-ref fixture "no-weakening" semantics fix on main
- **Decision:** (1) Gitleaks: PR #11's pushed merge commit `d68898a` carries main's since-scrubbed literal (PR13-SEC-11). Apply the D-ORCH-017 pattern: after reviews pass, create a fresh branch from current (clean) main, apply PR #11's net change (`git diff 63accea 37a2109`) as fresh commits, open a superseding PR linking #11, close #11 unmerged; security writes a supersede round naming the new head. No allowlist. The same applies to PR #4 if its push carries the literal. (2) The lane A step "Lint negative fixtures from the base ref against the head configs (PR2-SEC-08)" asserts an exact finding set, so ANY PR that adds a lint rule fails it — that is a defect in the no-weakening check, not a weakening. Fix on main first via a small devops PR (TASK-007 follow-up, CODEOWNED, reviewed by code-reviewer + security-analyst): the base run uses subset semantics (every base-expected finding must still fire; extra findings from new head rules are allowed) and skips the head-only completeness check ("every platform/* rule has a fixture"); the head run keeps exact semantics. No one-time exception, no skipped required step. (3) PR #11 keeps fix-cycle count 2 (supersede and the CI-harness fix are not fix cycles).
- **Why:** Required CI steps stay green and meaningful; the secret-scanning control is not weakened.
- **Alternatives:** One-time exception / merge with red fast-lane (rejected: breaks "merges require passing CI"); allowlist `d68898a` (rejected, D-SEC-PR13-02).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK007-03 · 2026-11-13 · devops-engineer (logged by orchestrator) · Base-ref lint fixture step: no-weakening subset semantics (PR #17 @ea78d38)
- **Decision:** (1) The base suite owns what its mode skips: the head-controlled `--testNamePattern` filter is removed and the required gate forbids test filters; mode set only by the step's own `env: LINT_FIXTURE_MODE: no-weakening` (unknown values fail the suite load; new workflow-policy rule `lint-fixture-mode` forbids the variable anywhere else, incl. `$GITHUB_ENV`). (2) In no-weakening mode every base-expected finding must still fire with the same rule id, `error` severity and message fragment; extra findings (new rules, known rules firing more widely, directive reports) are listed in the log/job summary tagged new/known and do not fail. (3) The head run inside `test:unit` keeps exact semantics, incl. the lint-script wiring test. Residual (LOW, security-analyst): the gate checks presence, not integrity (a PR's ci.yml could add `exit 0`); `/.github/` CODEOWNED + security record is the backstop.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-019 · 2026-11-13 · sdlc-orchestrator · PR #4 Firefox e2e evidence deferred to the hosted Linux lane; project naming; WebKit Tab deviation
- **Decision:** (1) On this Windows host the Firefox browser install is blocked (`EPERM` on `firefox.exe`, 3/3 attempts, not bypassed). PR #4's Playwright harness defines all six projects; 4/6 (chromium/webkit × mobile/desktop) are green locally (52/52). TASK-004's 3-engine × 2-viewport acceptance is accepted for merge with condition **COND-E2E-FF**: the firefox-mobile/firefox-desktop projects must run green on a Linux runner (lane C, TASK-015) before G3; owner TASK-015/qa-lead. (2) Project names follow TEST-STRATEGY §7.8 / required-suites.json (`<engine>-mobile/-desktop`), not `-360/-1280`. (3) WebKit-on-Windows `tabForward` focuses the first tabbable element instead of pressing Tab (WebKit Windows Tab leaves the page); Chromium/Firefox press real Tab — accepted pending code review. (4) Orchestrator error noted: the cooldown epoch given in the dispatch brief was one year early (2025); the developer computed the correct value and waited — no bypass occurred. Future briefs state ISO timestamps only.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-TASK007-04 · 2026-11-13 · devops-engineer (logged by orchestrator) · Amends D-TASK007-03 — base-ref fixture step pinned (PR #17 @8dd1d24)
- **Decision:** (1) The base-ref step runs under a base-sourced data-only Jest config (`tools/lint/jest.base-fixtures.config.json`), removing every head `jest.config.ts` lever (filters, passWithNoTests, moduleNameMapper, setup files). (2) The step's command lines are pinned exactly (`BASE_LINT_FIXTURES_SCRIPT`); required gates reject `shell:`, `working-directory:`, extra step `env:` and `defaults.run`; changing the step is a two-step change (update the pin on main first). (3) In no-weakening mode any unmatched finding WITHOUT a rule id (unused disable directive, parse error, ignored file) fails; clean fixtures run in both modes; `noWeakening` takes a minimum severity per case. (4) Root `.swcrc` deleted; any `.swcrc` is a policy finding; `@swc/jest` options inline with `swcrc: false`; `/.swcrc` and `**/.swcrc` CODEOWNED (PR17-SEC-01). (5) Policy tree walks skip nested checkouts with their own `.git`. Residual (replaces D-TASK007-03's): the base run is pinned (commands, env, suite, harness, modes, Jest config) and remains an accident guard (PR10-SEC-02): head code running earlier in fast-lane, job-level env, and owned configuration (`tools/deps/npm-cli.mjs`, lint configs/rules, lockfile) can still change its outcome; `lint-fixture-mode` matches literal and quote/continuation-split mentions only.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-020 · 2026-11-13 · sdlc-orchestrator · Merge order PR #17 → PR #11 (supersede) → PR #4 → PR #13 (supersede)
- **Decision:** PR #17 (CI fixture-step fix) merges first because it unblocks the critical-path PR #11 supersede branch. PR #4 then performs fix cycle 2 (code review R3-M1: CODEOWNERS + REQUIRED_CODEOWNED_PATHS + test for `tools/test-dom/`; security PR4-SEC-10: anchor the playwright-report/test-results lint ignores) TOGETHER with its merge of main (resolving the PR #17 conflicts in `jest.config.ts` and `tools/lint/negative-fixtures.test.ts` under PR #17's mode split), followed by delta code + security rounds naming the new head. PR4-SEC-11 is closed by R3-M1 in that cycle. The Firefox `EPERM` (COND-E2E-FF) is NOT worked around on this host (no Defender exclusion); Firefox runs on the Linux lane (TASK-015).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ORCH-021 · 2026-11-13 · sdlc-orchestrator · PR #17 residuals and the RANGE_BASE lever
- **Decision:** (1) D-TASK007-04 residual extended: until PR17-SEC-07/08 are fixed, owned `ci.yml` edits can still bypass the pinned step — a folded `run: >` rewrite (yaml-subset folds more-indented lines differently from GitHub) and workflow/job-level `BASH_ENV`/`PATH`/`BASH_FUNC_*` or an earlier step's `$GITHUB_ENV`/`$GITHUB_PATH` write; and (code-review R2.6) the head-controlled job env `RANGE_BASE` can point every "from the base ref" input (incl. the policy checker and the security-record diff range) at the head. Only a main-controlled mechanism closes the latter (repository ruleset requiring the workflow from main, or a `pull_request_target` checker) — a repository-settings change an agent may not make (D-ORCH-012); recommended to the repository owner. Procedural control now: the orchestrator's pre-merge check diffs `.github/workflows/**` against main for every PR and blocks the merge if `RANGE_BASE`, `BASH_ENV`, `PATH`, `BASH_FUNC_*`, `defaults.run`, folded `run: >` on gate steps, or `$GITHUB_ENV`/`$GITHUB_PATH` writes change without a security record that explicitly rules on them. (2) Routing: PR17-SEC-09 (`__mocks__` ignore + CODEOWNERS + repo rule) → devops before the first Wave-1 feature merge; PR17-SEC-07/08/10, PR17-SEC-02, the CR-in-summary note, code R2-m1 (prove `no-explicit-any` on + `reportUnusedDisableDirectives` = 2), R2-n1/n3 → TASK-015; R2-n2 (`.swcrc` mentions in WAVE-00/plan-data/ADR-0023) → architect sheet fold.
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-ARCH-075 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · PR13-SEC-04 / PR13-SEC-08 / PR #13 code review ruling on D-TASK023-01 (01): `platform_app` holds `SELECT` only on `reference_dataset_versions`; the seed (as migrator) is its single writer; the US-073 reconciler needs no write — it derives the last audited digest from `audit_entries` and is generalised to every audited dataset; the migrator gains no audit privilege; `dataset_version` column; `pg.Client` never a pool
- **Decision:** (1) The unmerged migration `20261112030000_reference_dataset_versions` and `prisma/sql/grants.manifest` grant `platform_app` **`SELECT` only** (reviewer option 1), carried by PR #13's superseding branch together with the D-ORCH-015 reconciliation; C5 asserts one manifest line; a `platform_app` `INSERT`/`UPDATE` probe yields `42501`. (2) TASK-221's reconciler **writes nothing** to the table: it digests the authz tables, compares with the latest `CONFIG_CHANGE` entry for `authz_matrix` in `audit_entries` (the tamper-evident store, `SELECT` for `platform_app`) and writes one entry per changed version; the dataset name stays in `RESERVED_DATASET_NAMES`; `down.sql` therefore has no digest row to drop and a down → up cannot back-fill a second baseline (closes PR13-SEC-08's TASK-221 item). (3) The reconciler is generalised to every audited dataset — for each `reference_dataset_versions` row it compares `(dataset, dataset_version, content_sha256)` with the last audited digest — because the seed runs on the **migrator** connection and `INSERT` on `audit_entries` belongs to `platform_app` only: **the migrator gains no privilege on any audit table**; the seed's `ConfigChangeSink` stays a port whose seed-path implementation records state and logs (D-TASK023-01 (04), D-ORCH-016 (2)); drift restores are recorded by the seed in two expand columns (`drift_restored_rows bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, `last_drift_restored_at timestamptz`, TASK-221 migration) and audited by the reconciler as `CONFIG_CHANGE` reason `drift-restored`. (4) The label column is `dataset_version` (STD `version` stays the row counter); the shared contract with the worker-side reconciler is the table, not `DatasetVersionStore`. (5) `SeedSql` callers (TASK-089 provisioner, TASK-221 wiring) pass a `pg.Client`, never a `pg.Pool`. (6) A future runtime writer takes the §4.5 definer-function form only; a direct grant and RLS on this SYSTEM table are rejected; reviewer option 4 (`CHECK (version < 2147483647)`) is not adopted. data-model §3.5, §4.4 (row split), §9.2; §12 A20; sheets TASK-023, TASK-025, TASK-089, TASK-221.
- **Why:** The grant matched the §4.4 matrix but was wider than any writer: a compromised `platform_app` could pre-write a public-repo-computable `(dataset_version, content_sha256)` to suppress a future `CONFIG_CHANGE`, or set `version = 2³¹−1` to make every later seed run overflow (persistent deploy DoS). Deriving the audited baseline from the audit log instead of from a writable row removes the write entirely and makes the baseline unforgeable by construction. Letting the migrator write `audit_entries` would have mixed the DDL credential into the audit-owner separation (§4.4 item 5) that SEC-P3-03 established.
- **Alternatives:** RLS on the SYSTEM table restricted to the `authz_matrix` row (rejected: bends the "SYSTEM = no RLS" class rule for one row and still leaves a writable baseline); a `platform_definer` function `platform.record_authz_digest(…)` (rejected now: no write is needed; kept as the sanctioned form if one ever is); keep `INSERT, UPDATE` and document the residual (rejected: an audit-suppression path on O4).
- **Reversibility:** High (unmerged migration, manifest line, two future expand columns).

### D-ARCH-076 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · PR12-SEC-02 (MEDIUM) partition-name squatting, PR12-SEC-03, PR12-SEC-04 item 3, PR12-SEC-08 (SPK-08 §8 items 6–7; D-SEC-PR12-01): guard matches the object's own name for every relation and type kind; `ensure_audit_partitions` identity test raising `AUDIT_PARTITION_NAME_TAKEN`; readiness = contiguity from the catalogue + `audit_partition_squatted`; plain `CREATE FUNCTION` for the guard with C14 owner/converse/dependency checks; event and audit triggers `ENABLE ALWAYS` with stated limits; prototype RLS not ported
- **Decision:** data-model §5.2 and §5.3 are amended before TASK-021 starts (D-SEC-PR12-01 (1)). (1) `audit_ddl_guard` gains, before its relation branch, a match on the created/altered object's **own** `relname`/`typname` against the audit pattern for `index`, `sequence`, `view`, `materialized view`, `composite type`, `foreign table`, `type` and `domain` in a protected schema (renames surface with the new name and are caught too). (2) `ensure_audit_partitions` replaces `to_regclass(…) IS NULL` with an identity test (`relkind = 'r'`, `inhparent = public.audit_entries`, owner `platform_audit_owner`, not `inhdetachpending`, matching `relpartbound`) that **raises** `AUDIT_PARTITION_NAME_TAKEN` / `AUDIT_PARTITION_BOUNDS_MISMATCH` (42501) and also refuses a `pg_type` of that name; a verified partition gets its registry row whoever created it. (3) The `audit_partitions` readiness check becomes **contiguity from the catalogue** (`pg_inherits ⋈ pg_class`) for months current..current + 2, plus `unavailable` and security event `audit_partition_squatted` for any `public` relation or non-relation type matching `^audit_entries_[0-9]{4}_[0-9]{2}$` that is not such a partition, whatever the month; `audit_partitions` rows (writable by `platform_app`) are no longer the readiness source. (4) PR12-SEC-03: the first-install guard file uses plain `CREATE FUNCTION`; replacement files assert the owner first; C14 asserts the guard function owner and `evtowner` = bootstrap role, the audit-owner converse (exactly three functions; `platform_schema_owner` owns 0 `pg_proc`/`pg_class` rows) and a `pg_depend` check. (5) **`ENABLE ALWAYS`** for both event triggers and every audit trigger incl. the per-partition `TRUNCATE` triggers (C6/C14 expect `'A'`), with the limits written down: it does not close `event_triggers = off`, `ALTER EVENT TRIGGER … DISABLE` or the RDS master's `GRANT … WITH SET TRUE; SET ROLE` path, which event triggers can neither prevent nor detect — those stay with the TASK-116 `evtenabled`/`tgenabled` tripwire and, at M5, `pgaudit.log = 'ddl, role, misc_set'` with the PR12-SEC-04 filters (COND-G4-PROD). (6) PR12-SEC-08: the prototype's RLS and placeholder policies on `audit_entries` and its approval-free headers are not ported; TASK-021's files carry the real rule-12 marker. (7) SEC-P3-37 item 6 is superseded. TASK-021 implements; TASK-033 tests (guard probe per object kind; guard-disabled variant: function raises, readiness `unavailable`; arithmetic unit test); risks.md v1.7 R-ARCH-27; ADR-0007 am. 9 item 4.
- **Why:** The spike reproduced the squat (migrator `CREATE INDEX audit_entries_2099_02` succeeds and cannot be dropped by the migrator). With `ensure(3)` and one missing month the strictly-future count is 2 or 3, so the "< 2" rule could never fire; the first symptom would be `23514` on every audited write — the whole booking/payment path — at 00:00 UTC of the squatted month, plantable years ahead. Three independent layers (prevent, fail loudly, detect on the day of planting) are needed because the linter is text-only and dynamic SQL evades it. `ENABLE ALWAYS` is free locally and closes the replica-mode bypass for every non-superuser, including a future `rds_superuser` member; its limits are stated so that nobody reads it as closing the master path.
- **Alternatives:** keep the name test and add only the readiness count fix (rejected: a far-future squat stays invisible until the month and a count can still be satisfied); reserve the names with a `CHECK`/naming rule in the linter only (rejected: not a boundary); grant the migrator `DROP` on squatting indexes (rejected: the guard's `c_idx` protection is deliberate; recovery is break-glass by design).
- **Reversibility:** High before TASK-021 lands (text); medium afterwards (bootstrap files are immutable; a change is a new file).

### D-ARCH-077 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · SPK-08 (TASK-012, PR #12) consumer findings ratified: §7.3 F-01 generated `tables.external` window, F-02/F-03 shadow rendering with owner refusal, F-04 `INCLUDE` and F-11 `WHERE` linter rules (owner TASK-024), "verify in P5 spike" notes closed; anchor format A-2/A-3/A-4 frozen, verifier A-1/A-5/A-6/A-7 and the AF-9/AF-12 nightly-walk qualifier (PR12-SEC-05); ADR-0007 am. 9
- **Decision:** (1) **F-01:** the root `prisma.config.ts` lists `public.audit_entries_YYYY_MM` for a generated bounded window (2026-01 … current month + 24, pure function) besides the eight audit parents, because Prisma's first-deploy P3005 emptiness check counts partitions; the lane-reordering alternative is rejected (PR12-SEC-07, D-SEC-PR12-01 (2)). (2) **F-02/F-03:** the shadow rendering emits `DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS … CASCADE` before `CREATE SCHEMA` for `platform`, strips grants and default privileges too, keeps `CREATE EXTENSION`, and its first statement **refuses unless `current_database()` is owned by `current_user`** (a mis-set `SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` on a migrator-owned developer database would otherwise wipe `platform`). (3) **F-04 / F-11 linter rules, owner TASK-024, due before TASK-026 merges:** no `DROP INDEX` of a covering-index tolerance-list name unless the same file re-creates it **with `INCLUDE`**; no `DROP INDEX` of an index an earlier migration created with `WHERE` unless the same file re-creates it **with a `WHERE` clause** (PR #12 R2-m1 wording — a plain re-create is exactly the defect), predicate changes flagged; rule: partial indexes stay raw SQL and are never declared as plain `@@index` until `partialIndexes` (still preview in 7.10.1) is enabled, then every partial index is declared with its exact `where: raw(…)`. (4) **Anchor format (hash/sink inputs, frozen before TASK-116 writes the first anchor):** A-2 `heads_digest` items `stream_id ‖ be64(entry_count) ‖ head_hash` sorted by the 16 raw UUID bytes, empty set = SHA-256(""), ADR-0007 am. 6's `(stream_id, head_hash, stream_seq)` wording superseded by a pointer to §5.5; A-3 sink line = RFC 8785 JCS of the named fields + `\n`, compared **as a string**; A-4 `created_at` = `clock_timestamp()` in the anchor transaction, rendered by PostgreSQL (`YYYY-MM-DD"T"HH24:MI:SS.US"Z"`, UTC). (5) **Verifier:** A-1 steps run to completion, every failing step emits its event, `failure_kind` by precedence SIGNATURE > HEAD_MISMATCH > ANCHOR_MISSING > chain kinds, `failure_kinds text[]` + details, `ANCHOR_SINK_MISMATCH` detail code, equal paging; A-5 strict DER parsing (WebCrypto converts DER → P1363); A-6 absent sink record tolerated only within [now − 15 min, now + 2 min], future-dated anchors fail; A-7 step (iii) on the latest anchor that passed (i)–(ii); **AF-9/AF-12:** the latest-anchor check is sound only with the nightly full walk from seq 1 that ignores `last_verified_*`; behind-head rewrites with an intact head row are caught within 24 h (SEC-TC-44 variant (d), `algorithm` and malleation rows, TASK-116); readiness is secondary to the security event; PR12-SEC-05 (e) sink versioning → COND-G4-PROD. data-model §5.5, §5.6, §7.3; ADR-0007 am. 9; sheets TASK-021, TASK-024, TASK-033, TASK-034, TASK-116.
- **Why:** A-2/A-3/A-4 are hash and sink inputs — changing them after the first anchor invalidates every stored anchor, so they are fixed now, from the prototype that already implements them. The spike proved the shadow hook works but also that a careless `DROP SCHEMA` is a foot-gun on exactly the database developers use; the owner refusal costs one statement. F-04/F-11 go to TASK-024 (linter owner after TASK-016 merges) rather than "TASK-016 follow-up" because TASK-016 is in its supersede round and TASK-026 is the next migration-adding task.
- **Alternatives:** dedicated `audit` schema now (rejected: M5 option; the window is a config-only change); keep "F-11 unless the same file re-creates it" (rejected: passes the plain re-create that drops the predicate); Ed25519/P1363 storage (settled, D-ARCH-029).
- **Reversibility:** High for linter rules and text; low for the anchor format once anchors exist.

### D-ARCH-078 · 2026-11-13 · principal-architect · PR11-SEC-16 (LOW) and PR #11 code review R2-n2: `E'…'` escape strings in `EXECUTE` operands are refused token-level (as `U&`), owner TASK-024 with PR11-SEC-14 b; data-model §8.3 "Limits" states the by-form limits (a)–(e) with their outcome controls; PR11-SEC-17 c / R3-n3 split between TASK-024 (setter keys, quoted names) and TASK-021 (DO bodies)
- **Decision:** PostgreSQL decodes `\xHH`, octal and `\u` escapes inside `E'…'` strings in the lexer, so `EXECUTE E'SET LOCAL \x61pp.org_id = ' || …` evaded rule 16 and the C-series while the setter name stayed visible. Ruling: refuse escape strings outright at token level (the `sqlText` tokenizer already marks the `E` prefix; one write form in the shared list matches the sentinel in `scans.ts` and the linter; never a bare regex, which also matches a string ending in a lone `e`); no committed migration, bootstrap file or template uses one; fixtures `context-escape-string` (linter) and an executed C-series fixture; the `tenant-key-forms.ts` header sentence is corrected. §8.3 "Limits" now lists: (a) escape strings (refused), (b) setter name + key both built in `format()` and reads via `pg_show_all_settings()` (review items / dynamic-SQL allow-list), (c) a role left set through `set_config('role', …)` or an `EXECUTE 'SET ROLE …'` in a DO body (outcome control: C11 converse + leak detector; literals flagged, PR11-SEC-15 b), (d) rule 11 blind to `SET "lock_timeout"`, `set_config('lock_timeout', …)` and SETs inside DO bodies (quoted names and `set_config` fold into `LOCK_TIMEOUT_VALUE` — TASK-024; DO bodies — TASK-021 with PR11-SEC-15 a), (e) any `EXECUTE` of a non-literal in DO/function bodies is review-required. The execution-level controls (prosrc scans by form, C3/C11/C13/C14, in-statement leak detector) are the boundary.
- **Why:** Text rules cannot be complete over dynamic SQL; writing the limits down keeps the linter from being read as a boundary (the PR #11 header overstated the control) and gives each limit an owner. PR #11's superseding branch carries its net change unchanged (D-ORCH-018), so the work lands in TASK-024.
- **Alternatives:** decode escape strings and scan the decoded text (rejected: more code for a form nothing legitimate uses; refusal is cheaper and matches the `U&` precedent).
- **Reversibility:** High.

### D-062 · 2026-11-13 · product-manager · US-008 delivery split (PR #20 code review M2, D-ORCH-022 (2)): TASK-010 delivers AT-US-008-01/02/04/05/06 and the api/worker log-line hops of AT-03; the audit hop is owned by TASK-029, the outbox hop by TASK-043, the job-payload hop by TASK-035, the Mock-carrier hop by TASK-079, and the end-to-end AT-US-008-03 assertion by TASK-092, which completes US-008; US-008 stays M0 Must, non-DC, nothing is split or deferred
- **Decision:** (1) US-008 is **not** completed by TASK-010. The plan sheets change from "TASK-010 completes US-008" to "TASK-010 partial; completed by TASK-092" (CLAUDE.md §7 step 3 form applied to task ownership only — the story's MoSCoW (Must), milestone (M0) and DC tag (none) are unchanged, as D-ORCH-022 (2) expected). (2) Scenario ownership. AT-US-008-01, -02, -04, -05, -06 → **TASK-010** (met in PR #20: validated/echoed/regenerated `X-Correlation-Id`, `request_id` + `client_correlation_id`, deep-walk redaction, e-mail masking, mandatory fields, body-free access lines). AT-US-008-03 ("One ID is visible end to end for the M0 booking") is decomposed by hop, following REQ-PLAT-011's own list (logs, audit entries, outbox events, queue jobs, outbound carrier calls): **api log lines and worker job log lines → TASK-010** (delivered: request context binds `correlation_id` to every api line including the error line; the worker job-context binds the payload's `correlation_id` to every job line); **audit entries → TASK-029** (the descriptor-driven middleware writes the request's correlation ID into every entry it creates — the entry *field* is TASK-025/US-087's, the *value carried from the request* is TASK-029's); **outbox event → TASK-043** (`outbox_events.correlation_id` is set from the request context of the business transaction by the writer, and the relay copies that value into the `correlation_id` of the payload v2 job it enqueues, so a correlation ID never changes between the row and the job); **job payload → TASK-035** (payload v2 `correlation_id` is populated by the producer from the originating request/outbox context — not generated — and the worker binds it as the logger's `correlation_id` for the whole job, re-proving TASK-010's binding on the real payload v2); **Mock carrier call → TASK-079** (every Mock adapter operation emits exactly one outbound carrier-call log line through TASK-010's `outboundCallFields` allow-list with `correlation_id` = `CarrierContext.correlationId` and no request/response body, and the call record it returns carries that correlation ID so the saga's `carrier_call` audit entry can cite it — this also lands the PR #20 code-review §5 note "carrier adapters must use `outboundCallFields`" on the first adapter); **end-to-end assertion → TASK-092** (one integration test titled with AT-US-008-03: POST the M0 booking with `X-Correlation-Id: demo-booking-0001`, run the saga through the worker on the Mock carrier, and assert that exactly that value appears in the api log lines, every audit entry of the booking (STATE_TRANSITION entries and the `carrier_call` entry), the outbox row, the BullMQ payload v2, the worker log lines and the Mock carrier call record). TASK-092 completes US-008; US-008 reads as Implemented only when TASK-010, TASK-029, TASK-035, TASK-043, TASK-079 and TASK-092 are all merged. **TASK-047 is not an owner** (it resolves tenant context from `platform.job_context`, not correlation; its `job-context.ts` edits must keep TASK-010's correlation binding, which TASK-035's line and TASK-092's end-to-end test catch). (3) Dependency order is already satisfied: TASK-092 depends on TASK-084 (→ TASK-029, TASK-043), TASK-043 (→ TASK-039 → TASK-035 → TASK-010) and TASK-079, so no `plan-data.mjs` dependency change is needed (contrast D-061, which added TASK-089 → TASK-014). Wave 2 is inside the generator's [0, 3] window for an M0 non-DC Must. (4) Traceability v1.6: REQ-PLAT-011 Task(s) = `TASK-010, TASK-029, TASK-035, TASK-043, TASK-079, TASK-092`, status "Planned (partial: TASK-010 validate/generate/echo + api/worker log lines; completes with TASK-029 (audit), TASK-043 (outbox → job), TASK-035 (payload v2 → worker logger), TASK-079 (Mock carrier call) and TASK-092 (end-to-end AT-US-008-03))"; REQ-PLAT-012 (Pino fields and redaction) stays wholly TASK-010. No REQ or story IDs, links, priorities, milestones or DC tags change; counts 434 / 91 / 293 / DC 134 unchanged. (5) Demo coverage confirmed: PRD §6.1 "One mock booking flows API → queue → worker (… correlation ID)" is demonstrated by TASK-092 (batch 2.5), which precedes TASK-121 M0 exit-demo journeys (batch 3.7, depends on TASK-094 → TASK-092). (6) PR #20 body and sheet: "partially completes US-008"; the AT-US-008-03 test in PR #20 keeps its title with a "(api and worker log lines; end to end in TASK-092)" suffix rather than being deleted. (7) STATUS: the fold is already queued under SHEET-FOLD-2 by D-ORCH-022; the exact sheet lines are in the D-062 handoff.
- **Why:** The logger is real (reviewer-verified: linear regexes, reserved keys, never-throw, identifier-only DB serializer), but four of the six hops in AT-03 describe data written by components that do not exist yet — the audit middleware, the outbox, payload v2 and the Mock adapter. Marking US-008 complete at TASK-010 would have let traceability show REQ-PLAT-011 Implemented while the propagation half of its text ("propagate it to … audit entries, outbox events, queue jobs and outbound carrier calls") had no owner — the silent drop PR #20 M2 flagged, identical to PR #13 M1. Ladder rung 4 (split into a smaller Must plus a Should) is wrong: nothing leaves R0 and every hop is still Must; only task ownership changes. Per-hop lines on the component owners rather than everything on TASK-092 because each component is the only place the value can be *set* correctly, and a saga-level test alone would surface a broken hop two waves after the component merged; the end-to-end assertion sits on TASK-092 because it is the first task where all six hops exist in one process chain (API → outbox → queue → worker → Mock carrier is its title). TASK-079 rather than TASK-092 for the Mock carrier hop because the adapter is the only code that can emit the outbound line with the context's ID, and it is the first `CarrierAdapter` implementation, so the `outboundCallFields` rule from PR #20 §5 must be proven there before the real DHL/FedEx/UPS adapters copy it. TASK-035 rather than TASK-043 for the payload-to-logger binding because payload v2 and the worker queue platform are TASK-035's artefacts; TASK-043 owns only the copy from the row into the payload it enqueues.
- **Alternatives:** Keep "TASK-010 completes US-008" and rely on the PR body (rejected: traceability and G3 read the sheets, not PR bodies). Put the whole of AT-03 on TASK-092 only (rejected: no hop would be proven at the component that sets it; a defect would surface in wave 2 instead of the owning wave). Create a new "correlation end-to-end" enabler task (rejected: dispatch-state churn, and TASK-092 already runs exactly that chain for US-202; D-ARCH-079 set the precedent of no new `late` task). Ladder rung 4 — split US-008 into a Must plus a Should (rejected: no scope is leaving R0; a Should would wrongly signal that end-to-end tracing may slip). Assign the Mock-carrier hop to TASK-047 as the PR suggested (rejected: TASK-047 is tenant context; it never touches the carrier adapter).
- **Reversibility:** High (sheet lines, a story note, one traceability row; `plan-data.mjs` folded by the architect batch under SHEET-FOLD-2).

### D-063 · 2026-11-13 · product-manager · AT-US-007-02 wording aligned with OPTIONS/405 `Allow` parity (PR #21 / TASK-017 code review R1-m3, D-ORCH-023 (2)): a 405 `Allow` lists every method the route supports including OPTIONS, and OPTIONS on a known route is 204 with the same `Allow`; US-007 stays M0 Must, non-DC; no scope change
- **Decision:** (1) AT-US-007-02 ("405 includes the Allow header", `docs/01-requirements/stories/EPIC-01-platform-foundation.md`) now reads: When DELETE is sent to a route that supports only GET and HEAD, Then `Allow` is "GET, HEAD, OPTIONS", And OPTIONS sent to the same route returns 204 with no body and the same `Allow` header. The former text ("GET, HEAD") contradicted the reviewed and implemented R1-m3 fix (`apps/api/src/platform/http/fallback.ts`, `tests/api/helpers/contract-validator.ts`, `tests/contract-stubs/stubs.ts`). The scenario title is unchanged so `docs/05-quality/tests/gherkin-test-map.md` row AT-US-007-02 (`api`, B) remains valid without edit; no other US-007 scenario changes. (2) REQ-PLAT-010 (REQUIREMENTS.md → 1.8) note: "The 405 response carries an `Allow` header listing every method the route supports, including OPTIONS (RFC 9110 §15.5.6); OPTIONS on a known route is 204 with the same `Allow`." The "shall" sentence, ID, M0, Must are unchanged. (3) US-007 notes record the amendment and point to this decision. (4) PRD → 1.8 (§11 row); NFR.md (1.5) and `traceability.md` (1.6) untouched: no ID, link, priority, milestone, DC tag or count changes (434 / 91 / 293 / DC 134), and REQ-PLAT-010's traceability row (TASK-006, Implemented) is a task-ownership fact this wording does not alter — the parity behaviour was delivered by TASK-017 on top of TASK-006's handler and is verified by PR #21's own tests; qa-lead's Test(s) column picks it up at the usual time. (5) Not done here: `docs/03-architecture/api/README.md` §3 "OPTIONS 204 + `Allow` including OPTIONS / 405 `Allow` lists OPTIONS" line — api-designer owns it per D-ORCH-023 (1); no code is touched.
- **Why:** RFC 9110 §15.5.6 requires a 405 to carry an `Allow` field listing the target resource's currently supported methods, and §9.3.7 makes OPTIONS a method the resource supports whenever it answers OPTIONS — which every known route now does (204). An acceptance test that pins "GET, HEAD" would therefore fail a correct server and pass one that violates the RFC, and would force the reviewers to choose between the story text and the standard at every future 405 test. The AC is the only place the exact list is written down; REQ-PLAT-010 only said "carries an `Allow` header", so the note is sharpened to carry the rule (list every supported method, OPTIONS included) rather than a literal header value, keeping the requirement atomic and testable on any route. The parity "And" line is added inside AT-US-007-02 rather than as a new scenario because R1-m3's finding was precisely that three components disagreed on one behaviour; a 405-only assertion would not catch the 204/405 drifting apart again, and adding a scenario would be a backlog change the task did not ask for. Nothing moves in or out of R0; US-007 is Must, non-DC, so no demo path is affected. Classified as a wording amendment (D-059/D-060 convention: PRD §11 row, REQUIREMENTS.md version bump, story note; traceability untouched), not a delivery split (D-061/D-062) and not a re-scope.
- **Alternatives:** Leave the AC at "GET, HEAD" and revert R1-m3 (rejected by D-ORCH-023 and here: contradicts RFC 9110 and the reviewed code). Change only the literal in the Then line without touching REQ-PLAT-010 (rejected: the REQ would stay silent on what `Allow` must contain, so the next reader could again infer "GET, HEAD" from Express defaults; one note clause removes the ambiguity at no cost). Make the OPTIONS 204 behaviour a new REQ/story (rejected: it is framework behaviour inside REQ-PLAT-010's "unsupported method" mapping and api/README §3's domain; a new ID for a header-parity rule is churn with no product value). Write the Then line as a regex/"contains OPTIONS" (rejected: a fixed, ordered list on a known fixture route is the more deterministic test; the REQ note carries the general rule).
- **Reversibility:** High (one AC line and one "And" line, one REQ note clause, two version rows; no code, IDs, priorities or counts).

### D-ORCH-024 · 2026-11-13 · sdlc-orchestrator · PR #21 round 2: R2-m1 fixed in-PR (light cycle); PR #20 union rule; D-TASK017-01 amended
- **Decision:** (1) PR #21 code review r2 at b31483b = APPROVED_WITH_NITS. R2-m1 (array/optional-group mounts and a root sub-app escape the drift walker) is fixed **in this PR** in a light fix cycle 2 (R2-n2, R2-n3 too), touching only non-owned paths (`tools/openapi/`, `apps/api/src/app.ts` comment) so the security round-2 record on b31483b stays valid under rule (d); a code-review delta check covers `drift.ts` + test only. The light cycle runs no Docker/integration/e2e/coverage, so it does not count against the 3-build-agent cap (D-ORCH-010). (2) R2-n1 (anchor the `import-x/no-relative-packages` ignore regex against `..` climb-outs, `eslint.config.mjs`, owned) → TASK-020 sheet (SHEET-FOLD-2), not this PR, to avoid voiding the record for a NIT. (3) **PR #20/#21 union rule:** whichever PR merges second must, besides the textual union of `app.ts`, `main.ts` and `request-context.ts` (keep both `requestId` and `apiMode`; keep the docs mount inside `routes` after `optionsResponder`), move PR #20's `routePrefix` from `routes.use(API_PREFIX, routePrefix(API_PREFIX), api)` to `api.use(routePrefix(API_PREFIX))` as the first layer of `api`, then `routes.use(API_PREFIX, api)` — the walker must not be widened to skip path-mounted middleware. Drift, `test:unit` and `test:api` re-run on the union commit; the union touches owned paths only if the merge brings them, in which case a merge-only security round applies. Expected order: PR #21 first, PR #20 second. (4) **D-TASK017-01 amendment (R2-n4):** item (2) "append-only ledger" now reads "add-only is a process rule; removal is a §18 breaking change caught by oasdiff (TASK-015, before v0.1.0-m0)"; item (4) coverage exclusion is exactly `!packages/shared/src/api/types.gen.ts` (not `!**/*.gen.ts`); the drift check walks the composed `createApp(...)` from `app.router`, resolving only literal mounts `/api/v1` and `/api/docs` with `/api/docs` the sole exclusion, and reports any path-mounted non-router layer as `unrecognised-route`. (5) Note for TASK-020/048: limiter or CORS mounted under a path will fail drift by design — mount path-less inside the router or add a tested exclusion.
- **Alternatives:** Route R2-m1 to TASK-020 (rejected: verified fix is small and the gap lets unlisted routes serve 200 while drift stays green); apply R2-n1 now (rejected: voids the security record for a NIT).
- **Reversibility:** High.
